# Inventory

What the character carries, where each thing sits, and the screen that shows it. Read from the game's code on 2026-10-11 (`InventoryLogic`, `UI`) and from the install's item database. Moving things, the weapon in hand, where the screen's views sit and what the open screen does to the keys and the hands were read and built on 2026-10-11 too. Nothing here has been compared with the running game yet: no recording carries the inventory (recorder 0.5.0 writes it; "Compared with the running game" below). What is built is at the end.

The player controller needs it for three things: what the character weighs (movement and stamina go by carried weight), what it wears (drawn on the body), and what is in its hands.

## The screen and its tabs

**It opens on its gear tab every time** (`InventoryScreen.Show`, `TabGroup`, the game player owner's `ShowInventoryScreen`; read after a container was found opening on the tab last looked at). In a raid the game makes the screen's controller anew at each opening, and the screen its group of tabs anew with the gear tab as the one it begins at; the inventory's key asks for no tab in particular (`Unchanged`), which is then that one, and a container asks for the gear tab outright. The tab last looked at is kept only across a screen opened from this one and come back from. Built so (`inventory_view::toggle`): by the key and by a container alike, the options tab included, which is the app's own.

**It comes in softly, and in parts** (`UIScreen.ShowGameObject` → `SoftShow` → `VisualExtensions.SoftChange` at speed 4 → `Co_ChangeCanvasGroupAlpha`; `InventoryScreen.Show`, `ShowWithTab(splitInFrames: true)`, `ItemsPanel.Show`, `UpdateEquipment`; `UIScreen.Close`; read and built 2026-11-02). Shown, the whole screen's group is set to 0 and gains four times the frame's time on the key's own frame and on each after it; once it is within 0.05 of whole, the next frame makes it whole (about a quarter of a second, linear in time, every opening alike). Shut, the fade is dropped and the screen is gone at once, mid-fade too; the next opening starts again from 0 (a fade still running when the screen is shown again without a shut would be made whole at once: `SoftChange`, not seen). The parts come over three frames: on the key's frame and the next only the bar of tabs, with no tab chosen (GEAR in its idle look), the two glows at the top corners and BACK; on the second frame after the key the tab group is shown with GEAR chosen, and with it the equipment, the health figures and the containers (the containers come after a `Task.Yield` that the engine runs on that same frame); the quick-use bar after another, on the third. The loot beside the equipment, where there is one, comes after a third yield, on the third frame with the quick-use bar (`ItemsPanel.Show`: `SimpleStashPanel.Show` or `ComplexStashPanel.Show` after the equipment's yield; `courses10`'s searches: the screen on 320, 355, 361, the loot panel on 323, 358, 364; the app since s187: the screen on 128, the loot panel on 131). The world's blur is separate (`screen_blur`, below). The battle screen is shown the same way as it comes back (below, "The battle screen's panels"). **Compared with** `courses10`'s `inventory-open-close` (eight presses of the key, a double tap among them): the group's alpha on every frame of the four openings from each frame's own time (93: 0.0333, 94: 0.2417 after a 0.052 s frame, ... 104: 0.9531, 105: 1; the reopening at 1279 from 0 again, 0.0426), the shut at 1272 at 0.498, and the parts on frames K, K+2 and K+3 at all four. Built (`screen_fade`, `inventory_view::Opening`, `follow_opening`, `rebuild`, `game_tabs`): the app's run of the take (`--tap` at the take's keys) has every step at 4 × its own frame's time (301: 0.0245 after a 6.1 ms frame, whole at 328 after 0.9603), the shut at 1480 mid-fade, the reopening from 0, the bar alone on K and K+1 (65 elements), the page on K+2 (679), the quick bar on K+3 (840), and GEAR idle on K and K+1 (51.06 wide, the game's 51.07) and chosen on K+2 (53.48, the game's 53.48). The app draws neither glow (`Left Glow`, `Right Glow`: not built). The page is built once on K+2 and the quick bar alone added on K+3; the fade changes colours only, nothing is laid out or built again for it.

The inventory screen (`InventoryScreen`) is one screen with a row of tabs (`EInventoryTab`), in this order:

| Tab | What it shows | Here |
|---|---|---|
| Overall | the character's statistics | shown, switched off |
| Gear | equipment, containers, and the stash or the loot | built (plain) |
| Health | the same panel with the body's health in place of the equipment | shown, the app's arrangement (`health.md`) |
| Skills | skills and weapon mastering | built: the game's SKILLS page with `TARKOV UI`, every skill at its level as set, those that change nothing here dimmed; MASTERING built: each begun family's weapons as the game's page (`skills.md`, "Weapon mastering") |
| Map | the map item | shown, switched off |
| Tasks (`Notes` in the code) | quests and notes | shown, switched off |
| Achievements | | shown, switched off |
| Prestige | not usable in a raid; hidden outside a regular session | shown, switched off |
| Options | not the game's: its settings are a screen of its menu (`settings.md`) | built, apart at the row's far right, by TRP's own choice |

It opens on Gear unless told otherwise (the tab group's default). Gear and Health are the same panel (`ItemsPanel`) in two modes. TRP's choice: tabs as the game has them; Overall, Map, Tasks and Prestige switched off for now (Achievements is treated the same).

**The Gear tab** (`ItemsPanel`, `EquipmentTab`, `ContainersPanel`) has three parts side by side:

- **Equipment**, a view for each of: earpiece, headwear, face cover, armband, body armour, eyewear, the weapon on the sling, the holster, the weapon on the back, the scabbard (and the dogtag's view).
- **Containers**, in this order: tactical vest, pockets, backpack, secured container; the dogtag's slot hangs on the pockets' view. Each shows the grids of the item in that slot, and the pockets show their special slots.
- **The stash, or what is being looted**; in a raid with nothing open it is empty.

Under them run the health parameters (among them the carried weight, which is the same sum the controller uses) and the quick-use bar.

### Where the views sit (read from the install, 2026-10-11)

The game's screens are not in its bundles. They are objects of its scenes, and the build's scene files (`EscapeFromTarkov_Data\level<N>`) carry no type trees; but an object's name and parent and a screen transform's anchors, offset, size and pivot have the engine's own fixed layout, so they can be read (`trp_data::screen`; `probe scenes ui` lists the scenes, `probe screen level44 "Gear Panel" 1` shows one object with what hangs from it). The build's settings list the scenes by their paths; the one whose path ends in `UI/CommonUIScene.unity` holds the inventory screen:

- `InventoryScreen` is 1920 units wide and as high as the screen, so the screen is laid out for a height of 1080. Its tab bar is 43 high across the top.
- `Items Panel` (the Gear and Health tabs' panel) has three children that matter: `LeftSide` (a row: the left panel with the equipment, then the containers panel), `Stash Panel` (680 wide, against the right edge) and `Quick Use Panel` (854 wide, at the bottom's middle).
- **The equipment panel** (`Gear Panel`, with the `EquipmentTab` script) is 494 by 650. Its slot views are its own children, each fixed by an offset from the panel's top left corner. The script names eleven of them in this order: earpiece, headwear, face cover, armband, body armour, eyewear, primary weapon, holster, secondary weapon, scabbard, dogtag; the reader finds that run of pointers in the script's fields and so knows which view is which slot's without going by the objects' names. As read: three views to a row, 125 square and 184 apart (the armband's is 44 high and the dogtag's sits under it); the two long weapons' views are 310 wide with the holster's and the scabbard's beside them; the rows begin 20, 186, 354 and 524 down. The primary weapon's view (the sling's) is the third row's.
- A slot's caption is a header 20 high that sits over the view, not in it.
- **The containers panel** is laid out by layout scripts while the game runs: a column, and in it for each container a row of its slot view (125 square) and then its grids side by side. Only that order is taken from it.
- A grid's cell is 63 units (`GridView`, in the code).
- **There is no figure in the panel.** In the game's menu the character stands in the scene behind the screen (the "environment" scenes), and in a raid the world shows through. The slots were first laid over a paper figure; the app drew one of plain shapes, which is its own, and still does with the options' `CHARACTER MODEL` off.
- **The character model** (a still model of the current player model in place of the paper figure, standing straight in the pre-raid screen's stance, behind a switch of its own; the model itself rather than a picture, so that nothing is stored, taken from the model already loaded so that it is not loaded twice). Behind the slots, over the whole panel, the character's own model in what it wears. **How the game's screens show it** (`PlayerModelView.Show`, `PlayerModelLoader`, read 2026-10-14): the menu's own copy of the player (`menuplayer.bundle`, whose animator is the body's `Player_anim_controller`) stood 5 m before the screen's view and 1 m below it, turned half round to face it; its animator's layer 11 (`GUI`, an override of every bone) given a weight of 1, the kind of thing in hand set in `WeaponType`, `WeaponTypeFloat` and `UI_WeaponType` (-1 for nothing, 0 a rifle, 1 a pistol, 2 a thing thrown, 3 a knife), and one update of the time the screen asks for, which every screen that shows the character gives as nothing. The layer's one state, `GUI.GUI_IDLE`, blends by `UI_WeaponType`: at -1 it is `inv_idle_hands_down`, the stance of the pre-raid screen, the arms down at the sides. With a weapon the game hangs the weapon's model from the ribcage and plays its `PATROL`; its animator then runs on, and a drag across the model turns it (`InventoryPlayerModelWithStatsWindow`, `MatchmakerTimeHasCome`: their `_rotator`).
  **Here** (`figure_view`): the pose is the body's controller on the model's bones, its `GUI` layer at 1 and nothing in the hands, updated once by nothing (`BodyData::load_standing`: a controller of its own for those bones and for the states such an update plays, each layer's first and the `GUI` layer's, read with the body's from the one table). The model is **the body's own meshes, pictures, materials and bind poses, by their handles**, on a skeleton of its own: nothing is read, made or sent to the graphics card a second time (the only new thing is the picture it is drawn onto, the panel's size in the window's pixels, 1.3 MB at 1080 lines). Each of its pieces is shown while the body's own is, so what is worn, an outfit of the other game's among it (its rig stood once over the model's bones as `body_view::see_outfit` stands it every frame), comes and goes on it as on the body. Its camera stands where the game's screens put theirs, 5 m before it and level with a point a metre up, and takes in its whole height; a light of its own, the range's sun without shadows. It is drawn **only when something changes** (what it wears, the window's size), on two frames once its meshes and pictures are on the card and the renderer has nothing left to build, and is otherwise still. **Departures on purpose**: it is still (the game's runs on), it has nothing in its hands (the game's holds the weapon in hand, in its own stance), and it does not turn by a drag.

- **The left panel's width** (read 2026-10-15, at night; `health.md`, "The inventory's HEALTH tab"): `LeftSide`'s layout gives the left panel and the containers' panel half each of its width less 5 (it is 0.317 of the screen's width and 604.69 more): 604.16 on a 16:9 screen. The equipment panel lies in it centred, 100 down. The HEALTH tab shows the same page with the health panel in the left panel.

The app reads the equipment panel at its start and puts each slot's view where the install has it, scaled by the window's height over 1080; cells are 63 of those units and the right-hand part is the stash panel's 680 wide. **The left part is the game's left panel's width** (before, the equipment panel's and 60), the same on the GEAR and HEALTH tabs so that the containers stay put between them. With the options' `TARKOV UI` on there is no model; the game's own flat outline of the character is behind the slots (`CharacterGear`, 0.20.0: "The game's own look" below), and under the slots the game's own strip of figures and the left panel's striped foot (`health.md`, "The GEAR tab's strip of figures"). The middle part's width, the captions' words and the figure are the app's; the app's page begins lower than the game's (its readout and tab row above), and the equipment panel sits 6 pixels down in it, not 100 units. With `TARKOV UI` on the page is laid out as the game's screen instead: "The game's own look", "The screen's places" below.

### The game's own look (`TARKOV UI`; read 2026-10-15, at night, built 0.20.0)

With the options' `TARKOV UI` on (`settings.md`, "Features") the GEAR page is drawn from the game's own screens, read from the install as the health panel is (`health.md`, "The inventory's HEALTH tab"); the app's boxes for the slots and grids stay over them, taking the mouse and holding what is in them, and draw nothing of their own. Compared with nothing.

- **The equipment panel** (`Gear Panel`, `EquipmentTab`): the game's flat outline of the character behind the slots (`CharacterGear`, `character_pmc`: the panel's script never hides it), and each slot's view (`SlotView`), named by the panel's script in its fields' order and each view's pieces by its own (`full border, search icon, empty picture, a camera's, the place of what is in it, the empty fill, the empty border, the chosen border and corner, the header's words, the header`): a shadow, a dark underlay, the header (`SlotViewHeader`: its idle background in three pieces and the slot's name in the install's words in capitals, `slot.ID.Localized().ToUpper()`; its arrow only out of a raid), and as the slot is full or empty (`SlotView.SetSlotGraphics`) the full border, or the empty border, the striped fill and the slot's own picture. The dogtag's view is left out (the app has no dogtag).
- **A thing's view** (`SlotItemView`, `GridItemView`): the prefabs the game makes them from (`ItemViewFactory.CreateFromPool`: the build's Resources `Prefabs/UGUI/Layouts/slot_layout` and `grid_layout`, in `resources.assets`, found by the engine's table of Resources paths, `ResourceManager` in `globalgamemanagers`). Its ground is its kind's colour in the database (`BackgroundColor`: `blue`, `violet` and the rest, `TaxonomyColorExtension.ColorMapping`) at 0.302 (`ItemView.UpdateStaticInfo`), white at 77 of 255 under the mouse (`DefaultSelectedColor`); a grid's view has the prefab's border colour, a slot's none (its border is the slot's own full border). Its caption (`Info Panel/Name` in a slot, `Info Panel/Caption` in a grid; `GridItemView.GetItemName`) is the short name between two spaces in #b6c1c7, in Bender at 11, where the prefab lays it. A stack's count (`SetCountValue`, when its count is not one) is at the bottom right, 3 units in, in its colour (`GetStackColor`: rounds #dadabc, money #8db500, the rest #b6c1c7). A thing bound to a key has the label (`BindPanel`: `bind_label`, at its own 25 by 25 at the top left) with the key's name. The weapon's pattern and shadow are only for another player's profile (`SlotItemView.UpdateStaticInfo`), not drawn. The two rows are laid out by groups that fit their contents (`VerticalLayoutGroup` and `ContentSizeFitter`, not solved here): the label and the count are placed as those groups put them here, by hand.
- **The containers' column** (`ContainersPanel`, beside the left panel and as large, 5 apart as the left side's row has them): its ground (`Scrollview Parent`, black at 0.498); a row for each container slot (`Slot Template`, laid in a column 120 down and 45 apart), its header as the equipment's (the slot's name in capitals: TACTICAL RIG, POCKETS, BACKPACK), its 125 by 125 slot panel with its fill and borders and the slot's picture (`SearchableSlotView.Show`: the Resource `Slots/Equipment/<slot>`, none for the pockets, at its own size about the panel's middle, `Image.SetNativeSize`), and its container's grids 7 to the right. **A container's grids** are laid out by its own layout where it has one (`ContainedGridsView.CreateGrids`: a vest's `RigLayoutName`, a backpack's `GridLayoutName`, the Resource `UI/Rig Layouts/<name>`, a `TemplatedGridsView` whose grid views take the container's grids in its list's order, `_presetGridViews`), else in a row 6 apart (`GeneratedGridsView`, the row template's `_containedGridsTemplate` in `sharedassets44.assets`). **A grid's view** (`GridView`, the generated view's `_gridViewTemplate`): `grid_cell` tiled a cell to 63 units (dark, its edges lighter, at 0.63), as large as its cells and one more, in a `border_shadow` frame 5 outside whose middle is not drawn (`fillCenter` off; the frame a pixel to a pixel of the screen, `PixelPerfectSpriteScaler`). **A tiled picture is laid from its rectangle's lower left** (Unity's `Image.GenerateTiledSprite`: whole tiles up and to the right, the last row and column cut), where Bevy lays its tiles from the top left; a grid is a unit higher than its cells for its last line, so until 0.160.0 its cells' lines lay a unit (a pixel at 1440 lines) above the game's and the bottom cell's edge showed inside the frame. The app draws such a picture in a clip of its rectangle, grown upward to whole tiles (`health_view::tiles_grown`), whole tiled pictures and the tiled parts of sliced ones alike; set beside a capture of the game, the lines fall on the game's pixels. **A grid's view is as large as its cells** wherever it lies (`GridView.OnGridResized`: its `sizeDelta` set to `ItemViewFactory.GetCellPixelSize`, 63 units a cell and one more), so a container's own layout, whose views may be larger in the prefab, has each sized to its grid about its pivot (`screen::LayoutGrid::sized`): the RUSH 100's main view is 320 by 448 in `UI/Rig Layouts/rush_100` and 316 by 442 for its 5 by 7 cells. Until 0.160.1 the app kept the prefab's size, and a strip of cells showed at the right and the bottom.
- **Laid out to a fraction of a pixel, edges filled by a pixel's middle** (read 2026-10-31 from the game's capture at 2560x1440, built 0.160.2). The engine never rounds a piece of a screen to the screen's pixels: each lies where its units put it at the screen's scale (a unit is 1.333 pixels at 1440 lines), and a pixel is filled by whatever covers its middle, a picture sampled there between its own pixels. A border kept a pixel to a pixel (`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler`: a grid's `border_shadow` frame, a quick view's, a thing's `border_generic`) is so drawn at a fraction too, so its light line falls whole on a pixel or half lit across it as its place falls: the game's own edges differ by place, the same three cells apart (the quick bar's third, eighth and eleventh views half lit at 1440 lines, the rest whole). The app, with `TARKOV UI`, lays out every screen unrounded from its roots, as the engine does (`health_view::lay_out_unrounded`; Bevy rounds a node's place unless told not to, and a piece set unrounded under a rounded panel lies from that panel's rounded corner, which moved the edges half a pixel until 0.160.2), its edges hard (`UiAntiAlias::Off`); the texts alone are moved to whole pixels once laid out, less than half a pixel (`texts_on_pixels`): the app draws them as pictures at the screen's pixels, which a fraction off are blurred, where the game's are drawn by their distance fields.
- **A thing in a slot** (`SlotView.CreateItemView`, `ScaleItem`; read 2026-10-31, built 0.160.4): its view is stretched over the slot's place (`_slotPlace`: in a container's row the slot's whole panel, `Slot Panel`, 125 units square), so its ground reaches the slot's edges; the slot's frame when full (`Full Border`: `slot_border_full`, 16 pixels sliced 7, 6 pixels out of the slot, kept a pixel to a pixel) puts its light pixel the second out and a dark one the first, both outside. Its picture is its icon at its own size (as large as its cells and a unit more), shrunk to fit the slot where it is larger either way, never grown (`ItemView.UpdateScale` with the slot's place as `_iconScale`: the smaller share where either is under one, else one), in the slot's middle. Until 0.160.4 the app kept a line of a pixel inside the slot and fitted the picture inside that: a pixel of the panel between the frame and the ground, the picture 2 to 4 pixels smaller. The same rule sizes every view's picture: a grid's view is its icon's own size, a part's in the edit build screen fitted.
- **A container's row as high as its grids' view** (`GeneratedGridsView`, `TemplatedGridsView`; built 0.160.4): its own layout's height where it has one (the Triton's `Commando`, 275 by 259, its grids' extent), else its grids' with the default row's padding under them (a unit: the pockets' row 65 high, not 64), as the loot panel's view already was. A pixel short until 0.160.4, every row under the pockets lay a pixel high of the game's (the backpack's frame on 774 for 775 at 1440 lines).
- **A thing's view in a grid, framed** (`GridItemView`, `grid_layout`; the edit build screen's parts, `modding_selectable_layout`; read 2026-10-31, built 0.160.2): the view lies at its first cell's corner (`GridView.SetItemViewPosition`: 63 units a cell) and is as large as its cells and a unit more (`ItemViewFactory.GetCellPixelSize`), so its edges lie on the grid's lines about its cells, shared with a neighbour's. Its ground (`Color Panel`, its kind's colour) is a unit inside its edges all round, so it stops at the lines; its border (`Border`: `border_generic`, a white line a pixel wide round a picture of 16, sliced 2 in, at (73, 81, 84), kept a pixel to a pixel) lies over the whole view, over its picture and tag and under its words; its picture is its icon at the icon's own size (`MainImage.SetNativeSize`), the icon made as large as the view (`GetCellPixelSize`), so the whole view, centred. The app draws the ground inside a border of the node's own that draws nothing (Bevy fills a node's ground inside its border) and lays the view's parts out from its corner in a node over it (`health_view::ViewFrame`, `inventory_view::game_item_view`). Until 0.160.2 the app's view lay a pixel inside the lines with a line of its own a pixel further in and its picture inside that: the cell's line, a gap, its line, a gap, its ground, where the game has the line, its border, its ground. A cell is 63 units at the screen's scale with its fraction, as the grid view tiles it: rounded to whole pixels until 0.160.3, the things fell a fraction of a pixel further off the view's lines at each row in a window whose unit is no whole share of a pixel (79.68 pixels a cell inside 2560 by 1366, drawn 80), their borders beside the lines several pixels down a drawer scrolled far.
- **The screen's places** (read 2026-10-15, late at night, built 0.21.0): the inventory screen's objects are laid out by their anchors on the screen (its items' panel is the whole screen; 1920 by 1080 on a 16:9 screen): the bar of tabs across the top, 43 high (`Tab Bar`); the left side (`Items Panel/LeftSide`) at 11.6 across and 48 down, 1213.3 by 902, its row the left panel and the containers' column; the right's panel for a container opened or the stash (`Stash Panel`) at 1228 across and 75 down, 680 by 873; the quick-use bar at 935 down. The left panel's own ground is its plain picture, black at 0.498. Over the left side lies its frame (`Border`, out of the row): `Allscreens_Borders_Default`, a 3 by 3 white texture with a clear middle pixel, sliced a pixel in from each side, white at 0.149, at 2 units to the pixel. **Its texture is sampled at its nearest pixel** (`m_TextureSettings.m_FilterMode` point): blended, its stretched middle would lay a pale wash over both panels. With `TARKOV UI` the app's page is as large as the window and its parts are put at those places by the anchors, on a screen of any shape; its own tabs stay, in the band of the game's bar (the game's are not drawn yet), and the app's readout and meters are hidden while the screen is open.
- **The containers' column scrolls** (`ScrollRectNoDrag`, a `ScrollRect` that cannot be dragged, on `Containers Scrollview`; read by the engine's order of its fields, its pointers to its content, view and bar followed): what moves is the column of rows (`Content`), which is as high as its rows with its padding (`ContentSizeFitter`: 120 above, 60 below, 45 between); what shows of it is the scroll view's own rectangle (`m_Viewport` points to itself), the whole height of the column; it is cut to its parent (`Scrollview Parent`, a mask), 595 wide. **The wheel** moves it 63 units a notch (`m_ScrollSensitivity`; a notch reads one), up for a notch up, over the scroll view alone (its rectangle starts 37 in from the column's left; the wheel over the strip left of it or over the bar does nothing, the engine's events going to no scroll view there), and only while its bar shows (`ScrollRectNoDrag.OnScroll`); **stopped at its ends** (`MovementType.Clamped`, no elasticity, no inertia). **Its bar** (`Scrollbar`, at the column's right edge, 10 wide, black; shown only while the rows are higher than the view, `ScrollbarVisibility.AutoHide`) runs from the bottom up (`Direction.BottomToTop`): its handle (`Handle`, (0.714, 0.757, 0.780)) takes the share of the sliding area (10 in from each end) that the view is of the rows, 8 units longer at each end, 6 wide, placed by the anchors the bar sets on it (`Scrollbar.UpdateVisuals`). Held on the handle and moved as far as a drag takes, the handle follows the mouse from where it was taken (`OnBeginDrag`, `UpdateDrag`); held elsewhere on the bar, the bar moves a handle's share toward the press on every frame until the handle is under it, twice on the frame it is pressed (`ClickRepeat`, which waits for each frame's end), each value kept to four places. The handle is tinted by the bar's state (`Selectable`, colour tint): idle white, under the mouse or chosen 0.961, held 0.784, each change going over linearly in 0.1 s (`fadeDuration`, `Graphic.CrossFadeColor`); the bar is chosen when pressed and stays so until something else is pressed (`EventSystem` selection: `PROVISIONAL`, the screens' input module not read). Nothing of the game's puts the column back to its top when the screen opens (`AutoZeroing` off): the app keeps its place too. The drag's threshold is the app's 10 pixels (`ESTIMATE`, as for a carried thing).
- **The bar of tabs** (`Tab Bar`; read 2026-10-15, late at night, built 0.22.0): across the screen's top, 43 high, its ground `tab_bar_gradient`; a row (`TabSizeController`, its middle 10 in) of eight tabs in the game's order (`TabElement.OrderNumber`): OVERALL, GEAR, HEALTH, SKILLS, MAP, TASKS, ACHIEVEMENTS, PRESTIGE. **All tabs are as wide as the widest asks** (`UpdateLayout`): each asks for its words' sample (`SizeSample`, a text that only sizes it) laid out by TextMeshPro, and 85 more (`_widthAdjustment`); each tab's left is `(width + gap) * OrderNumber` from the row's middle, the gap −26, so that they overlap: **each tab over the one after it, the chosen over all** (`TabGroup.SelectTab`, as the inventory's group is made, `setAsLastSibling` on: every tab set last among its siblings from the last to the first, then the chosen one; read after the chosen tab's words were found cut by the next tab's edge). **The mouse goes to the tab drawn on top**, so where two overlap the earlier has it, unless the later is the chosen one. **A tab is chosen by a click** (`Tab.OnPointerClick` → `TabGroup.SelectionChangedHandler`: the button let go over the tab it went down on; a click on the chosen tab does nothing): nothing changes as the button goes down; on the frame it comes up the old tab goes idle, the new one is chosen, the old page goes and the new one is built whole (GEAR and HEALTH not split over frames after the opening, `ShowWithTab`), with `ButtonClick`, and no fade. **Compared with** `courses10`'s `inventory-tabs`: five switches, each on the release's frame (10 to 13 frames after the press); OVERALL lit at (264, 47), within GEAR too, with HEALTH chosen, and HEALTH pressed at (734, 38), within SKILLS too, with MAP chosen. Built (`inventory_view::choose_tab` on the change from held to hovered, `game_tabs`' buttons spawned from the last to the first with the chosen one raised): the app's run (`local\run_tabs_s186.sh`) switches on the release's frame, its sounds there (the SKILLS page's two `MenuDropdownSelect` with them), and lights OVERALL at (264, 47) with HEALTH chosen; until then a tab was chosen as the button went down, and where two overlapped the later had the mouse. **A text's width as TextMeshPro lays it out**: each letter's glyph's advance in its font asset (`TMP_FontAsset`: sampled at 48, advances in whole pixels at that size) at the text's size over 48, and after every letter but the last the asset's `normalSpacingOffset` (2 for Bender) and the text's own spacing in hundredths of the size, kerning where the text keeps it (the tabs' do not); bold in the asset's bold typeface (its weight table's 700) where it has one, else with `boldSpacing` besides; rounded up to a hundredth. The widest is ACHIEVEMENTS (113.86, so every tab 198.86 wide) in English. Their words are the install's for each tab's key (`LocalizedText`: `OVERALL`, `HEALTH_TAB`, `Prestige` and the rest), in the case its text's style puts them (`Prestige` drawn in capitals). **Each tab** (`Tab`): its idle version (`Normal`: a fill, its outline, its icon and its words in #c7d3d9, Bender 16) or, chosen, its chosen one (`Selected`: a light fill, its outline, the icon and the words in black, bold) (`Tab.UpdateVersion`); idle, with the mouse on it its hover fill and outline in place of its own (`Tab.Hover`); a shadow at its right (`tab_shadow`, under the next tab). **A tab's fill is cut to the tab's shape**: the fill (`tab_idle_fill`, `tab_hover_fill`, `tab_selected_fill`, tiled from the tab's lower left at its own 80 by 32) lies under a mask (`FillingMask`, `tab_mask` sliced to the tab, 13 and 36 kept at its two sides) that does not show itself (`Mask.showMaskGraphic` off): what of the fill lies where the mask is not clear is drawn (the mask's stencil is written with alpha clipping). The icons keep their sprites' shape (`preserveAspect`), from the left. The prestige tab is not worked in a raid (`SetInteractable`), so it is never lit under the mouse, and its mark of a level ready to finish (`NewState`) shows only when the next level's task can be finished and has not been seen (`InventoryScreen.PrestigeLevelAvailabilityChanged`), never for a character with none. The game shows the prestige tab only in its regular session (`SessionMode`), which SPT's server is not read for: it is shown (`PROVISIONAL`). **The app**: the bar drawn from the scene where the game draws it, the fills cut to their masks from the two pictures' pixels at the window's scale (Bevy's screens have no stencil), afresh when the tab chosen or under the mouse changes; the app's own `OPTIONS` drawn as the first tab is, without an icon, at the bar's other end as far in (TRP's own place for it). Compared with nothing; the widths set beside the sizes the scene keeps for its texts sized to their words (`probe textwidths level44`): of 115, 53 the same to the hundredth (sizes 11 to 42, the tabs' samples among them), most of the rest short by whole letters' spacing, as if kept from before the asset had it; the bold rule is TextMeshPro's published one, not borne out by the scene (its few bold sizes disagree with one another).
- **The panel for a container opened** (read 2026-10-15, latest at night, built 0.23.0): a container looted in a raid is shown in the stash panel's simple panel (`ItemsPanel.Show`: `SimpleStashPanel.Show` for any loot but a corpse's gear, which has `ComplexStashPanel`); with none open, neither shows, and the right side is empty. Its script names its parts (read by its fields' order after Odin's data and the input node's field): in a raid its header shows (`_header`, the search being out of a raid only: `InitSearch`), a toggle in its disabled look (its back, its stripes cut to their mask, its icon `StashHeaderIcons_Off`, 20 by 20: the loot's mark; it and its word laid by their rows at what they ask, since 0.104.2: they had been drawn at no size), its words the install's for `LOOT` (`_simpleGridName`, "STASH" for the stash); the container's name in its box at the panel's top right (`_containerNamePanel`, shown for a container that is not the player's), the container's short name in the install's words in capitals (`ItemController.ContainerName`, given the short name by `LootItem.CreateLootContainer`; the app keeps its own crates' names), the box sized to the name by its row and fitter, growing left and up from 2 in and 4 above the corner; the money, what is only in the lobby (sorting, pin, lock, priority), the sorting table's and the search's tabs and the mark of what is had from outside a raid off. **The column of filters** (`FilterPanel`, a vertical group of 18 tabs 32 high and 2 apart to the panel's left) shows when the grids do (`GridView.Show`): its first, all, chosen (`Selected`), each other one at 0.15 of its alpha, its picture and its icon (`FilterTab.SetInteractable`), unless something of its kind lies in the container's grids (`SetFilterTabAlpha`): weapons, magazines, rounds and their boxes, medicine, food and drink, knives, parts, grenades, barter, rigs, visors, containers, armour, info, keys, special things, things found in the raid (`FilterPanel._filterRules`, by the game's item classes; here by the install's database's nodes of the same names, `PROVISIONAL`; found in the raid never, the app not keeping it). **The grids** are made as the containers' column makes a container's (`SearchableItemView.Show`: `ContainedGridsView.CreateGrids`, its own layout or the default row) into the scroll view's content (`_gridsContainer`, a column with 1 of padding at the left), cut to the panel's mask (its own picture not shown, `Mask.showMaskGraphic` off) and scrolled as the containers' column is, by the wheel 63 units a notch and by its bar at the panel's right (`ScrollRectNoDrag`; this one is elastic, `MovementType.Elastic`, with no elasticity: let past an end, it is back within a hundredth of a unit on the same frame, Unity's `SmoothDamp` taking its least time); TRP's rule that a container opens at its top holds. Over them its frame (`border_shadow`). **Unity's layout, more of it**: a column (`VerticalLayoutGroup`) is laid out as a row is, down (the filters); a group sized to what it holds (`ContentSizeFitter` on a layout group) takes its children's least or preferred size (`LayoutUtility`: a group's from its children's with its padding and spacing, overridden by a `LayoutElement`), about its pivot (the header's row stands above the panel's top). Compared with nothing.
- **The quick-use panel** (`Items Panel/Quick Use Panel`, `InventoryScreenQuickAccessPanel`; read 2026-10-15, latest at night, built 0.24.0): at the screen's bottom (558.5 across and 935 down at 1920 by 1080, 854.2 by 115), a header (its words the install's for `QUICK USE`) over a row of two groups of views, 64 by 64 and 70 apart, 10 more between the groups: the grenade's (`G`), the four slots with a key of their own (`ItemV`, `Item1`, `Item2`, `Item3`: the sheath, the holster, the sling, the back) and the seven keys' things (`Item4` to `Item10`), named by the panel's script (`_grenadeSlot`, `_boundSlots`, `_boundItems`: dictionaries kept as their keys' names and their views). The script shows them all (`Show`). **Each view** (`QuickSlotView`): a dark ground, a cell, its install place, its caption (the short name in the install's words of what it holds, none for nothing: `ShowInfoPanel`), its key's label (`HotKey`: the binding's keys' words) and its border, the caption, label and border over the thing. None of the inventory's views names a `Background`, so none lights for the thing in the hands (`SwitchVisualSelection` does nothing; the raid's own quick bar is another). **The thing in a view** (`QuickSlotItemView`, the Resource `quick_slot_layout`): no ground of its kind's colour (its `Background` is not drawn: `courses10`'s `hover-tooltips` at frame 3000 lists only the view's picture and value over the cell; the app drew its kind's colour at 0.302 there until 0.182.1, and in the battle screen's bar, the same prefab), its picture turned 45 degrees and made as large as fits the view: its size turned, `(w - h, w + h)` over the square root of two, each part within the view's, the smaller share (`UpdateScale`; a square picture by its height alone); its found-in-raid mark only where the view's context asks (none here). **Its figure** (`_resourceValue`, the object `Value`; `TryGetResourceText`: not only for a thing with side effects): shown for a thing with, asked in this order, a side effect (`SideEffectComponent`, a knife naming `StimulatorBuffs`: its doses over its `MaxResource`, each to two places with none kept where nought (`0.##`), in green, `<color=#009225>`; the app spends none, so full), a resource (`ResourceComponent`, a barter thing's, `0.##`: none can be bound, `IsAtBindablePlace`), a medkit (`MedKitComponent`, meds whose `MaxHpResource` is above nought: what is left over the most, rounded) or food or drink whose `MaxResource` is above one (`FoodDrinkComponent`: `HpPercent` over it, rounded); none for anything else. Its text Bender Outline at 11 in its own red (1, 0, 0), its box as large as its words ask (`ContentSizeFitter`), anchored at the view's bottom middle by its own bottom middle, 3 units up; the inventory's bar and the battle screen's alike, the battle bar drawn again as a figure changes. Compared with `courses10`'s `hover-tooltips` (the water's `60/60` in slot 4): the game's centred on its view and 3 units over its bottom as ours, red; our ink 36.8 units wide against the game's 37.1. Not built: the picture drawn red for a weapon with a known malfunction (`UpdateInfo`). The picture's own size is taken as its cells at 63 units (`PROVISIONAL`: the game's is its icon's rectangle). Binding stays the app's (a key let go over a thing); the game binds by a thing dropped on a view too, not built. Compared with nothing.
- **Set beside captures of the game** (its GEAR and HEALTH tabs at 2560 by 1440 and 1920 by 1080). The app's two tabs were captured at the same sizes (`--resolution`) and set beside them; what differed was read from the game and built, as follows. Set beside them after: the tabs, the slots, the strip, the containers' rows, the special slots, the quick-use bar, `BACK` and the version line at the same places to the pixel at both sizes, the views' words and figures as the game's (what is in them differs: the app's run carries the crate's Triton and Berkut). Not set beside: the hover of `BACK`, a magazine counted in words, a key's uses, the screens at other shapes.
- **What lies behind the screen** (`InventoryBlur`, on the game's camera prefab `Cam2` in `resources.assets`; `screen_blur`): the world, blurred. The inventory screen turns it on as it opens in a raid and off as it shuts (`InventoryScreen.Show`, `Close`: `CameraManager.Blur`), its value going from 0 to 1 over half a second of frames and back as long (`InventoryBlur.Update`). Each frame the camera's picture is copied into a square of 256 by 256, passed down to 128 by 128 and back up eight times (the prefab's `_upsampleTexDimension`, `_downsampleTexDimension`, `_blurCount`; each copy one linear sample, Unity's `Graphics.Blit` into temporary textures), and the picture is then the sharp one and the square mixed by the value: its material `FastBlur`'s fragment program, read from its compiled form (DXBC in the shader's packed blob), samples both and writes `value * (blurred - sharp) + sharp`, nothing else (no darkening: the captured raid was dark). Since 0.62.0 worked in gamma space, as every one of the camera's effects (`health.md`, "The camera's effects in gamma space"). The screen itself has no ground of its own: the app's dark sheet over the world (`BACK` at 0.94) is gone with `TARKOV UI` on.
- **The screens composed in gamma space** (`gamma_ui`). The game lays its pictures and colours over one another and over the world by their stored, gamma-encoded numbers: in a capture of the game the left side's frame (white at 0.149) over a ground of 18 comes to 51 (gamma's numbers: 53; linear light: 110), and a panel's black at 0.498 leaves 0.5 of the world behind it (linear light would leave 0.73). Bevy blends in linear light, which drew everything half clear lighter (the left panel's foot, white at 6% at most, as a pale band). With `TARKOV UI` on the frame is encoded to its gamma numbers before the screens are drawn and decoded after, and the screens' two shaders (Bevy's `ui.wgsl` and `ui_texture_slice.wgsl`, patched where they are loaded, not copied) encode their colours and pictures as they read them. The world is untouched; the app's own look (the switch off) blends as before. **A picture is filtered on its stored numbers too**: the game's engine blends a picture between its four nearest pixels as they are stored, where the hardware decodes an sRGB picture before it blends; a pixel half on a light line came out lighter in the app (a grid's frame half lit at 72 to 77 for the game's 53 to 63 at 1440 lines), and a line moved a fraction of a pixel by the scrolling looked twice as wide. The patched shaders read the four pixels at their middles, encode them and blend them (`gamma_ui::gamma_sample`); a picture with levels of detail, and one the install sets to its nearest pixel (whose sample is one of the four), keep the hardware's sample, encoded. Set beside the game's capture: the half-lit edges at 61, 66, 54 and 81 for the game's 56 to 59, 63, 53 and 79, the pixels beside the lines within 4 of the game's.
- **The bar's right end** (`InventoryScreen/BackButton`, a `DefaultUIButton`): `BACK` in the game's place (25 in from the right, 3 down, 200 by 36), its words in their colour (0.906, 0.898, 0.831) in Bender at 28 (the font asset `Bender Shadowed SDF`, whose face is Bender Regular), its ground clear at rest; a click on it shuts the screen as Escape does (`ButtonFeedback.OnPointerClick`: pressed and let go over it). Its hover (`DefaultUIButtonAnimation`: the ground sliding in from 15 to the left and fading in over 0.2 s, the words fading out and back in black) is not built: its colours are in Odin's data, not read. The app's `OPTIONS` is drawn after the game's last tab as the row would lay a ninth (TRP's own place for it, at the bar's right; the game's `BACK` keeps the bar's end).
- **The prestige tab** (`InventoryScreen.Show`: `SetInteractable(… && !InRaid)`): in a raid it does not work, and its group (`Tab._canvasGroup`, the tab's own `CanvasGroup`) is drawn at 0.3 (`SetUnlockStatus`), fill, outline, icon and words alike. The scene reader now knows the engine's own parts of an object (a `CanvasGroup`) that scripts point to.
- **A thing's view, its words and marks** (`GridItemView.UpdateInfo`, `SetItemValue`, `SlotItemView`): what it writes at its bottom right, by its kind, the first that applies: a weapon its magazine's rounds over its capacity (or, with none, its chambers' rounds over their count), red under 0.15 full, else #dadabc, by the view's format (in a slot `<color><size=30>count</size></color>/most`, in a grid the count coloured and `/most` plain); armour, a plate, an insert or a rig that holds plates its own and its parts' durability summed over their greatest, white, red at none (every `RepairableComponent` in it where it has an `ArmorComponent` or an `ArmorHolderComponent`; since 0.104.2, as made: the character's armour takes no hits); a magazine its rounds over its capacity in #dadabc; a box of rounds its count; medicine its resource left over its whole (`MaxHpResource`, where it has one) and food or drink over its whole where that is more than 1, both in #ff5335, rounded; a barter thing its resource in #dadabc; a key its uses left over its uses (`MaximumNumberOfUsage`) in #ff5335 (the app keeps no count of a key's uses: as new); else a stack's count. **A magazine's count as the character knows it** (`Magazine.GetAmmoCountByLevel`, no skill of magazine drills, the app having none): `0` empty, `?` unchecked, the count when full (one short in a weapon worn, its chamber's), else the words `~ Empty`, `< 1/2`, `~ 1/2`, `~ Full` by a third of it. **Checked** (the profile's `CheckedMagazines`): every magazine carried as the run begins (`LocalPlayer.Create`), and the weapon's own as it is checked in the hands in first person (`FirearmController.CheckAmmo`), kept again from the start when the run begins again; a magazine looted later is not, and shows `?`. A weapon's round (`Weapon.AmmoCaliber`, the template's `ammoCaliber` without `Caliber`) under its caption in a slot (`Info Label`), at the bottom left in a grid (`Inscription`). In a slot a weapon's mastering, its mark (`MasteringIcon`, gold) and level (`0` and the level and one, `SlotItemView.UpdateInfo`; here `--mastering` for every weapon), and since 0.209.1 its slider (`MasteringSlider`, from the bottom up, 0 to 100, its value the share into the level times 100 cut to a whole number), laid out as the prefab lays them: the slider's column first in the bottom row (padding left -3, top 23, bottom 26: the track 10 units wide at the view's left, from 26 units below its top to 29 above its bottom; its fill area inset, the fill its share of it and 5 units more, so a level just begun shows 5), the mark at its sprite's size (22 by 29) at the view's left edge by its row's padding of -10, its bottom 3 above the view's, the level right of it (the slider's two layout elements: one left out, one 10 wide; UGUI leaves an object out only where all of them say so). Compared with a capture of the game's, 2026-11-07, to a pixel or two; and a weapon's or a knife's durability's bar at the right, its track sliced and its fill as the template's durability over its most (a weapon as made: full), the value put left of it; the mark's size and place and the level's as that capture has them at 1080 lines (the views' layout groups are not solved: `ESTIMATE`). A part's marks at the bottom left (`ItemViewStats.SetItemIcon`): its class's ground (`Mod Types/icon_type_mod_<generic, gear, vital>`) and its kind's mark over it (`Mod Tactical Types/icon_mod_<kind>`), by the database's names of the game's classes (`health_view::MOD_CLASSES`, `MOD_KINDS`; `Muzzle` and `Sights` for the code's `MuzzleMod` and `SightMod`, `PROVISIONAL`): a magazine the green gear ground and the magazine. A thing in one of the four slots with a key of its own is labelled with that key's words as a thing bound to a key is (the AKM on the sling, `2`). A caption is bold where its text is (the slot's `Name`). A repair buff on the thing or on anything in it (`GridItemView.TryGetBuff`, a rare one first; malfunctions spec, "Repair buffs"), known: its ground 209, 153, 45 at 32 of 255 where nothing red colours it, and the buff's attribute icon at its own size (white at 180 of 255) over its value, in the bottom right column (`RepairBuffIcon`: the column lower right, one unit between its members). Not built: the found-in-raid, insured and repair-required marks, an armour's durability as its figure, the mastering's slider at the view's left.
- **The containers' rows** (`ContainersPanel.Show`, `SlotView.Show`, `SearchableSlotView.CreateSlots`). A thing not shown in its slot (`NotShownInSlot`: the pockets) has the row's slot panel off, so its grids begin at the row's left; the pockets' row has 10 more between its parts. A container with slots of its own but an armour's (the pockets' three; an armoured rig's plate and insert slots are `ArmorSlot`s, which `CreateSlots` leaves out: so it shows none, as a capture of the game's Bagariy shows) has the row's special slots' panel turned on last in the row: its header (`InventoryScreen/SpecialSlotsHeader` in capitals, SPECIAL SLOTS) and a view of 64 units for each slot, 10 apart, its striped fill, its empty border a unit outside and its picture (`SpecialSlot`) in its middle; the page's boxes over them take the mouse. **A special slot that holds a thing** (`SlotView`, by whether it holds one) has its fill, its empty border and its picture off and its full border on, six units outside (`probe containers` lists the template's parts as saved); the thing's view there is laid out as a special slot's (`SlotItemView._isSpecSlotLayout`, for an address `IsSpecialSlotAddress`: a slot whose name holds "SpecialSlot", or any slot of a thing not shown in its slot, the pockets): no mastering and no durability bar, its value both figures at 11 (`ValidateMastering`, `ValidateDurability`, `ValueFormat`); a weapon keeps its round's words (`Weapon.AmmoCaliber`, "26x75" on an RSP-30). Until 0.185.1 the app drew the empty slot's "SPEC" behind a thing and a flare's mastering and bar; seen in the app, not set beside a take (none holds a thing in a special slot). A row is as high as what it shows. The dogtag's view (the game hangs it in the pockets' row, where a character's own is not shown) is in the equipment panel under the armband, as the game's panel has it.
- **The character's own pockets and dogtag**: a character of the game always has its pockets and its tag. Every run begins with the pockets of the install's Standard edition's BEAR, empty (`Loadout`; the edition and side `PROVISIONAL`, the player's profile not read), and a BEAR's tag (`dogtags_bear`; the install's starting characters have none, SPT's server gives a profile its own as it is made); both kept when the run begins again.
- **Turned pictures**: a screen's object may be turned about the screen's axis (the sheath's empty picture, 45 degrees): the scene reader keeps the turn (`Layout::turn`) and the app turns it about its middle.
- **Bold texts**: a text bold in its style is drawn in its font asset's bold typeface (found by the asset's face where its name does not begin with its font's: `DON'T USE! Jovanny Lemonad - Bender Bold SDF`, face Bender Bold) and asked of Bevy at the bold weight, which finds a face by its family and weight (Bender Regular and Bender Bold are both Bender): until 0.29.0 every bold text of the game's panels was drawn regular.
- **The version's line** (`PreloaderUI._alphaVersionLabel`, `Preloader UI/BottomPanel/Content/UpperPart/AlphaLabel` in the preloader's scene): along the screen's foot over every screen, in its band 31.5 high 2 over the foot, 12 in, in Bender at 16 in its colour at a third; with `TARKOV UI` on, saying `TRP` and the app's version where the game's says its build and SPT's its own (the words the app's).
- **A text's rim and shadow** (the red text on medical items has one). TextMeshPro draws a text by its font asset's material, which may draw something under the letters: read from the material (`material_properties`: its keywords, floats and colours), a dark rim all round where it has an outline (`_OutlineWidth`) or an underlay laid unmoved under the letters and dilated (`UNDERLAY_ON`, `_UnderlayDilate`; `Bender Outline SDF`: an outline of 0.1 and an underlay of 0.6, black), a drop shadow where the underlay is moved (`_UnderlayOffsetX`, `_UnderlayOffsetY`; `Bender Shadowed SDF`: 0.3 right and down, black at 0.33). How wide or how far, in pixels for a letter of one, the atlas sampled at the asset's point size (48 for Bender): an underlay moves by its offset times its scale ratio (`_ScaleRatioC`) times the gradient's scale (`_GradientScale`) of the atlas's pixels (the shader's offset of its texture); an outline and an underlay grow by half their width times their ratio (`_ScaleRatioA`, `_ScaleRatioC`) in the distance field's own measure, whose whole spans twice the atlas's padding (`_GradientScale` less one: 10 for Bender, so 0.6 of Bender Outline is 0.91 pixels at a size of 11, a pixel and a fifth at 1440 lines); **the letters themselves are widened by the face's dilation** (`_FaceDilate`, 0.15 in Bender's materials: the shader's weight, `_FaceDilate * _ScaleRatioA * 0.5`, in the same measure: 0.42 pixels at a size of 11 at 1440 lines), and the rim's edge lies as much further out; **both edges fade over the shader's ramp**, one unit of the distance over its scale, which comes to twice the padding over the gradient's scale in pixels (1.8) at any size. The app draws the face's widening as four copies of the words in their own colour moved that far each way (where it comes to 0.15 pixels or more), a rim as twelve copies in its colour moved round to its edge less a quarter of the ramp and a fainter ring (0.4 of its alpha) a quarter of the ramp outside it, and a shadow as one copy moved, under the words (`health_view::effect_words`): every text of the game's panels, a thing's caption, round and figures among them (a medkit's red figures have the outline's rim). Until 0.33.0 the measure was taken as the gradient's scale once (half as wide: half a pixel, unseen, where the game's texts on things, rounds and uses show a rim). Every text of the views of a thing and of the HEALTH tab is drawn with its font asset's own material (`probe textmats`: no text names a preset of its own). Bolder and rounder since 0.34.0 (set beside a capture of the game's GEAR tab at 1440 lines, the game's rim is bolder and rounder; before, the face was not widened and the rim had eight hard copies), set beside that capture by eye (`local\shots\ours_text_zoom2_s83.png`, `user_gear_s83.png`). The ramp as two rings and the face as copies (`PROVISIONAL`: copies of the words, not a distance field; the field's measure from TextMeshPro's generator as published, not read). **Since 0.35.0 this is only the way a text is drawn where its font asset could not be read; otherwise it is drawn as the game's (next).**
- **A text drawn as the game's** (compared in a recording of both games: the letters had been too bold). Set beside the recording, the app's item captions and figures were too bold and too narrow, its slot headers as the game's. Read then from the install: **the two font assets' differences** (`probe fontscale`): Bender Normal SDF (headers, the panels) has a bold typeface (Bender Bold SDF), a spacing of 2 hundredths of the size after every letter (`normalSpacingOffset`) and a material that widens the letters a little (`_FaceDilate` 0.04); **Bender Outline SDF** (the things' captions, rounds and figures) has **no bold typeface**, a spacing of **10** (and 8 more for a bold letter, `boldSpacing`), and its material widens by 0.15, outlines by 0.1, lays a black underlay dilated by 0.6 and sharpens by 0.3 (`_Sharpness`); a caption is bold with a spacing of −4 of its own. Both materials are drawn by the full `TextMeshPro/Distance Field` shader (read from the material's shader: its outline is always drawn). The app had drawn a bold caption in Bender Bold's own letters, with no spacing at all, and the effect by copies of the words, whose soft edges laid one over another came out near solid. **Now every text whose asset is read is drawn from that asset's own atlas** (its signed distance field: 1024 by 1024, one byte a texel, a half on the glyph's edge, a gradient's scale of eleven texels to each half, as measured across a letter) **by the shader's own formulas** with its material's figures (`trp_data::tmp_text`, whose head gives them): laid out by its glyphs (their advances, bearings and atlas rectangles, `m_GlyphTable`), the asset's and the text's spacing after every letter in hundredths of the text's own size (`currentEmScale` goes by `m_fontSize`, not by a `<size>` run's: a figure's large digits are spaced as its small ones), a bold letter in the asset's bold typeface where it has one, else made bold by the shader (`_WeightBold` 0.5: a quarter of it added to the face's dilation) and spaced by `boldSpacing`; the font's kerning where the text keeps it; lines broken at a box's width where asked (a window's description); a line that does not break, truncated (`m_overflowMode` 3), cut at the first letter whose pen (the kerning between the letters before it in) plus its own advance passes the box's width less its margins and plus 0.0001 (`GenerateTextMesh`; with an ellipsis, 1, as many letters as leave room for "…"), and a text's preferred width (`CalculatePreferredValues`) kerned the same way (the cut had no kerning, so a one-cell caption of `M-LOK 4.1"`, 60.69 units unkerned and 59.91 kerned in a box of 60, lost its `"`, which the game shows); aligned by where the last letter that shows ends (`lineInfo.maxAdvance`: a trailing space not counted), from the first line's ascender to the last's descender. Each glyph's quad, with room for what the shader draws round it, is shaded pixel by pixel (the field blended between texels) and laid over the ones before as the screen blends (`One, OneMinusSrcAlpha`); the words are an image of their own, made as they are drawn or changed (a figure the panel's script writes anew included), kept while alike, at the window's own pixels (`health_view::draw_tmp_texts`). A caption's outline wears its white face down within the glyph and its underlay lays about two pixels of black round it at 1440 lines, as in the game's. **Set beside the recording** (`local\shots\tmp_gear4_s84.png` beside its frame at 36 s; the recording keeps black at about 14 and the app's white at about 201, being of a limited range): "AKM" 33 by 11 pixels with 48.5% of it bright (the game's: 33 by 11, 48.2%; the app's before: 28 by 11, 63%); "ARMBAND" 88 by 14, 45.9% (87 by 14, 46.1%); the large "30/30" 86 by 30, 29.0% (85 by 31, 29.3%), the gap in its "30" 6 pixels (5); headers where the game's are to the pixel ("TACTICAL RIG" 880 to 999 in both). **Not yet the game's**: a slot view's things right of its middle 2 to 3 pixels left of the game's (the caption, the round, the figure and the durability's bar alike: the view's layout, not the words); the shader's screen-space measure taken as an overlay canvas's (`PROVISIONAL`: √2 times the screen's pixels to the atlas's; a camera's canvas the same where it is orthographic); a text's auto-sizing, its overflow modes and its rich text beyond sizes and colours not built.
- **The tabs not built** (dimmed as the game dims its prestige tab): OVERALL, MAP, TASKS, ACHIEVEMENTS and PRESTIGE drawn with the tab itself solid and its words and icon at 0.3 of their alpha, none of them lit under the mouse or taken by a click; a departure of the screen's for all five, TRP's own, until they are built. The game draws a tab that does not work in a raid (its prestige tab) with the whole tab at 0.3 (`Tab.SetUnlockStatus`: its group), see-through, which was tried for all five and dropped: see-through, the tabs show one another where they overlap. A text's rim or shadow is drawn at its words' alpha.
- **The window that shows a thing** (the inspect window opened by a double click). A double click on a thing that is not a container, medicine, food or drink (`ItemUiContext.Inspect`) opens the game's `InfoWindow`, read from the prefab the preloader's item context points to (`ItemUiContext._infoWindowTemplate`; `sharedassets49.assets` in the player's install; `probe infowindow`), 670 by 500: its caption the thing's name (`ItemSpecificationPanel.ItemName`: the database's `<id> Name` in the install's words; a weapon's followed by its build's name, `BriefItemName`, as its tooltip's: built 2026-11-02, `courses10`'s `weapon-inspect-menu`, "Colt M4A1 5.56x45 assault rifle T.O 2K24"), under it its kind as the handbook files it (`_handbookNode.Category`: the item's category and its parents', the first at the root, in the install's words, " > " between: "Keys > Mechanical keys"), at the right its weight to three places and the unit (`GetTruncatedWeightString`, `" kg"` in the install's words), its box sized to the words (`ContentSizeFitter`) with its icon at its left; the preview's ground and corners; the row of buttons, empty (`InitInteractionButtonsPanel` not built); the description (`<id> Description`) in its box, cut to it (its scrolling not built). As its scripts leave it for a thing of the range: no tag (`UpdateTag`), no wish-list mark, **the panel of the parts on a thing off** (`_modsPanel`, on for a thing with slots: not built), the mastering's bar off. **Since 0.99.0 its figures and its model** (`inspect-window.md`): a weapon's durability row and bars, the compact rows, the window as tall as they ask; the thing's model in the preview, turned by a drag and zoomed by the wheel, its loader until it is in (before, the thing's own picture stood there). **Where it opens** (`OpenWindow`): with no window open in the middle of the screen, else 40 units right of and below the top left corner of the window opened last (`WindowData.Index`), moved back onto the screen where it would leave it (`CorrectPositionResolution`). **How many** (both kinds of window): a fifth of a kind shuts the oldest of that kind, a ninth of all the oldest of all. Moved by its caption bar, shut by its cross or Escape; its sounds in `sound.md`. A thing not known ("Examining"): "Unknown item", its kind "?", no description, its figures "?", its model black. Every thing's texts are read as the run begins (`trp_data::inventory::item_texts`). With `TARKOV UI` off the app's own window says what it said and the description besides. Compared with nothing: captured at 1440 lines (`local\shots\inspect_key_1440_s83.png`), not set beside the game's.
- **How the pieces are drawn, as the engine draws them** (found in captures at 2560 by 1440 and two recordings of the game: the slots' headers cut off, the quick-use bar's label broken and its views spaced wrong, the menu's lines stretched with a black line through them, the shadow round the window cut and disconnected, the right of words cut off without their rim, a faint white line over the health's figures). Each was a way the app drew the game's pieces that the engine does not; at a scale of a third (1440 high) they showed, set beside a recording of the game (`SPT level 69.mp4`, frame 30 s and 105 s):
  - **Pieces laid out to a fraction of a pixel.** Rounded one by one to whole pixels (Bevy's layout), pieces that meet in the game's units fell a pixel apart or over one another: a slot's header is three pictures (an end, a middle tiled from a 16-unit picture, an end), and its middle stopped a pixel short of its right end, the background showing between; the menu's lines and the window's outline the same. The game's pictures are now placed exactly (`LayoutConfig { use_rounding: false }` on every picture's piece); texts keep their rounding.
  - **Edges not softened.** Bevy softens every node's edge over a pixel, so two see-through pieces side by side left a light seam where they meet (the quick-use views' dark grounds); with `TARKOV UI` the camera draws the interface with hard edges (`UiAntiAlias::Off`), as the game's engine draws its own.
  - **Each texture sampled at its edges as it is set** (`m_TextureSettings.m_WrapU`, `m_WrapV`): the interface's are clamped; drawn repeated, a picture's edge took in its far side (the faint line along the top of the left panel's foot).
  - **A pixel-perfect border's offsets in pixels** (`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler.UpdateScale`): the script divides the border's offsets from what it hangs from by the screen's scale, so a quick-use view's shadowed frame lies 5 pixels round it, not 5 units; set 5 units out, two views' shadows met in the gap between them and darkened it to black.
  - **A content-sized object in a group** (`ContentSizeFitter` in a `VerticalLayoutGroup` that does not set its width): the fitter sizes it on the axes the group leaves to it; the quick-use panel's header is its words and its padding (8 and 20), 128 units by 21, where it had kept its saved 100.
  - **A tiled picture with a border** (`Image.Type.Tiled`, `GenerateTiledSprite`): its border whole as a sliced one's, its edges and middle repeated at their own size; the quick-use header's slanted end is its right border, and tiled whole it was cut square.
  - **A view's caption over its border**: the game's border is a picture over the view, taking no room; a thing's view is now cut at its outer edge, not inside its border (`OverflowClipMargin::border_box`), where a caption's last letter and its dark rim at the right were lost.
  **Compared** in captures at 2560 by 1440 with the build before beside the one after, and with the recording where it shows the same thing: a header's right end continuous as the game's (its pixels within half a pixel of the recording's), the line over the foot gone, the quick-use views' frames and gaps as the game's (the gap's pixels 28 to 57 against the game's 37 to 60), the health's bars' frames a pixel nearer the game's, the grid's lines without the gap that had shown the ground. Not in either recording: the menu of a right click and the window that shows a thing (seen only before and after: the menu's line through each line, and the window's corner, gone). **Left as they were**: a view's caption and the slots' views 2 to 3 pixels right of the game's (the views' insets in their cells), the crate panel's `LOOT` set too high on its header (its text's group), the panels' cells about 10 levels darker than the recording's.
- **`OPTIONS` as a ninth tab, with the game's settings cog** (next to the others, with a single cog beside the word as the other tabs have their icons): the app's own tab drawn as the game's first tab is, where the row would lay a ninth (`TabSizeController`'s `width + gap` on from PRESTIGE, its square left end under PRESTIGE's slanted end as each tab's is under the one before), drawn under all but when chosen. Its icon objects (`Normal/Icon`, white; `Selected/Icon`, black) show the single cog beside the words of the game's GAME SETTINGS button on its raid screen (`Matchmaker Offline Raid Screen/Content/NonLayoutContainer/SettingsButton` in the menu's scene, `MenuUIScene`, `level48`: `IconIdle` with `icon_gear`, white, at rest, and `Icon` with `icon_gear_black` when lit, each 31 by 31, in the scene's own file, `sharedassets48.assets`): `icon_gear` at rest, `icon_gear_black` chosen, swapped in as the game's `Image.sprite` is and kept to their shape in the objects' own boxes, as the TASKS tab shows a menu's pictures (`service_tasks`, `service_tasks_selected`) in the same objects. 0.189.2 had the two cogs beside the word on the game's settings screen (`SettingsScreen/Caption/Settings Icon`, `settings_icon`). With `TARKOV UI` off the app's row has OPTIONS after PRESTIGE too, no tab with an icon. Before, from 0.31.0, it stood apart, mirrored, its slanted edge to the left, without an icon, and with `TARKOV UI` off at the row's far right. Seen in captures at 1920 by 1080, idle and chosen (`local\shots\tabs_after_s190.png`, `tabs_chosen_s190.png`; the single cog, `tabs_cog_s191.png`, `tabs_cog_chosen_s191.png`); the game has no such tab to compare with.
- **Not yet the game's** with it on: `BACK`'s hover, the found-in-raid, insured and repair-required marks (a repair buff's: 0.227.0), an armour's durability as its figure, a weapon's caption with its build (`BriefWeaponBuildName`), a container's window (the app's own; the window that shows a thing is the game's, above), the pages of the tabs the app has not built (OVERALL, MAP, TASKS, ACHIEVEMENTS, PRESTIGE: drawn dimmed, not worked), the filters' clicks (drawn, not worked), a container's search (its unsearched panel and its search button; the app's containers open searched), the corner glows (`Left Glow`, `Right Glow`).

## The battle screen's panels (`TARKOV UI`; read and built 2026-10-16)

Under `TARKOV UI`, the character's speed and stance panel is drawn as the game's, the health silhouette is shown in the raid outside the inventory, and a setting hides the in-raid HUD's three parts (the health figure, the stance figure, the quick slots) automatically, always shows them or never does, as the game's does. With `TARKOV UI` on, three panels of the game's battle screen (the object under `Common UI` whose name ends `BattleUIScreen Variant`, in the scene that holds the inventory screen, `level44`) are drawn in play, read and drawn as the inventory's panels are (`battle_view`; `probe battle` prints what their scripts name): the stance panel, the health panel and the quick bar. The app's plain meters are then not drawn; its own panel of the frame rate, the debug figures and the keys stands at the top right, under the quick bar (`F1`). Off, the plain meters as before. **Set beside a capture of the game** (2560 by 1440, all three always shown): the quick bar's place and size to a few pixels (its first picture 576 units in, its last ending at 1464), its keys' labels (`U` for the knife: the captured game's binding), the AKM's view lit white with its caption in black under it, the silhouette and the stance panel where theirs are. The fades and the panels' rules are compared with nothing.

- **The stance panel** (`BattleStancePanel`, the screen's lower left corner, 181 by 264). **The stance's figure**: eight (`_battleStances`, each a figure and its stance: `Stand0` to `Stand5` at 1, 0.83, 0.664, 0.498, 0.332 and 0.166, `Crouch` at 0, `Settle`, lying, at −0.332), the one shown nearest the stance it is told (`ChangeStanceImage`: −0.35 lying, else the stance's target as it is set, `MovementContext.SetPoseLevel`; the smoothed stance as lying begins or ends), stepped to a figure at a time, a step every two frames, from the last figure a ladder reached (`LadderSetActive`, `_currentStanceIndex`: a stance told midway begins again from there). **The stance's slider** (from −0.332 at the foot to 1, its marks the scene's) has its arrow at the figure's stance. **The speed's slider** (0 to the top speed, `MaxSpeed`) has its arrow at the speed told (`Player.RaiseChangeSpeedEvent`: sprinting the sprint's pace, else the clamped speed; and the clamped speed as a limit changes), and twenty notches the script makes (`BattleStanceNotch`, at a twentieth of the top each, the row sharing itself among them, `HorizontalLayoutGroup`) and its last at the top, each white where that speed can be set and white at 0.078 where not (`ChangeMaxSpeed`: had where `Math.Round(notch − StateSpeedLimit / MaxSpeed, 2)` is no more than the engine's epsilon). **A slider's arrow** is laid out as the engine's slider lays it (`Slider.UpdateVisuals`: the handle's anchors along the slider at the value's share, across it over the whole), not where the scene keeps it (the speed's handle stands at 0 in the scene, its value 0.575); the handle is turned in the scene (−90, −180) and its own picture switched off, and the arrow under it is drawn turned with it about its pivot (the drawing now turns what hangs from a turned object, and leaves out an image or a text switched off). **The legs' and the arms' stamina** (`_sprintFillImage`, `_handsFillImage`): each filled across by its pool's share; drawn in the legs' green and the arms' blue, either in the spent sprite under 15 (`Stamina.Current < 15`). **The noise** (`BattleUINoiseLevel`, three sprites): `MovementContext.CovertNoiseLevel`, 1 lying, 2 sprinting, else 0 where the covert movement is nearly whole (`CovertEfficiency` over 0.9: none with a leg damaged, else `InverseLerp(0.4c, 0.4c − 0.2, speed / top) × (1 − walk overweight)`, `c` the Covert Movement skill's speed, 1 at level 0, the app having no skills as a system), 2 off the ground faster than 0.8 of the top, else 1; set as any of the panel's events comes. The voice's panel is off; the bipod's draws nothing with nothing mounted.
- **The health panel** (`CharacterHealthPanel`, the upper left, 146 by 262): its ground (`battle_char_back`, white; at 0.2 with the colours green to red), and the seven parts (`BodyPartImage`), each coloured by its health's share `h` (`EHealthColorScheme.GetBodyPartColor`, then `Saturation(0.5)`): white to red (the game's default), red brought up to white by `h²` (`Color.Gamma`: (1, h², h²)); green to red, the hue `h² × 120` (`ColorFromHSV`); black destroyed; then every channel halved and the alpha whole (the scheme's alpha, 0.8 − 0.6h², is lost: `Saturation` makes a new colour). **Its effects' icons** in a grid to its right (`_effectsPanel`, a `GridLayoutGroup` of 24 by 22, down a column of eight first): the effects at work (`GetEffects`: started, or critical and left over, which only lethal intoxication is), one icon of a kind (`Counted` by its sprite) with its count where there are more than one and it is not of a kind that stacks (`IStackable`: tremor, of the app's), pain left out while a painkiller works (`EffectDescriptionFilter`, `PAIN_HIDDEN_BY`).
- **The quick bar** (`QuickAccessPanel`, an `InventoryScreenQuickAccessPanel` as the inventory's, read by the same reader: `QuickViews::load_battle`; its script lists its fifteen child input nodes before its views, where the inventory's lists none): at the screen's top, its middle 57.5 units right of the screen's, 10 down, as wide as its row asks (a `ContentSizeFitter`: 892 units). Two groups 14 apart: the grenade's, the sheath's, the holster's, the sling's and the back's views, each 74 wide (a weapon's view for its under-barrel weapon off: `BoundSlotView.ShowSubItem`, none of the app's has one), and the seven keys' views, 64 wide and 10 apart. Every view on (`ShowMainSlot`, `BoundItemView.Show`), with its key's words (`HotKey`), its thing's ground and picture turned and fitted as the inventory's quick-use views' (`QuickSlotItemView`), its caption the thing's short name (`ShowInfoPanel`). **The thing in the hands lights its view** (`RefreshSelection`, `QuickSlotView.SwitchVisualSelection`): its ground (`Background`, the view's dark ground) white and its caption black, every other view's ground black at 0.6 and its caption white, the captions in the normal style. **A malfunction** (`malfunctions.md`, "What the shooter is told"): made known at the shot, 150 ms on the bar is raised as by a change of the hands and each slot view's pistol with no known malfunction glows blue (its `InstallPlace`, 0.175 s legs up to 0.3 and back, 4.9 s, ended by a quick draw of the holster's weapon); a weapon with a known malfunction has its view's border and picture red and its `InstallPlace` (181, 0, 0, 92), in this bar and the inventory's; any other weapon's `InstallPlace` (0, 0, 0, 52). Not built: the grenade's view (the app has no grenades), the selectors of a healing's part and of a grenade and their arrows.
- **When they show** (`BattleUIScreen`, `BattleUIComponentAnimation`). Each fades to whole in half a second from where it stands (`DOFade`; DOTween's default ease, out quad: the recorded fire mode's panel fits it to the seventh decimal, below) and, raised a while (`Autohide`), stays three seconds (`VISIBILITY_DURATION`) and fades out as long. **Frame by frame** (`courses8`'s check, the recorder's `hud`, every frame): a panel raised by a key fades in on the key's frame and is stepped on it; the fade ends on the frame past half a second; the three seconds count from that frame (the game awaits the fade, then `Task.Delay(3000)`, a wall clock's wait); the fade out is made on the first frame the wait has ended by, after that frame's tweens have stepped, so its first step is the frame after. The app's panels go the same way: a step that ends hands over on its own frame, the rest of that frame's time dropped (`battle_view::Fader`); a panel made when it is shown (the lower right's) is made at its first step's alpha, so that step is drawn on its own frame (`sight_panels`). **What is heard after the frame's tweens** is first stepped on the next frame, its words set on its own: the zeroing (made in the player's late update: `Player.LateUpdate`, `StartFovCoroutine`, `PreCalibrate`, `CalculateLocalSightTarget`, `ShowAmmoCountZeroingPanel`; at the sights coming up, a zeroing key, a scope's mode), a variable scope's zoom reaching or leaving an end under the wheel (`ScopeZoomHandler.Update`, `OnSmoothScopeStateChanged`), and a speed limit's change (worked out in the movement's update, `ProcessSpeedLimits`, the only place `OnMaxSpeedChangedEvent` is raised); a shown panel then starts from where that frame's step left it. Heard with the key, and stepped on its frame: a scope's snap by its key (`ForceChangeScopeState`) and a change of sight or mode (`SightChanged`), the checks, the fire mode, T+B, and the arms' drain begun or ended by the aim's key (`IsAiming`: `Physical.Aim` adds or takes off the consumption, `HandsStamina.OnChanged`), while the aim's limit (`SetAimingSlowdown`) waits for the movement's update: so the aim's key raises the stance panel twice, before the frame's step and after it. The speed told raises the stance panel by itself (`RaiseChangeSpeedEvent`), which a limit's change does not call; the app raises it so only where no limit moved (`PROVISIONAL`: a limit's change alone not compared). **Compared with `courses10`'s `battle-panels`** (Autohide, the M4A1 with the Razor 1-6x): the recorder's `hud` alphas of the ammunition's, the optic's and the stance panel on all 5,631 frames, fitted by `battle_view::Fader` on the recorded frame times with the shows so ordered, within 7.7e-5 (the ammunition's), 1.6e-5 (the optic's, the stance's); each show stepped on its own frame instead, 0.033 off (the zeroing 0 on the aim's frame 153 and 0.0331 on 154; re-shown by the snap on 1389 mid fade-out, 0.1071 then 0.1402; the zoom's hide on 1837 first stepped on 1838; the stance panel 0.0331 on the aim's key's frame 1168 and 0.0650 on 1169, where one raise gives 0.0656). The app's run (`--dump-ui`, the take's keys, the same build of the M4A1) after the change: the zeroing first stepped the frame after the aim, PageUp and the snap; the zoom's hide a frame after the wheel; the stance panel raised twice at the aim's key and faded out where the later raise puts it.
