The fire mode by `B` under quick presses: the input model on the take's own frame times switches on the game's frame each time (`byhand`, 7 of 7); a run of the app may land a lone press's switch a frame late, its 36 frames coming to under 0.3 s where the game's came to 0.30006: its frame clock, not its rule. **The stance panel** is raised by a stamina pool's change (`Stamina.OnChanged`: a drain begun or ended, a cost taken off at once), the stance set (or lying begun or ended), the top speed or its limit changing, the speed told changing; held up while a sprint lasts, let fade three seconds after it (`PullUpBattleStancePanelBySprint`). **The health panel** by damage taken (`ApplyDamageEvent`), an effect begun that calls for it (`IEffectTriggersUIPanel`: chronic fatigue, encumbered and overencumbered, of the app's), and, not raised a while, an effect taken off. **The quick bar** by a change of the hands begun (`HandsChangingEvent`, `method_14`: not the first weapon, which the character holds as it is made, before the screen shows); and by a quick slot's key or the grenade's going down (`PressSlot4` to `PressSlot0`, `ThrowGrenade`), which the bar hears as an input node of its own before the hands do: shown then with no fade out, whatever the setting, `Never` too (`InventoryScreenQuickAccessPanel.TranslateCommand`, `SetVisibility(true)`), and up until the hands' change raises it a while or the screen comes up again; with nothing bound to the key it stays (compared with `courses10`'s `battle-stances-quickslots`, Autohide, `4` with nothing bound: the bar up on the key's frame and whole the 4.3 s to the inventory's opening, the app's the same by `--dump-ui` and `uitrace --beside`, every view on the game's pixels; `Never` and the grenade's key read from the code only). By the game's own settings (`Game.ini`: `QuickSlotsVisibility`, `StaminaVisibility`, `HealthVisibility`, each `Always`, `Autohide` or `Never`; `HealthColor`), as the install has them or as the options' HUD page keeps them (`settings.md`, "The GAME page", "The HUD page"): the captured game's were autohide, autohide, autohide and white to red, now always shown for all three. **Each time the battle screen comes up** (the run's start, the inventory shut) every panel starts out, then comes in when raised, or fades in at once where always shown (`UpdatePanelsVisibility(false)`); the settings are taken then. **The inventory opening shuts the battle screen** and every panel goes at once (`BattleUIScreen.Close`), and shutting it shows the battle screen again from the start: the game's screens stand in a list, showing one shuts the one before (`ScreenController.PrepareShow`, `CloseCurrentScreen`), and shutting it shows the one before afresh (`ShowScreenAsPrevious`, `DisplayScreen`, `Screen.Show`); read, and so seen in the game: the panels go at once as the inventory opens and fade back once it is shut. **The battle screen itself fades in as it comes up**, under each panel's own fade: `BattleUIScreen.Show` calls `UIScreen.ShowGameObject`, the same soft show as the inventory's (above, "The screen and its tabs": its whole group from 0, four times the frame's time a frame from the shutting key's frame, whole the frame after it passes 0.95), and a panel drawn under it is drawn at the product of the two. **Compared with** `courses10`'s `inventory-tabs` (Always; the inventory shut at 2670): the sprint bar's fill at 0.0011 on the key's frame (the screen's 0.0333 times the panel's 0.0331, out quad over half a second), 0.0104 after a 0.0174 s frame, 0.7235 on 2697 (0.9788 times 0.7393), and the panel's alone from 2698 (0.756), the screen whole; and with `inventory-open-close` (Autohide: nothing of it drawn but the `ActionPanel` pointer's group, 0.0337 on the key's frame, whole 0.25 s on). Built (`battle_view::follow_screen`, `screen_fade`; the battle screen's roots: the three panels of `battle_view`, `sight_panels`, `action_view`): the app's run with `--hud always`, shut at 10801: the stance panel's bar 0.0006 on the key's frame (0.0244 times 0.0242 after a 6.1 ms frame), 0.4322 at 10830 (0.727 times 0.5947), as the product on the app's own frame times. Hidden too once the character is dead. The weight's rise (`OverweightIncreased`) does not raise the health panel here (the app's weight changes only with the inventory open).

**When the interface is laid out** (the app's own rule: the optimisation's step 6). The engine's layout of every screen (Bevy's `ui_layout_system`) runs only on a frame where something it is laid out from has changed: a node's own layout, a text's or a picture's measure, the window's size or scale, the hierarchy (a node added, removed or moved), or what places a node (its turn, a scroll, an outline). On any other frame every node keeps last frame's size and place, which the layout would have given again. Nothing drawn changes; with the inventory open and still, a frame costs 0.45 ms less (4.4 to 3.95 ms, about 10%). The texts set on whole pixels after the layout (`texts_on_pixels`, above) stay set until the layout next runs, as they were drawn. `TRP_LAYOUT_EVERY_FRAME` lays out every frame, as before. **Compared** (the two ways, fixed steps and seeded rolls): twelve scenes captured identical pixel for pixel (the battle screen, the inventory's pages, an inspect window, the OPTIONS tab, the edit build screen, shots, the dummies) and seven screens' interface the same element for element; five scenes driven by hover, drag, the wheel, a shot, a change of weapon and a body's search the same in their dumps and last frames. Not tried: a change of the window's size or resolution with a screen open (the verification log). **A filled bar is written only when its edge moves a pixel** (the app's own rule, session 287): a fill (the stance panel's legs' and arms' stamina, the HEALTH panel's bars, the bar of a thing being used) is a clip as wide as its share, and the layout rounds a node's edges to whole physical pixels; its width is written only when the rounded width would differ from the one laid out, worked out from its last unrounded and rounded widths for every place within a pixel it could stand at (`panel_pieces::set_fill_width`). Otherwise the stamina moving laid the whole interface out on every frame of a sprint or an aim (0.17 ms of the profiled frame). Drawn the same. The app's plain meters (`hud`) are not written while they are hidden (with `TARKOV UI`), and are on the frame they show.

**When the screens are read** (the app's own rule: the battle screen's part at the start, the inventory's in the background). The game's screens are read from the install on a thread of their own as the run starts (`HealthLook::read`), in two parts with one reader of their pictures: first what the first frame may show (these panels, the quick bar in a raid, the stance's and the pointer's sprites, the effects' icons, the examine's panel, the version line) with the inventory's bar of tabs, which the screen is laid out by as the run begins, and their fonts: the start waits for that part alone; then the rest (the inventory's panels, its windows and menus, OVERALL, SKILLS, the edit build screen) and the install's words for its texts, merged in on the frame it comes, its pictures' places going on from the first part's and no font read twice. The warm-up waits for it, so that every screen is whole from the run's first frame and its pictures go up to the card under the curtain, as when all was read at the start. With `TARKOV UI` off the first part is empty and the rest is the health panel's and OVERALL's layouts. Measured: the start's wait 0 ms (171 to 473 ms before), every screen captured as before.

## Equipment slots

The character's equipment is one item (`InventoryEquipment`, template `55d7217a4bdc2d86028b456d`, "Default Inventory") with fifteen slots (`EquipmentSlot`, in the code's order): FirstPrimaryWeapon, SecondPrimaryWeapon, Holster, Scabbard, Backpack, SecuredContainer, TacticalVest, ArmorVest, Pockets, Eyewear, FaceCover, Headwear, Earpiece, Dogtag, ArmBand.

Each slot's filter is in the template (read from the install): the two primary slots take 25 kinds or single weapons, the holster 6, the scabbard knives, and each of the rest one class of item (vest, armour, backpack, pockets, secured container, headwear, face cover, visors, armband), the earpiece 12 named headsets and the dogtag 14 named tags.

Eight of them are drawn on the body (`AllVisualSlotNames`): Backpack, TacticalVest, ArmorVest, Eyewear, FaceCover, Headwear, Earpiece, ArmBand.

## What a slot accepts (`Slot.CheckConditions`)

A slot holds one item. It takes an item when all of these hold, asked in this order (report 11, read and built 2026-10-30):

1. the slot is empty and not locked (`Locked`, the first filter's `locked`: the pockets worn, an armour's built-in soft inserts and collars, a helmet's built-in plates; 477 slots of the install). A locked slot gives nothing up either (`RemoveItemInternal`), though a move asks the raid's own rules first (`MovePathCheck`: a thing kept on in a raid is refused as that);
2. in a raid, a plate's slot (`ArmorSlot`, its `_proto` the armour slot's) of armour worn, directly in a slot of the character's equipment, takes nothing (`CanAcceptRaid`: "You can't modify ballistic plates in equipped body armor. Take it off first."). Only the putting in is refused; the code asks nothing of a plate taken out;
3. the filters pass, each in turn (`ItemFilterExtension.CheckItem`): the filter's ids name the item's template, or a class the item belongs to, and its excluded ids name neither; and none of the excluded ids names anything the item holds, at any depth (a part with a part on it). A class matches by the game's code type (`ItemFilter.CheckItem`: `JsonTypes.TypeTable`, an item's type its parent class's); ours by the template's chain of parents. **Set against the install**: every slot has one filter, none has an excluded list, every class a filter names is one of the game's types, and the two ways agree for every slot but the hideout's sorting table (six night-vision devices its `BuildSlot` takes by the tree and not by type);
4. no item worn elsewhere blocks this slot: an item's template can name slots it blocks (`BlocksEarpiece`, `BlocksEyewear`, `BlocksHeadwear`, `BlocksFaceCover` and so on; `SlotBlockerComponent`), and while it is worn those slots take nothing;
5. **the conflicting items** (`CheckConflictingItems`), where the slot merges with its item (below): nothing of the whole the slot joins names anything of the item's among its conflicting items, by template, nor the other way round. The whole: from the slot's item up through the places that merge, never into the character's equipment (`GetRootItem`); then, where that item's own place merges, what holds it. The things compared: each and what merges with it, depth first (`GetMergedItemContent`). The refusal names the first thing there that something coming names, else the first thing coming that something there names: "Cannot install both {the one there} and {the one coming} at the same time", by their short names;
6. a thing for the special slots alone (`IsSpecialSlotOnly`: four of the install) goes only into a slot whose name has `SpecialSlot`;
7. the other way round of 4: the slots the new item would block are empty;
8. a weapon's malfunction allows it (firing spec, "A malfunction");
9. a weapon has all its vital parts (not in a slot named `BuildSlot`); the item is a single one, not a stack, and is not put inside itself.

**Which places merge** (`MergesWithChildren`): a slot by its own `_mergeSlotWithChildren`, else by its item's `MergesWithChildren`; a grid and a magazine's rounds by their item's; a chamber (`patron_in_weapon`) never. Every weapon and every part merges but seven parts (the PB's built-in silencer, six night-vision and thermal devices); armour, rigs and helmets do not, but their plates' and attachments' slots say they do (295 armour slots, 191 of rigs, 174 of helmets); of the character's own slots, the face cover's, the head's, the ears', the eyes' and the arm band's. So the conflicts are asked over a whole weapon with its parts, over a helmet with its attachments, and over the things worn on the head together (a helmet against a headset worn, both ways); a weapon's slot on the character, the armour's and the vest's ask nothing. **The install's lists**: 469 templates carry `ConflictingItems`, 6,295 names in all, most pairs named one way only (the check goes both ways); 5 name a class and 13 nothing, which the check by template passes over.

**In TRP** (`trp_sim::inventory`: `Item::slot_takes` the slot's own, 1, 3, 6 and the stack; `move_item`, `split_item` the rest, with `conflict_at`, `conflict_root`, `merged_content`, `conflict_between`; `Refusal::Locked`, `Conflicting`, `PlateWorn`): every move, split and drop of one thing on another asks them, and the slots lit while a thing is carried are the ones a drop would fill (`check_accept`), so a slot that would refuse it is not lit. The refusals are said in the install's own words (`inventory_view::why`). Before 0.158.0 a slot asked its filter alone.

Not read further: `CanTransferTo`, the unlootable items.

## How many of a kind in a raid (`RAID LIMITS`; read and built 2026-10-30)

The game limits how many of some kinds the character may carry (`GlobalConfiguration.RestrictionsInRaid`: a template, `MaxInRaid`, `MaxInLobby`; a `MaxInRaid` less than its `MaxInLobby` is raised to it). The raid's own inventory goes by `MaxInRaid` (`InventoryController.IsLimitedAtAddress`), the stash's out of a raid by `MaxInLobby` (`BackEndInventoryController`). A thing coming onto the character from elsewhere (a move, a split, a stack put on a stack: `ItemManipulator.CheckItemCountLimit`; a thing moved within what the character has is not counted) is refused where, for any kind among it and everything in it that has a limit, what comes is more than the limit less what the character holds of it already, stacks counted whole (`CheckOverLimit`): "Exceeded the limit of item {its name} in the inventory ({the limit} max)". Before a raid the menu refuses to start one over the limits (`CheckInRaidItemsRestrictions`). **The install's**: 215 kinds; most of the large backpacks one, some rigs two, 78 things none at all (the arena's crates among them), the rest 4 to 1,000,000.

**In TRP**: the options' `RAID LIMITS`, off as it starts (`trp_sim::inventory::raid_limits`, `Rules::limits`, `within_limits`; `Refusal::OverLimit`), `MaxInRaid` on every move, split and stack onto the character from a crate, the ground or a body. Not built: the check as a raid begins (the range has none), the lobby's limits.

## Grids

A container's room is one or more grids (`Grid`), each so many cells wide and high (`cellsH`, `cellsV` in the template), with a filter of its own: a list it takes and a list it refuses (a backpack takes any item and refuses secured containers and a list of cases). A vest has several small grids; the starting pockets are four grids of one cell and three special slots.

**An item's size in cells** (`CalculateCellSize`, `CompoundItem.CalculateExtraSize`; read again 2026-10-17) is its template's width and height, and for an item with parts, more: every part, and every part's parts, gives its own extra cells to the left, right, top and bottom, and of those the *largest* on each side is added, not the sum; a part whose extras are **forced** (`ExtraSizeForceAdd`: most silencers, some stock tubes) has its cells added beside that, all such summed. So a rifle is as long as its longest barrel and stock make it, and a forced silencer on the end one more. **Only a thing that merges with its parts looks into its places at all** (`MergesWithChildren`: true for a weapon and its parts, false for a backpack or armour), and then into every place but the chamber's (`patron_in_weapon`, which the game never merges); a place's own word (`_mergeSlotWithChildren`) changes nothing in the size (it says to merge where the thing would anyway). **A fold** (`FoldableComponent.Folded`): a folded part takes its reduction (`SizeReduceRight`) off its own right extra; a folded weapon, or a stock looked at alone, takes it off the right as a forced cell; and a folded thing does not look into its folded slot (`FoldedSlot`: the AKS-74U's stock goes out of its size). Read from the install so, the Standard edition's two starting rifles come out at 5 by 2 cells.

**Turned.** An item lies as it is or turned a quarter (`LocationInGrid`: column, row, and which way), which swaps its width and height.

**Placing by hand** (`AddInternal`): the item's rectangle has to lie inside the grid, cover no taken cell, and pass the grid's filter; a grid may also cap how many items it holds.

**Finding room** (`FindFreeSpace`): rows from the top, and in each row cells from the left; the first cell from which the item's rectangle is free takes it. The item is tried as it lies first and, if it fits nowhere and is not square, turned. (A stash that stretches adds rows; a character's containers do not stretch.) Built so since 0.195.6 with the grid's filter asked once and its taken cells marked once a search, the same places tried in the same order (`Item::room`; the same place as trying each against every thing lying there, by a test).

## Moving things (`ItemManipulator.Move`, `MovePathCheck`, `Add`; read 2026-10-11)

A move takes an item out of where it is and puts it at an address: a slot of some item, or a cell of one of its grids with a turn. It is tried in full before anything is changed, and the screen tries it the same way to show whether a place will take what the mouse holds. Refused, in this order:

1. The item is at that address already.
2. The address is the dogtag's slot: nothing is moved there by hand.
3. **In a raid:** a weapon part whose template says it is not to be changed there (`RaidModdable` false) is neither put into a slot nor taken out of one; and a slot marked as required (`_required`: a vital part's, such as a barrel's or a receiver's) is neither filled nor emptied.
4. **In a raid:** an item whose template names equipment slots it is not removed from (`CantRemoveFromSlotsDuringRaid`) stays in such a slot of the equipment. In the install the pockets (7 templates) and the secured containers (12) each name their own.
5. The destination has to take things from this owner (a container that cannot be put into during a raid, a trader's, another player's: not read further; nothing in the install's database is marked as closed to putting into).
6. **Backpacks in backpacks:** counting the backpacks the address lies in, the item itself if it is one, and the deepest run of backpacks inside it, the sum may not pass `MaxBackpackInserting` of the game's settings (7 in the install).
7. The item would go into itself.
8. The place's own rules: a slot's ("What a slot accepts") or a grid's ("Placing by hand").
9. **A weapon goes onto the character whole:** an item with slots that is put into a slot of something the character wears is refused while a vital part of it is missing.

Then it is taken out and put in. Not read further or not built: quest items and their own stash, pinned and locked items, limits on how many of a kind may be carried, what cannot be looted, a part that blocks folding, and the room a weapon in a grid needs when a part makes it larger (the game checks the weapon's new size against the grid before it lets the part on).

### Dropping one thing on another (`ItemController.ExecutePossibleAction`, `CompoundItem.Apply`)

Let go over another item, in this order until one works:

1. **Merge:** both stack (`StackMaxSize` over 1), are of one kind, and the other has room for the whole stack: the two become one.
2. **Put into** the other, if it holds things: into the first of its slots that takes the item (in a raid a part is not offered a required slot), a weapon's chambers last (`Weapon.AllSlots`: a round let go on a weapon that takes no rounds into a magazine of its own goes into its empty chamber, `firing.md`, "A round into the chamber from the inventory"), else into the first place its grids have ("Finding room"). A slot that refuses a whole stack is given one of it.
3. **Top up:** both stack and the other has some room: as much as fits goes over and the rest stays.

Let go over an empty slot or a cell, it is the move above; with a stack on a place that takes only part of it, as much as fits (not built). With Ctrl held only part of a stack is moved (not built). Swapping two things by dropping one on the other is written and switched off in the game (`UnityUtils.DisabledForNow`).

### With the mouse (`GridView`, `SlotView`, `DraggedItemView`)

A held item is its own picture (`DraggedItemView`, made as the drag begins, `ItemView.OnBeginDrag`): the thing's icon at its own size (63 units a cell and one, `ItemViewFactory.GetCellPixelSize`), white for a thing examined (black at 0.85 for one not: "Examining"), with nothing round it, its middle at the mouse (`SetInCenter`: its pivot in its middle; before 0.104.1 this spec had it hang by the point taken, which the code does not). Over a grid it would go to the cell its own top left corner is nearest: the corner's place in the grid over the cell's 63 units, rounded, and kept inside the grid (`GridView.CalculateItemLocation`, from the picture's corner, half its size from the mouse). **That corner is kept as the mouse last moved** (read 2026-11-02, built 0.183.0; `DraggedItemView.OnDrag`: the picture's lower left stored with the thing, on the frame the drag begins and on each frame the mouse moves while held; not on the frame it is let go, whose release the input module hears before the move): `R` turns the picture and puts it in the middle again but does not store the corner anew (`RotateItem`, `RotateByInput`, which asks the grid under it to light again at once), so until the mouse moves the grid takes the corner from before the turn with the size after it, its left over a cell and its bottom over a cell less its new height, rounded (a half to the even one, `Mathf.RoundToInt`). Compared with `courses10`'s `drag-and-drop`: frame 2686, the M4A1 turned back from 2 by 6 to 6 by 2 with the mouse still, lit at the backpack's (2, 4) 168 by 84 pixels, and (0, 2) 336 by 168 once the mouse moved a pixel at 2713; frame 2926, the bottle turned and let go unmoved, put at (0, 1) turned where its middle would put it at (0, 0). The app (`inventory_view::grid_location`, the corner in `Held`): its 5 by 2 M4A1 turned back at the same place lit (2, 3) 168 by 168 on the frame of `R` and (0, 2) 336 by 168 on the mouse's next move; the kit's own water bottle, moved as the take's, lit and put at (0, 1) turned, and back at (0, 0) by the take's sixth drag (`--dump-ui local\ui_dump_drag-and-drop_r_s185.jsonl`). `PROVISIONAL`: a move on the frame of `R` kept at the size before the turn (which of the event system and the held picture the engine updates first is not read; the take moves on no such frame). **The drag begins** once the mouse is 5 pixels or more from the press, in the screen's pixels (`PointerInputModule.ShouldStartDrag`: the squared distance at least the threshold's square; the threshold the game's one event system's, `EventSystem.m_DragThreshold` 5, read with `probe fields level210 "Application (Main Client)/EventSystem"`, the field after the first selected object's pointer and the navigation flag; the engine's default is 10): the take's eight drags each began on the first frame 5 pixels or more away (5.0 to 6.3; the frame before, 2 to 4.5), and the app's four of the weapon on the same (5.4, 6.1, 5.0, 6.3). The edit build screen's turn by a drag waits on the same threshold (`DragTrigger.onBeginDrag`). **A slot that refuses the thing says why at the mouse** (`SlotView.HighlightItemViewPosition`, not as a preview: `SimpleTooltip.ShowInventoryError`, the error's own words, `GetLocalizedDescription`, in `<color=red>`, at once, the simple tooltip's place, 10 units right of and above the mouse; closed as the mouse leaves the slot or the thing is let go, `DisableHighlightSilent`), but not for a thing already there or with nothing to do (`ItemAlreadyThereError`, `NoPossibleActionsError`, `ItemNotApplicable`, `CannotApplyItemError`), and over its own slot nothing (`DragItemContext.CanAccept` refuses with no error). **Each view shows a refusal's words by a list of its own** (read 2026-11-10, built 0.254.0). A slot (`SlotView.HighlightItemViewPosition`) says every error but a thing already there and nothing to be done. A grid, and a thing in a grid (`GridView.HighlightItemViewPosition`), is also silent for a place taken, a thing that does not fit and no room (`Grid.PlaceTakenByAnotherItemError`, `Grid.WontFitToGridError`, `NoFreeSpaceError`), which the red cells alone say; it does say its own filter's "Item is incompatible" (`Grid.ItemFiltersWontAllowError`). **Rounds over a magazine** (`Magazine.Apply`, asked in this order): "Wrong ammo type" where its cartridges do not take the round (`Magazine.ItemFiltersWontAllowError`, the key `Inventory Errors/Ammo is incompatible`), then "Magazine is full" (`Magazine.FullMagazineError`); in the simulation `rounds_onto_magazine`, `Refusal::WrongAmmo` and `Refusal::MagazineFull`. A load that would go through keeps the refused colour, since `GridView.GetHighlightColor` gives its failed colour to any result that is not a move, a merge, a split, a hand-over or a swap, and a load's result (`LoadMagOperationResult`) is none of them; read from the code, not recorded. **A quick move refused** (Ctrl and a click, `ItemUiContext.QuickFindAppropriatePlace` with its warnings shown) is a warning among the notices in the error's own words: "No space for this item" where what was looked in has no room (`NoFreeSpaceError`, the key `No free room for that item`), "No actions available" where there was nothing to look in (`NoPossibleActionsError`). A drop shows no notice. Seen in the app: rounds held over a full magazine, the cell red and "Magazine is full" at the mouse; a repair kit over a special slot, "Item is incompatible". Not built: a full magazine's count made known by the hover (`StrictCheckMagazine`), and `LoadInsertedMagazineError` for a magazine fitted to a weapon that is not reloaded from within. The words by the refusal (`inventory_view::refusal_tip`): a filter's "Item is incompatible" (`ItemFiltersWontAllowError`), "Slot is locked" (the key, which the install lacks, said as it is), "Slot is not empty", "Item cannot be stored within itself", "Cannot attach while in raid" (`NotRaidModdableError`, `CannotModifyVitalInRaidError`), "Cannot move item during the raid", "You must fix malfunctions first", "{0} is blocking this slot" (the blocker's name), "Conflicting slot {0}", and those the app already said in the game's words (two things conflicting, an armour's plates worn, the raid's limits, not examined); `PROVISIONAL`: none where the game's error has words the app's refusal does not keep (`DeepMoveError`'s limit, `MissingVitalPartsError`'s slots), `TooLargeStackError` (whose words are its debug text), `PlayerIsBusyError`, nor the app's own refusals. Compared: the M4A1 over the holster, "Item is incompatible" in red (1, 0, 0) at 703, 714.67, 175.93 by 20.53 pixels, the game's 175.95 by 20.53 at the same place (frames 740 and the app's 265), gone on the frame it is let go in both (752; the app's 281). `R` turns it a quarter while it is held, whatever its shape (`RotateByInput`: a square thing's picture turns too; the picture turned 270 degrees, `ItemViewFactory.VerticalRotation`). **What a drop would do shows under it** (`DraggedItemView.UpdateTargetUnderCursor`): over a grid, the cells it would take, from that corner and kept inside the grid, in a panel of the colour of the drop (`GridView.HighlightItemViewPosition`, `GetHighlightColor`: a move green 0.06, 0.38, 0.06; a merge, a split or a hand-over yellow 0.69, 0.66, 0; a swap blue 0, 0.16, 0.48; anything else red 0.68, 0, 0; each at 0.57); over a slot, the slot's borders tinted in the slot's own colours (`SlotView.HighlightItemViewPosition`: the engine's `Color.green` where it would take the thing, whatever the drop would do, merge or move; `Color.red` otherwise, `GetHighlightColor` of a refused move; read 2026-10-30, 0.148.0: before, the grid's colours stood for them). **Every empty slot that would take a thing carried is green**: as the drag begins, the game asks every view (`ItemUiContext.CheckCompatibleItems`), and a slot view whose slot is empty (`SlotView.CheckAcceptHandler`) tries the drop there (`DragItemContext.CanAccept`, the drop's own `ExecutePossibleAction`, with Ctrl held a part of a stack) and, if it would go, sets its borders green (`HighlightItemViewPosition` as a preview: a slot that would refuse it is left as it is). It asks again for a view made while the thing is held and as Ctrl, Shift or Alt goes down or up (`ItemUiContext.Update`), and puts the borders' colours back as the drag ends (`DisableHighlightSilent`). So a slot of another kind is not lit, nor one a thing worn blocks or that would block a slot in use, nor a weapon's part not to be changed in a raid, nor the slot the thing is in. This applies to every slot view: the equipment's, a container row's, a special slot, a part's slot in the inspect window. The colour lands on the view's empty border (`_emptyBorder`), which is `border_generic` everywhere, a line of one pixel (`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler`; its sprite 16 square, the line its outer pixel, the rest clear): one unit outside the view's rectangle for the equipment's, a container row's, the dogtag's and a special slot (127 about 125, 66 about 64), on its own edge for a part's slot (`ModSlotView`, whose full border is the same line), dark grey at 0.609 (the part slot's 0.18 grey) until it is tinted. The app draws the same line in the tint over it (`inventory_view::AcceptLine`), pixel for pixel the game's recoloured line (checked in a capture); in its own look, the slot's own border. A full equipment or container slot's full border is a shaded frame six units out (`slot_border_full`), for which the box's own line stands under the mouse (`PROVISIONAL`). Not built: the game lights a slot refused only because the character is busy with a thing (`PlayerIsBusyError`), for which the app's moves have no refusal. The thing's own view meanwhile keeps its place, its picture at 0.85 (unless it blinks: `ItemViewAnimation.SetDragState`, `UpdateMainImageAlpha`, the same for a thing not examined), its ground, border and words' panel hidden, and the panel of a thing not examined (`ItemView.InfoVisibility`, `UpdateInfoVisibility`: `ColorPanel`, `_border`, `_infoPanel`), all back as it is let go; only the view it was taken from (built 0.183.0: `inventory_view::dim_taken`, the ground `light_related`'s). Compared with `courses10`'s `drag-and-drop`: on the frame the drag begins the game's bottle drops to 0.85 and loses its colour panel, border, caption and value (2868); the app's, on its own start frame (210), the same, and the M4A1's in its slot its picture, caption, round, key and bars. A click on a thing with no key held does nothing but stop a magazine's work (`ItemView.OnClick`). Over a slot that holds something, it is dropped on that thing. The moves without dragging have a section of their own, next.

### The box under the mouse, and what goes with it (read 2026-10-14, night)

**The game's.** The box under the mouse shows its highlight in place of its background (`ItemView.OnPointerEnter`, `UpdateColor`): white at 77 of 255 (`DefaultSelectedColor`). A box's background is otherwise the thing's own colour from the database (`BackgroundColor`; the app draws one colour for every thing). While a thing is carried, every box that would take it is lit too (`GridItemView.CheckAcceptHandler`, `ShowAcceptHighlight`): green, or yellow where it would join a stack or go into a container, at 10 of 51. **Nothing in the game lights what goes with a thing under the mouse.**

**TRP's own, from S.T.A.L.K.E.R.**: when a weapon is highlighted, its ammunition, magazines and attachments are lit orange, and the other way round, as S.T.A.L.K.E.R. does. How that game does it, read from GAMMA's scripts, loose in its mods: when a cell is pointed at (`ui_inventory`'s `On_CC_Hover` sends `ActorMenu_on_item_focus_receive`), Anomaly's `itms_manager` lights, for a weapon, its rounds, the scopes, silencer and launcher it takes, its parts, and the repair kits and upgrade tools for it; GAMMA's magazines (`Mags Redux`, `magazines.script`) light, for a weapon, its magazines, for a magazine, its rounds, and for rounds, the magazines that take them. Every cell of a kind so named is lit, in every list shown, blue at 40 of 255 over the cell (`utils_ui`, `clr_list_hl`), and the cell pointed at white.

**Here**, two things go together when the one has a place for the other by the game's own filters, either way round:

- a slot of the one, or of a part on it and the parts on those, takes the other: a weapon's magazine slot, its chamber, a revolver's cylinder's chambers, a mount's rail; a slot takes what its filter lists and its excluded list does not;
- or a magazine's cartridges (`Cartridges`) take it, the magazine loose or on a weapon.

So a weapon lights the magazines it takes, its rounds and the parts that fit it or a part on it; a magazine, the weapons it fits and its rounds; rounds, the weapons and magazines that take them; a part, what it fits. A container's grids do not count (a backpack would go with everything), nor does a thing with another of its kind. Every box on the screen is lit: on the page, in the slots and in the windows. While a thing is carried, what goes with it is lit and its own box is as it was. The box pointed at has the game's own highlight. **A switch of the options** turns the orange off (`COMPATIBLE ITEMS HIGHLIGHT`; `settings.md`, "Features"): the box pointed at is lit then as the game lights it, and nothing else.

**The orange** is the game's own for a box (a thing with a repair's bonus: 209, 153, 45, `GridItemView`), laid over the box at 0.40, with a border of it: the share is the app's, chosen in a capture. Built in `trp_sim::inventory::go_together` and the app's `inventory_view::light_related`, worked out again only when what is pointed at changes or the screen is drawn afresh: at most 1.8 ms of an update (the debug build) while the pointer crossed ten things in the crate of rifles. **The game's own lighting of the boxes that would take a thing carried** (read 2026-11-02, built 0.183.0, `inventory_view::light_related`): as the drag begins every view is asked (`ItemUiContext.CheckCompatibleItems`, `GridItemView.CheckAcceptHandler`, with the game's highlight scope at `All`, `courses10`'s setting; `Merge` would light only stacks and containers, `None` nothing), but not the thing itself nor what is in it; where its container would take the drop onto it (`Container.CanAccept` with the view's thing as the target: the drop's own try, `ExecutePossibleAction`), its colour panel takes the engine's yellow (1, 0.92, 0.016) for a thing with grids or a stack and its green otherwise; where it would not, the engine's green only for a repair kit on what it can mend where repair is offered (`ItemView.CanInteract`; here, the workbench's REPAIR GEAR); each at 10/51 (`ShowAcceptHighlight`, `UpdateColor`), back to its own as the drag ends. So the backpack the bottle lies in is not lit while its first free place is the bottle's own (`ItemAlreadyThereError`), and a full magazine is not lit for rounds. Compared with `courses10`'s `drag-and-drop`: the vest's view (`Slot Panel/slot_layout/Background`, 876.92, 224, 166.67 square) yellow at 0.1961 for the bottle and the rounds, the backpack's too in its sixth and eighth drags, nothing for the M4A1; the app's vest the same box, colour and alpha on the drag's first frame, its backpack not in the fifth drag and lit in the sixth (`local\ui_dump_drag-and-drop_r_s185.jsonl`, frames 210 and 349). `PROVISIONAL`: tried once as the drag begins, not again as Ctrl, Shift or Alt goes down or up as the game's is. With `COMPATIBLE ITEMS HIGHLIGHT` on, the orange keeps its place over it. The empty slots that would take it are lit since 0.148.0 ("With the mouse"). **Compared with nothing**: the game has no such thing, and S.T.A.L.K.E.R.'s was read, not run.

**On the frame the page is drawn afresh** the app's new boxes do not yet know where the mouse is, so what was under the mouse stays lit for that frame (else every box the mouse lights went dark for a frame). **Not a thing that has moved since** (weapons and backpacks had flashed when put on with Alt): put on with Alt, its new box in its slot is not under the mouse, and lit for that frame it flashed white (seen: the Mk47 from the rifles' case on the back, its ground 91 on the first frame and 15 after; since, 15 on both). The game lights only the box under the pointer (`ItemView.OnPointerEnter`).

### A thing's name, and an armour's points: the tooltips (`GridItemView.ShowTooltip`, `ValueProxyPointerEnterHandler`, `SimpleTooltip`, `MultiLineTooltip`, `Tooltip`, `RectTransformExtensions.CorrectPositionResolution`; read and built 2026-11-02, compared with `courses10`'s `hover-tooltips`)

**The game's.** The mouse coming onto a thing's view in a grid or a slot (a `GridItemView`, one found: `IsSearched`) asks the simple tooltip for the thing's name after 0.6 s (`SimpleTooltip.Show(name, null, 0.6)`): it shows on the first frame at or past 0.6 s from the frame the mouse came on, whether the mouse still moves or not, and goes on the frame the mouse leaves the view (`OnPointerExit`: `HideTooltip`, which also ends a wait). Moving onto the view's own parts (its caption, value, marks, durability's bar) neither starts nor ends it. A drag begun before it shows ends the wait (the take, frames 3631 to 3659: none came). A thing in a quick slot (`QuickSlotItemView`) has none. Its words (`GetItemName`): "Unknown item" in the install's words for a thing not examined, else its full name in them (`Item.Name`), a weapon's followed by a space and its build's name (`ItemFactory.BriefItemName`): the player's saved build of it first (`BriefWeaponBuildName`, none here), else the first of the install's builds that renames (`ItemPreset.ChangeWeaponName`, or one of no weapon's stock) whose parts but its magazines and rounds the weapon all has (`BriefWeaponPresetName`, `ItemPreset.Children`), in the install's order (its file's, by id), the name cut to 28 letters and "..." past 30 (`ItemCachedName.BriefName`). The stock M4A1's own preset renames ("Carbine"), so a stock M4A1 is "Colt M4A1 5.56x45 assault rifle Carbine", and its caption "M4A1 Carbine" by the same rule (`GetItemName(address)` with its short name); the take's M4A1, built otherwise, "... T.O 2K24". The game's other words before the name, at once and in red: a weapon's known malfunction and its rounds that do not fit are built (`malfunctions.md`, "The inventory"); a part that cannot be taken out, a teammate's tag and a case not to be opened in a raid (`GetErrorText` and the rest) are not.

**The simple tooltip** (`Preloader UI/UIContext/SimpleTooltip`, its prefab read: `probe scenepanel level49 UIContext/SimpleTooltip`): a black ground (`Background`) round its words, 6 units in at the sides, 2 above and 5 below (its `VerticalLayoutGroup`), as wide as its words, at most 300 units (the root's `LayoutElement`): wider words are broken into lines 288 units wide and the ground is 300; its frame (`Border`, `border_generic` sliced in (0.322, 0.349, 0.353)) on the ground's edge and the frame's shadow (`Border Shadow`, `border_shadow` sliced, white), both kept a pixel of the picture to a pixel of the screen and the shadow 4 pixels out on every side (`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler`: its 4 units' offsets divided by the screen's scale); its words in Bender at 14 (18.67 pixels at 1440 lines) in (0.651, 0.690, 0.710). Its lower left 10 units right of and above the mouse (`_defaultOffset`), placed again on every frame (`Tooltip.Update`, on the same frame as the mouse), moved back onto the screen from the edge it crosses (the left's checked before the right's, the bottom's before the top's) and its top left put on a whole pixel (`CorrectPositionResolution`, `Math.Round`). It is placed by its size as the layout last left it, so on the frame its words change it lies where the last words' size puts it, the new size then growing from its lower left: the take's vest, 230.72 down on its first frame and 231 after (frames 214, 215). **New words while it shows** (`SimpleTooltip.SetText`): the game's tooltip is one object, and a view whose words change while it shows sets its label's text (an effect's icon: `EffectIcon.Co_UpdateTooltip`, once a second from the mouse's coming on, and `BuffsUpdatedHandler` as its buffs change); the layout then sizes the ground to the new words. Bender's figures are not alike wide (`probe tmptext` at 14 and 1.33 pixels a unit: "(11)" 19.67 pixels, "(88)" and "(00)" 29.01), so a stimulator's tooltip grows and shrinks by a few pixels as its seconds count down. The words' box (TextMeshPro): 20.53 pixels a line at 1440 lines, the second 21.28 below the first. In the take: one line 29.87 high, two 51.15; "Bottle of water (0.6L)" 201.64 wide at 13 right of and 43 above the mouse.

**An armour's points.** On an examined armour holder with plates (`IsValueProxyActive`: its `ArmorHolderComponent` has one), the mouse coming onto its value (`ValuePointerEventsProxy`, on the value's group, as large as the words) closes the simple tooltip and shows the tooltip of lines at once (`MultiLineInfo(item, false)`: "ARMOR POINTS", a break, each plate as the inspect window's class dropdown lists them); leaving the value closes it, and the name does not come back until the mouse comes onto the view anew (the take, frames 525 to 791). **The tooltip of lines** (`UIContext/MultiLineTooltip`): placed as the simple one but with no offset (its lower left at the mouse); 10 units in at the sides, 5 above and below, its rows 3 apart (its `VerticalLayoutGroup`), an empty header first (no height, its spacing after it), each row a line of words 20.53 high, or a break: `tooltip_separator_2` sliced across the whole width, as high as its one pixel's border at its sprite's 65.31 pixels a unit (2.04 pixels at 1440 lines); the same ground, frame and shadow. A row's words: an armour class's mark (`<sprite name="armor_classes_N">`) where the class changes, else `<space=1.5em>`, then a space and the words; the mark puts the words 26.52 pixels on at 18.67 (the take: "CHEST - 36 / 36" 171.72 wide, its words alone 145.21), 1.5 em 28. The take's vest: 257.28 by 221.68 for ten rows.

**Here** (`crate::tooltip`, built with `courses10`'s `hover-tooltips`):the simple tooltip and the tooltip of lines, each one object the screens ask for on each frame they want it (a frame none asks closes it; another key starts the wait again), drawn from their prefabs (`HealthLook::simple_tip_look`, `lines_tip_look`: ground, frame, shadow, padding, widest, the separator) with the words drawn and measured as the game draws them (`trp_data::tmp_text`), placed as above (`tooltip::corner`). The inventory's things (`inventory_view::item_tooltip`, both looks of the item views, the page and the windows, not the quick bar; none while a thing is carried or a menu is open), an armour holder's value (`ValueProxy`), the edit build screen's parts (`edit_build::tip`), the health's effects' icons (`health_view::show_effect_tooltip`) and the inspect window's dropdowns (`info_tips`: plates and a round's chances in the tooltip of lines, an armour's zones in the simple one, a line each, as `string.Join("\n")` makes them) all use them; the context menu's dimmed line still draws its own. **New words** (2026-11-10, `tip_draw::swap_simple`): words of the simple tooltip's shape (the same scale, the same armour class's mark at their start, drawn in the game's face: `tip_shape`) are taken in place, as the game's `SetText`: the new text written into the words' entity (`TmpText`) and, where their size differs, the ground, the words' box and the frame's pieces sized again, the pieces kept (`tip_layout::resize_frame`); other words make it afresh, as does any change to the tooltip of lines. Before, it was made afresh at every change, and its frame's pieces, new entities, were drawn a frame late, so the frame went missing for a frame each time a stimulator's seconds moved; a tooltip's frame still comes a frame after its ground and words on the frame it is first shown (the pieces are laid out, visible and stacked above the ground on that frame: the cause is not found). **Tested**: `new_words_of_the_same_shape_keep_the_tooltip` (taken in place: the same root, the new size; another shape: a new root, the old despawned; the same words: nothing), `a_tooltip_s_shape_is_its_scale_its_mark_and_the_game_s_face`. **Seen** (`trp.exe --inventory --feature tarkov_ui=on --resolution 1920x1080 --fixed-dt 50 --carry 5c0e530286f7747fa1419862 --move 100:690:505 --tap 110:rmb:2 --drag-at 130 --drag 747:539:747:539 --move 300:435:24 --tap 302:lmb:2 --move 400:430:244 --screenshot-frame 420 --screenshot-frames 26 --screenshot local/shots/stim_tip3_s282.png --screenshot-crop 380,40,620,230`: a Propital used from its menu, the HEALTH tab, the mouse on its buffs' icon): `stim_tip3_s282_420.png` says "(291)" on its four lines and `stim_tip3_s282_445.png`, 1.25 s later, "(290)"; of the 25 pairs of frames between, one differs (426 to 427), and only from the figures rightwards: the figures, and the ground's right edge with its frame about 3.5 pixels further out, the frame whole on that frame (`stim_tip3_zoom_s282_426.png`, `_427.png`; with the frame's pieces made anew at the new size, `stim_tip_zoom_s282_428.png` has no frame on that frame). Not set beside the game. `PROVISIONAL`: the app asks for the effect's words on every frame, so they change as the rounded seconds do, where the game's change once a second counted from the mouse's coming on. **Compared** (`trp.exe --start armor --tap 60:f:3 --feature compatible_items_highlight=off --resolution 2560x1440 --move ...`, `local\ui_dump_hover-tooltips_s185.jsonl`, beside the take): the delay (shown 0.601 and 0.605 s after the mouse came on, the frame before under 0.6), the bottle's box 201.62 by 29.87 at 13 right and 43 above the mouse (the game's 201.64 by 29.87, the same), its words 8 in and 2.67 down, the shadow 4 out, the M4A1's 13.33 right; "Unknown item" on an unexamined armour; the ANA M2's points 257.29 wide (as the take's ECLiPSE, whose widest row is the same "FRONT PLATE - 55 / 55"), each row 10.67 down, 24.53 apart, the break 2.04 high; the name gone on the value's frame and not back off it. **Differences left**: two lines' words 41.63 high, the game's 41.81 (the second line 21.10 below the first, the game's 21.28: the prefab's text has a line spacing of 1, a hundredth of its size more between lines, `m_lineSpacing`, read with `probe fields level49 SimpleTooltip/Background/Text` as the float after the character's and the word's spacings; `trp_data::tmp_text` lays out no line spacing, nor reads it, for any text); a class mark drawn where the take's picture has it (`anything-pic6.png`: its lit part, pixels brighter than 60 of 255, 21 by 20, 2.67 in from the row's left and 0.24 above its top; the app's 22 by 20 at the same place, the sprite drawn a unit a pixel, 0.15 units in and 1.9 above the row: `PROVISIONAL`, TextMeshPro's sprite asset not read); with the mouse moved by `--move` the app's hover lands a frame after the move (the scripted mouse is set after the frame's hover is worked out). No tooltip begins while a thing is carried (`PROVISIONAL`: the take's drag rested on no view for 0.6 s).

**A locked slot, and a plate in its armour** (read and built 2026-11-07: a locked thing, an aramid slot or an attachment that cannot be removed, shows its tooltip when hovered). In the window that shows a thing, a locked slot's part takes no mouse (`ModSlotView.ModLock`: its group's raycasts off), but the slot itself does, and 0.6 s after the mouse comes onto it shows its lock's words in the simple tooltip (`OnPointerEnter`: `Tooltip.Show(text, null, 0.6)`; `OnPointerExit` closes it). The words are the lock's reason, two line breaks and a name (`TooltipData.ToString`), as `ItemSpecificationPanel.GetModLockedState` sets them, the first that holds: a plate in armour worn, its slot not locked ("Equipped locked slot": "You can't modify ballistic plates in equipped body armor. Take it off first.", no name); a locked slot ("Locked slot": "Built-in armor components cannot be changed", no name); a part not to be changed in a raid, or a vital part's slot ("Raid lock" in red: "You can't modify that part during the raid.", and the part's whole name); a chamber not checked (above). Where the slot is an armour's with a plate or an insert in it (`TryGetArmorSlot`), the words are the plate's line instead, the reason after it: its class's mark (`<sprite name="armor_classes_N">`, none under class 1), a space, the slot's name in bold with a colon ("CHEST:"), the plate's whole name, " - ", its durability and its greatest each to a place ("40/40": `ArmorPlatesFormatter.FormatArmorPlateTooltip`). An unlocked plate's own view in its armour's slot says the same line with no reason (`GridItemView.ShowTooltip`: `ShowTooltipArmorPlate`). **Here** (`inventory_view::info_parts`, `InfoSlot::tip`, `info_lock_tips`; `Carried::plate_words`, `tooltip_words`): as read; the simple tooltip draws `<b>` bold (`health_view::rich_runs`) and a mark the words begin with as the tooltip of lines draws its marks, the first line put on by the mark's room (`MARK_EMS`; `TmpLayout::lead`). Looked at, not compared (`PROVISIONAL`: the simple tooltip's mark is in no take).

### A box that blinks (read 2026-10-14, night)

Things in the game blink: read from the code, whether that gives them time to load, and why TRP's did not.

**What the game does.** A box blinks while its thing is being added to a place or removed from one, examined, or having rounds loaded into it (`ItemView.Blinking`); it then takes no clicks (`IsInteractive`) and, being removed, shows no name. The blink is the picture fading to nothing over half a second and back over the next, round and round (`ItemViewAnimation.CG_StartBlinking`, two tweens with the tweening library's default easing, out and quadratic): the thing's own picture alone (`_mainImage`), each half from the picture's alpha as it is when its tween takes its first step, the next begun on the frame the last is done, its clock from there (`courses10`'s `dead-scav-examine`: 0 on 739 and 740, 0.0331 on 741; built, the app's examination's loader and bar no longer fading with it). Being added or removed lasts from the move's `Begin` to its `Succeed`: an inventory operation raises `Begin` as it is made and `Succeed` when what it waits on is done (`AbstractAsyncOperation`): the thing leaving its owner (`OutProcess`) and coming to the new one (`InProcess`). For anything but the character's own hands both are done at once, so in the single-player game the two come together and nothing is seen to blink (online, a server's confirmation lies between). **The hands are what a move waits on**: a weapon put into the slot the hands last drew from is taken in hand for it (`Player.TrySetInHands`, `TryProceed`), and the move succeeds when the weapon has appeared there: what was in hand is put away (`DropCurrentController`), the weapon's controller is made, and its draw plays to its `IdleStart` mark (`SpawnController`, `SpawnOperation.WeaponAppeared`); a weapon in hand taken out of its slot is put away first, and the move succeeds when the next thing is in hand (`TryRemoveFromHands`, `SetControllerInsteadRemovedOne`). **It is not a model loading**: the single-player game loads every loot item's model before the raid, behind its loading screen (`BaseLocalGame`, "Loading loot...").

**Here** the boxes blink as the game's do for the hands: a weapon put into the slot the hands last drew from blinks until it has appeared in them (`FirearmEvent::Appeared`, the draw's last mark), and the weapon in hand taken out of its slot blinks until it is put away and the next is in hand (at once for the empty hands, whose own coming up is not waited for); while blinking it takes no clicks and lights nothing (`inventory_view::Blinking`, `blink`). In the app the hands' taking a weapon also waits for its rig to be made when it has none yet ("Empty hands", *In the app*), so the box blinks through that as well. **The place it left**: the game's thing shows there as well, blinking with the new, until the move succeeds (its view is not taken away before; `courses10`'s `drag-and-drop`: the M4A1 in hand put on the back, both views blinking from 1082 and the old gone at 1176, 0.868 s, as the hands came empty at 1170; put back on the sling, both from 1627 and the old gone at 1732, 0.924 s, as the hands came to rest). Here a weapon moved out of a worn slot by a drop that waits on the hands is drawn in the slot it left as well, the slot drawn full, until it stops blinking (`Carried::leaving`, `keep_leaving`): the app's run, both views blinking together from the drop, the old gone 0.824 s after it (the hands' put-away, the app's own) and 0.940 s after the second (the draw to its last mark). **Not built**: the place left in a grid or by a move that is not the hands' (none waits in the single-player game); the blink of a magazine filled round by round, which the app has not (a thing examined blinks since 0.178.0: "Examining").

**A thing in use** (medicine, food or drink; read 2026-10-31, built 0.162.0): the health raises a drain on it as its work is added and as it ends (`ActiveHealthController.MedEffect.Added`, `Residue`: `DrainItemEventArgs`, Begin and Succeed). Meanwhile its view shows its drain loader (`ItemView._drainLoader`: the prefab's `Loader`, 44 units, in the view's middle at its own size; the loader of a picture still to come is the other, `Small Loader`, 22: `probe itemviews loaders`, the script's fields in their order), takes no clicks (`IsInteractive`) and writes its figures every frame, so what is left counts down as the health takes it (`ItemView.Update`). It does not blink in the game (a recording of 2026-10-31). **Here** (`Carried::drained`, from the health's item at work): the loader turned as the app's loaders are, the box taking no clicks, the count as each step leaves it; and, TRP's own, its picture and ground blinking as a worked magazine's. Seen in captures (`local\drain_strip_s162.png` to `strip3`): no loader while the hands take the bottle, then the loader, the bottle and its ground fading in and out, 60/60 to 20/60 by frame 1000. **Compared with nothing**: read from the code, seen in captures.

## Clicks, keys and windows (read 2026-10-12, afternoon)

**Set beside the game on 2026-10-12** (sixteen recordings made by hand: "Compared with the running game", "The clicks, the keys and the moves"): all sixteen recorded moves are the model's own.

Read from `ItemView.OnClick`, `ItemUiContext`, `ItemContextInteractionsSwitcher`, `ItemManipulator.QuickFindAppropriatePlace`, `InventoryExtension.FindSlotToPickUp`, `InventoryEquipmentExtension`, `ItemController.ExecutePossibleAction`, `GridView.AcceptOnce`, `SplitDialog`, `StepSlider`, `GridWindow`, `FastAccess`, `BindResult`, `InventoryController.IsAtBindablePlace`, `PlayerInputTranslator`, `Player.SetSlotItem` and `Process`, `InputManager`; and from the install: its key bindings (`SPT_Runtime\user\sptSettings\Control.ini`; `probe keys <part of a name>`), two of its game settings (`Game.ini`) and the screen's quick-use panel (`probe scenes zzz` prints it).

### A click on an item

A click is the button pressed and let go on the same thing; the engine counts a second one on the same thing within 0.3 s as a double click (its own input module, in its published source: the count goes up while each press follows the last by less than that, and an even count is a double click). By the left button:

| Held | What the game does |
|---|---|
| nothing, a double click | food, drink or medicine is used, if the setting `ItemQuickUseMode` allows it (the install has `InRaidAndInLobby`): "Use", else "Use All", and never a look at it (built 0.37.0: `health.md`, "Built: the double click"); a container is opened in a window of its own; anything else is inspected in a window |
| Ctrl | the thing is sent to the first place found for it on the other side ("Sent to the other side", below), the tooltip shut, and the thing's group's `pickup` played wherever it went (the menu's lines that send a thing so play the move's own sound, `RunWithSound`); nothing if no place is found |
| Shift | the thing is sent to the sorting table (a raid has none) |
| Alt | the thing is put on (the menu's "Equip"; "Put on", below) |

With Ctrl or Shift held a double click is not taken as one. The right button opens the menu; the middle one examines, else folds or unfolds, switches on or off, else checks a magazine (built 0.178.0: "Examining").

**What counts as a container** (`Item.IsContainer`): what is searched (`SearchableItem`: backpacks, vests, pockets, secured containers), a plain container, one that locks, a stationary one.

### The menu of a right click (`ItemView.OnClick`, `ItemUiContext.ShowContextMenu`, `SimpleContextMenu`, `InteractionButtonsContainer`, `ContextMenuButton`; read 2026-10-16, late at night)

**What opens and shuts it.** The right button on a thing opens the menu where the mouse is (`ShowContextMenu`; not while the player is searching a container: "Can't open context menu while searching"), moved in if it would leave the screen and its top left then put on a whole pixel (`CorrectPosition`: `RectTransformExtensions.CorrectPositionResolution`, the left edge checked before the right, the bottom before the top, then `Math.Round`). It shows on the frame the button is let go. **Any of the mouse's buttons let go shuts it** (`SimpleContextMenu.Update`), after a line pressed has done its work (each line shuts the menu as it is clicked, `autoClose`). Nothing covers the screen behind it: a press elsewhere is that place's. **But the menu and its ground's shadow take the mouse** (the shadow, 23 units out of the ground, is a target of the pointer): the thing under the menu is no longer under the mouse, its view back in its own colour the frame after the menu shows, and nothing under the menu or its shadow lights or is pressed; the mouse between two lines is on the shadow, and no line is lit (`courses10`'s `inspect-window`, frames 205, 1723). **Escape shuts it**, its menu of lines with it, and nothing else, with no sound, unless a window is open ("Escape", below).

**Its lines** (`InventoryItemContextInteractions`, the inventory and a container searched in a raid), **in this order**, each shown only if the thing's class has it (`Item.ItemInteractionButtons`) and the switcher says so for now (`ItemContextInteractionsSwitcher.IsActive`); a thing not examined shows only Inspect, Examine and Discard. In a raid (`Gameplay`), as read:

| Line (caption) | Whose (class) | Shown in a raid when |
|---|---|---|
| Inspect (INSPECT) | every thing | always |
| View map | a map | always |
| Top up | a stack | never in a raid |
| Reload, Load, Unload | a weapon | Reload always; Load with no magazine in it; Unload with one, unless its magazine is internal |
| Equip, Unequip | weapons, knives, armour and other equipment, backpacks and vests | Equip when not worn, Unequip when worn |
| Modding, Edit build | weapons, armour, headwear | never in a raid |
| Overlook | a quest item | always |
| Use (USE) | medicine; food and drink | medicine always but a medkit with no points; food and drink with a whole above 1 |
| Use all (USE ALL) | food and drink | a whole of 1 or more |
| Heal all (HEAL ALL) | a medkit | **never in a raid** (`HealAvailable` wants not `Gameplay`) |
| Install, Uninstall | a magazine | Uninstall in a weapon, Install anywhere else |
| Repair, Insure | | never in a raid |
| Open (OPEN) | a container: backpacks, vests, a locked container | where it lies somewhere |
| Unpack | | never in a raid |
| Unload ammo (UNLOAD AMMO) | a magazine, an ammo box, a weapon with an internal magazine | the weapon's never in a raid; the others always |
| Load ammo, Apply mag preset | | never in a raid |
| Examine | every thing | not yet examined |
| Check magazine (CHECK MAGAZINE) | a magazine | something in the hands, the magazine not checked, not the one in the weapon in hand, and rounds in it |
| Turn on, Turn off (FLIP DOWN, FLIP UP) | a thing with a switch in it | by the switch |
| Fold, Unfold | a thing with a folding part | by the part |
| Disassemble | a weapon | the player's own |
| Add offer, the market's searches | | never in a raid |
| Tag, Reset tag | a magazine, a locked container | Tag always; Reset tag when tagged |
| Watch profile | a dog tag | its owner an offline inventory (`OfflineInventoryController`) |
| Add to wishlist (ADD TO WISHLIST) | every thing | not a quest item, money, a special slot's thing or a secured container |
| Pin, Lock, Unpin, Unlock | | never in a raid |
| Discard (DISCARD) | every thing | in a view that may discard |

**A line that cannot be done now is dimmed** (`SetButtonInteraction`, `SetUnlockStatus`: the line at 0.3 of its opacity, no clicks) and its reason shown in the game's simple tooltip while the mouse is on it, at once and by the mouse as any simple tooltip ("A thing's name, and an armour's points: the tooltips"), gone as the mouse leaves the line (`SimpleContextMenuButton.OnPointerEnter`, `OnPointerExit`: `SimpleTooltip.Show` with no delay); a dimmed line still lights under the mouse, at its 0.3, which is the button's own group (`SimpleContextMenuButton._canvasGroup`: in Discard's template the button below the divider, which stays whole: `courses10`'s `inspect-window`, frame 2163). Its reasons (`IsInteractive`: for Use and Use all, health's `HasPartsToApply` and its error; for Reload, Load and Unload "You can't do this to weapon with internal magazine", busy hands, a known malfunction; for Fold "You can't fold this item"; for Disassemble "You can't disassemble equipped item"; for Unload ammo from a magazine in a weapon "You can't unload ammo from an installed magazine", from an empty one "You can't unload from this item"; for anything that changes the thing, whatever stops the thing being changed).

**Lines with lines of their own** (an arrow at the right, `HasSubInteractions`; opened as the mouse comes onto the line): Add to wishlist (the wish list's groups), Load ammo, Apply mag preset.

**A line** (`ContextMenuButton`, the preloader scene's `Context Menu Area/ItemContextMenu`): 18 high, the menu's lines 2 apart inside a padding of 5 at the left and the top; from its left 12, an icon's place of 18 by 18 (`IconPlaceholder`) whose icon is 16 by 16 in a plain line and 28 wide by 16 high in Discard's and the wish list's (the line's sprite, the Resource `Characteristics/Icons/<line>`, swapped in and nothing more: `ContextMenuButton.Show`; kept to its own shape within that box, `preserveAspect`), 13 on its caption (the line's name in the install's words, in capitals; Bender at 11, white), 13 on the arrow where it has one, and 5 at the right. Every line is as wide as the widest. Its plate is the sliced sprite `subbutton` tinted by its colour, and the mouse on it tints it with another (`_defaultBackgroundColor`, `_selectedBackgroundColor`): (0.169, 0.176, 0.180) and (0.204, 0, 0) for an ordinary line; Discard's own template (`Dispose Template`: a divider, `tooltip_separator`, 2 high and 4 above it) (0.282, 0.051, 0.051) and (0.204, 0, 0); Add to wishlist's (0.067, 0.200, 0.259) and (0.129, 0.310, 0.392). Behind the lines, black at 0.79 a pixel out all round, and a shadow (`popup_shadow`, sliced, 24 out, white at 0.5).

**What each line does, of those a raid shows**: Inspect opens the window that shows the thing; Equip puts it on (as Alt and a click); Unequip takes it off to the first place found for it (`Uninstall`: `QuickFindAppropriatePlace`, as Ctrl and a click); Use and Use all as a double click ("Built: the double click", `health.md`); Open opens its window; Discard puts it down (as the discard key); Check magazine checks it ("Checking a magazine", below); Fold and Unfold turn the fold ("A fold from the menu", below); Disassemble takes the weapon apart ("Disassemble", below); Tag opens the window that edits its tag and Reset tag takes the tag off ("A tag", below); Add to wishlist opens its own lines ("The wish list", below).

**Built** (2026-10-16, late at night, 0.38.0; `trp_sim::item_menu`, `item_menu_view`, `health_view::menu_layout`): the lines, their order, which a raid shows and which are dimmed with their reasons, by the rules above (`trp_sim::item_menu::lines`, five tests). A thing not known shows Inspect, Examine and Discard alone ("Examining"); Watch profile never shows (a switch's state is kept since 0.46.0, so Turn off shows where a switch is on; a fold's since 0.47.0, so Unfold where a thing is folded). The menu opens on the right button let go on a thing and shuts on any button let go, a line let go on doing its work first. With `TARKOV UI` it is the game's own `ItemContextMenu`: a copy of the line's template for each line (Discard's and the wish list's own) laid out by the game's layout, its icons from the install, its captions in the install's words and in Bender, the line under the mouse in its selected colour, a dimmed line at 0.3 with its reason in the game's simple tooltip by the mouse (`crate::tooltip`; until 2026-11-02 beside the line, the app's choice of place). Without it, the app's own look of the same lines. **Compared with `courses10`'s `inspect-window`** (2026-11-02: the M4A1 on the sling, its menu twice, its wish list's lines, a dimmed line's reason, Escape; the app's run `local\ui_dump_inspect-window_fixed_s185.jsonl` beside it by `uitrace --beside`): the menu's ground, shadow, lines, captions, icons, divider and arrow on the game's pixels from the same click; every line's colour, at rest and lit, a dimmed line lit at 0.3, the wish list's line kept lit while its lines are open; the menu shown on the frame its button is let go (a frame late in the app until then: drawn before the screen's systems had opened it); the thing under it unlit the frame after (it stayed lit until then: the app's menu took no mouse); the reason's tooltip with its words at the game's place from the mouse (434,816.67 with the mouse at 413,857; until then beside the line, 169 pixels right and 43 down); Escape shutting the menus alone on its frame (the app shut the screen until then); no sound in either. Not compared: the water's and the rounds' menus (the app's character carries neither where the game's did); a press dragged off a menu line. **Seen in captures** (`local\shots\menu_*_s86.png`, 1080 lines): an AI-2 in the rig (INSPECT, USE, ADD TO WISHLIST with its arrow, DISCARD under its divider); USE lit under the mouse and done (the arm 30 to 59, the kit 100 to 70); the AKM on the sling (INSPECT, RELOAD, UNLOAD, UNEQUIP, DISASSEMBLE dimmed with "You can't disassemble an equipped item", ADD TO WISHLIST, DISCARD), in both looks. **Not compared with the game** until `courses10` (above). **Seen in captures since 0.47.0** (`local\shots\menu_2560_s89.png` and the menus of `fold_crate_s89.png`, `check_menu_s89.png`; the menu had been drawn stretched): each line's icon drawn in its icon's own box and kept to its sprite's shape, as the game's. Until then the app drew the sprite swapped in at its own size and then pressed it to the shape of the template's sprite, which made INSPECT's, TAG's and the wish list's icons wide or flat. **Not built**: nothing of the lines a raid shows since 0.51.0, when the wish list was built ("The wish list", below; Turn on and Turn off are built since 0.46.0: "A switch on the head"; Check magazine, Fold and Unfold since 0.47.0: "Checking a magazine", "A fold from the menu"; Disassemble since 0.47.3; Tag and Reset tag since 0.49.0: "A tag"; Open shows on a simple container, a case, since 0.49.0 too, as the game's), and Unload ammo on a box of rounds (on a magazine it is built since 0.44.0: "Loading and unloading a magazine"; Reload, Load, Unload, Install and Uninstall on a weapon's magazine since 0.45.0: `firing.md`, "A magazine taken out or put in from the inventory"); the menu's sounds; searching (the game's menu will not open while a container is searched).

### Loading and unloading a magazine (`PlayerInventoryController.LoadMagazine`, `UnloadMagazine`, `LoadMagazineProcess`, `UnloadMagazineProcess`, `GridView.AcceptItem`, `ItemUiContext.UnloadAmmo`; read 2026-10-17)

**How each begins.** In a raid a magazine is **loaded by dropping rounds on it** (`GridView.AcceptItem`: a stack of rounds let go on a magazine loads as many as the magazine has room for, up to the stack, `min(MaxCount − Count, stack)`); the menu's Load ammo is never offered in a raid. It is **unloaded by the menu's Unload ammo** (`ItemUiContext.UnloadAmmo`): a magazine's own, or a weapon's line for the magazine in it (the weapon's is never offered in a raid). A box of rounds is emptied at once (`UnloadAmmoInstantly`), not round by round. Either process first ends any process running (`StopProcesses`), and waits for it to be gone.

**How long a round takes.** Each round waits `BaseLoadTime` (0.85 s in the install's globals) to go in, or `BaseUnloadTime` (0.3 s) to come out, times `(100 − the skill's buff + the magazine's LoadUnloadModifier) / 100`. The buff is Mag Drills' load speed or unload speed, 0.6 for each level of the skill (`SkillManager`: `MagDrillsLoadSpeed`, `MagDrillsUnloadSpeed`, `PerLevel(0.6)`). The wait is the engine's own timer of whole milliseconds, rounded up (`Task.Delay(CeilToInt(time × 1000))`), and the round moves on the first frame after it ends. At the skill's elite level each round after the first is quicker by `LoadTimeSpeedProgress` per cent of the first's (1 in the install), to no less than 40 per cent of it.

**One round at a time.** Loading: the wait, then one round from the stack onto the top of the magazine's rounds (onto the top stack where it is the same round, else a stack of its own), the stack going when it is empty; so many times. The next wait starts as the round is put in, so the rounds come one wait apart. Unloading: the top round of the magazine is found a place first (`QuickFindAppropriatePlace`, the order `UnloadAmmo`: the character's equipment, its containers in the order for rounds, the vest first; onto a stack of the same round with room for one, else the first free cell), then the wait, then it moves there; until the magazine is empty, or a round finds no place, which ends it. Each round plays the interface's load or unload sound (`GUISounds.PlayUILoadSound`, `PlayUIUnloadSound`: one of the `UISoundsWrapper`'s `_loadSounds` or `_unloadSounds`, chosen by `Random.Range(0, count − 1)`, which never takes the last of the list).

**What stops it** (it ends where it is, what has moved stays moved): the inventory closed (`InventoryScreen.Close`), its panel shown again (`ItemsPanel.Show`, a tab changed), a left click on the magazine being worked (`ItemView`), a medical item starting its work (`ActiveHealthController`'s effect `Started`), another load or unload, and the magazine or the rounds thrown out with what holds them (`ThrowItem`: `TryStopLoadProcess`).

**What is shown** (`ItemView.SetLoadMagazineStatus`, `ItemViewAnimation.StartLoading`): the magazine's view blinks, its picture and its ground, while it is worked, and the game's load component counts the rounds with a button to stop.

**The load component** (read 2026-10-17, night: `ItemViewLoadAmmoComponent`, `ProgressSpinner`, `ItemViewAnimation.SetLoadingStatus`, `StartLoading`, `OnPointerEnter`; the Resource `prefabs/ugui/layouts/itemviewcomponents/itemviewloadammocomponent`): made over the view as the work begins (from its template, `_loadAmmoComponentTemplate`) and destroyed as it stops, its pieces centred on the view: the **spinner** (`ProgressSpinner`, 53 units across: its back `progress_spinner_back`, and its fill `progress_spinner_fill`, a picture filled radially from the bottom, clockwise, `Radial360`) filled by the work's share done; within it the **loader** (`spinner_big`, 26.4 units) turning a whole turn clockwise in each round's time, shown only where more than one round is to go; and the **stop cross** (`close_window_x`), off. With the mouse on the view the spinner and the loader are red and the cross on (`SetStopButtonStatus`), white again as it leaves. Its figures are the work's as it began (`LoadMagazineEventArgs`: the first round's time and the rounds to load; `UnloadMagazineEventArgs`: the rounds out so far, the rounds to go, the time; a check, `SetInventoryCheckMagazineStatus`: one "round" of the check's whole time), and its clock its own from then: the share is `(t + done × round) / ((to go + done) × round)` of the time `t` since it began, and the loader's turn `t` (with what was done) over a round's time, its fraction (the elite skill's quicker rounds are not shown). **The ground blinks** with the picture while the component is up (`StartBackgroundBlinking`): from its colour to an alpha of 0.6 and back, half a second each way with the tween's default easing (out, quadratic); while it runs it is what the ground shows, under the mouse too; stopped, the ground is its colour.

**Medicine and food** show the use's progress only by the game's `UsingPanel` (the game scene's: "USING", the item's name, and a bar filling over the effect's working time), and only where the hard settings' `MED_EFFECT_USING_PANEL` is on: it is off in this install (`probe hardsettings`). Nothing but the hands shows a use's progress, so nothing is drawn here for it.

**Built** (2026-10-17, night, 0.53.0; `screen::load_ammo_component`, `radial_fill` (a UI material: a sprite's cut filled radially from the bottom, clockwise, in the screens' gamma space), the app's `MagazineWork::shown`, `Shown::shares`, `turn_spinners`, the ground's blink in `blink`): with `TARKOV UI`, the game's load component over a magazine loaded, unloaded or checked, its pieces where the prefab lays them over the view, the spinner and the loader by the shares above, red with the stop cross under the mouse; the ground's blink; without `TARKOV UI` a bar along the magazine's foot by the same share. **Tested**: the shares (`the_spinner_fills_by_the_work_and_the_loader_turns_by_the_round`). **Seen in the app** (`local\shots\spin_unload_zoom_s91.png`, `spin_unload2_zoom_s91.png`, `spin_hover_zoom_s91.png`, `spin_app_zoom_s91.png`): the AK55 unloading, the fill half round from the bottom by the left, the loader turning inside it, red with the cross under the mouse; the app's bar. **Not compared with the game** (no capture of it). Its clock is the frame's time from the work's first frame (the game's is the wall clock from `Begin`): the same but for a frame.

**Recorded** (`courses2`, `3-menu-magazine`, the AKM's 30-round magazine, modifier 0; the character's Mag Drills at level 40 in the SPT profile, a buff of 24): unloading, the process begun at 8.0368 s, the first round out at 8.2856 (0.249 s, the first wait and a frame to begin), then a round every 0.2334 s (28 frames at 120 a second) to the thirtieth at 15.0756, all of them onto one new stack in the vest (the 60-round stack beside it full); loading those 30 back, begun at 20.9751, the first in at 21.6356 (0.660 s), then a round every 0.650 s (78 frames) to the last at 40.4948. **By the rule**: an unload's wait 0.3 × 0.76 = 0.228 s, which ends within the 28th frame; a load's 0.85 × 0.76 = 0.646 s, within the 78th. Both are what was recorded, to the frame. The first round came a frame or two after its wait (the round is put in by an operation the game completes on a later frame, while the next wait has begun), each later one a wait apart.

**Built** (2026-10-17, 0.44.0; `trp_sim::magazine_process`, `inventory::load_round`, `unload_round`, `takes_round`, `rounds_to_load`; the app's `inventory_view::work_magazine`): rounds let go on a magazine load it, as many as it has room for (a full one, or a round its filter does not take, refused with the reason); the menu's UNLOAD AMMO on a magazine unloads it; a round each wait, its sound one of the game's own seven of each list (the last never, as the game's choice has it); the magazine blinks while worked; stopped by the inventory shut, a tab changed, a medical item's work begun, a plain click on the magazine, or another load or unload. The base times from the install's globals, the magazine's modifier from its item. **Tested** (`magazine_process`'s tests): with the recorded character's level the rule gives 28 and 78 frames a round at 120 frames a second, as recorded. **Seen in captures** (`local\shots\munload_*_s88.png`, `mload_*_s88.png`): the AKM's 30-round magazine unloaded round by round onto the backpack's stack of the same round, which the order takes before a free cell, and a drop of that stack mid-unload loading the five rounds it had room for, 0.850 s a round. `PROVISIONAL`: Mag Drills untrained (a buff of 0: no skills yet), so 0.3 and 0.85 s a round; the magazine itself, which the unload's order looks in first, not looked in.

**Not built**: a box of rounds emptied at once (its Unload ammo says so); a weapon's magazine loaded through the weapon (a drop on a weapon with a tube is the hands' reload); what a process does if its magazine or rounds are thrown out.

### Checking a magazine (`ItemUiContext.CheckMagazine`, `PlayerInventoryController.InventoryCheckMagazine`, `CheckMagazineProcess`, `StrictCheckMagazine`, `CheckMagazineAmmoDepend`; read 2026-10-17)

**What the ammunition's panel says** (read 2026-10-24, 0.113.0: `Idling.CheckAmmo`, `Player.OnShowAmmoDetails`, `BattleUIScreen.ShowAmmoDetails`, `AmmoCountPanel.GetAmmoCountByLevel`, `GetAmmoCountByLevelForFoldingMechanismWeapon`; built in `trp_sim::inventory::ammo_words` and `sight_panels`). A look at the weapon's magazine in the hands (the check key, `CheckAmmo`) names its count on the battle screen's ammunition panel at the lower right as it begins, before its animation: at rest, with a magazine, in first person (with a feed malfunction the key examines it instead; a look into the chamber names nothing). **The words**: one short of full or more "Full", none "Empty"; else by the look's level, Mag Drills' level over 10 (`Profile.MagDrillsMastering`), at least the magazine's `CheckOverride` (held within 0 and 2; 1 on 51 of the install's magazines): at 0 one of "Almost empty", "Less than half", "About half", "Almost full" by `(int)(3.33 count / max + 0.5)`; at 1 the count on a magazine of ten or fewer, else "Approx." and the count to the nearest five (`(int)(count / 5 + 0.5) × 5`) from five on, "Less than 5" below; from 2 on the count. A revolver's cylinder or a weapon of several chambers: "Full" only when full, "Empty" at none, else the count. Each key looked up in the install's words. **The details line** under it: the top round's full name (`Cartridges.Last`; a cylinder's, the round in the chamber that fires next, `GetFirstAmmo`, none where that chamber holds no round: since 0.150.0 the logic's chambers and the kinds kept beside them, more-weapons spec, "The rounds and cases seen in chambers of their own"), none where it is empty; in the panel's details look (size 20, (0.741, 0.780, 0.804), hung 4 left of the words' right and 5 below them), the magazine's icon (`icon_info_magsize`) 8 to its left.
