Every other show of the panel (the zeroing, the fire mode) leaves the line out. Since 0.113.0 a look that did not begin no longer makes the magazine known (it did, the game's does not).

**What the character knows of a magazine.** The profile keeps the magazines whose count the character knows (`CheckedMagazines`). Every one carried as the raid begins is known (`LocalPlayer.Create`); one found later is not, and its view writes `?` for its count. A magazine becomes known by the menu's Check magazine, by a check of the weapon's magazine in the hands (`FirearmController.CheckAmmo`), and **whenever a round put in or taken out leaves it empty or full** (`CheckMagazineAmmoDepend`, run as each add or removal is raised); at Mag Drills' elite level, by being put into the character's own things. A shot from a magazine left neither empty nor full unknows it (`UncheckOnShot`, true in the install's globals).

**The check** (`InventoryCheckMagazine`): it first ends any process running (`StopProcesses`: a load, an unload or another check), then waits `BaseCheckTime` (3 s in the install's globals) times `(100 − Mag Drills' check speed + the magazine's CheckTimeModifier) / 100` (−50 to +15 in the install; most 0). **The wait is the engine's frame clock, whole** (`WaitForSeconds` in a coroutine begun on the click's frame), not the round's timer of whole milliseconds. Its end makes the magazine known (`StrictCheckMagazine`), shows "Magazine checked: <its short name>" (`NotifyMagazineChecked`), and plays the magazine's own drop sound (`PlayItemSound(ItemSound, drop)`). The menu's line is offered only for a magazine not known, with rounds in it, not the one in the weapon in hand, and something in the hands; done on a known one it does nothing.

**What is shown, and what stops it**: as a load (`SetInventoryCheckMagazineStatus`: the magazine's view blinks for one round's length, `StartLoading(time, 1)`), and stopped by the same: the inventory closed, its panel shown again, a plain click on the magazine, a medical item starting its work, a load or unload, the magazine thrown out. Stopped, it is not known.

**Not recorded**: course 3's magazine was known from the start, so no line was offered (`3-menu-magazine`, 41.7 s: the menu opened and Unload ammo chosen).

**Built** (2026-10-17, 0.47.0; `MagazineProcess::check`, `RoundTimes::check`, the app's `work_magazine`): the menu's CHECK MAGAZINE starts it, the magazine blinking as a load does, stopped as a load is; at its end the magazine is known, the note says so in the game's words, its drop sound plays. A load or unload that leaves a magazine empty or full makes it known. **Tested**: 3 s is 360 frames at 120 a second, and a wait is not rounded to the millisecond. **Seen in the app** (`local\shots\check_menu_s89.png`, `check_done_s89.png`): the Saiga-12K's 5-round magazine unloaded into the crate of shotguns, `?/5`; CHECK MAGAZINE, 3.000 s, `5/5`. `PROVISIONAL`: Mag Drills untrained (no check speed taken off). **Every wait of the magazine's work now counts from the frame it began on**: the app had counted that frame's own time too, a frame early (a load's first round was a frame earlier than the game's timer has it). **A shot** (`FireOperation`'s shot: a round taken from the magazine runs `UncheckOnShot`, which re-tests the magazine allowing it to be unknown): the app sets the weapon in hand's detachable magazine item beside its logic's count whenever the logic is at rest (the rounds it fired or chambered are gone from the item, the last stack's first), and unknows it where a shot left it neither empty nor full (`inventory::known_after`); a weapon taken in hand takes its magazine's rounds from the item (the logic had kept its own count while the weapon stood by, whatever the inventory did meanwhile). **Seen in the app**: one shot of the AKM, then the inventory, the rifle reading `?/30` (`local\shots\shot_uncheck_s89.png`). **A tube**: its shells are in its item as in the hands: full at the start (the hands' choice), each shell the hands take from the pockets or the vest put onto its top, those fired or chambered gone at rest, known when left full or empty. **Not built**: the chamber's own known state (`CheckChamber`); the notice's own look (the note says it); the elite level's knowing at a touch.

### A chamber not checked (`Profile.CheckedChambers`, `InventoryController.CheckChamber`, `ItemSpecificationPanel.IsChamberLocked`; read and built 2026-11-02)

**What is kept.** The profile keeps the weapons whose chambers the character knows (`CheckedChambers`, a list of weapons' ids); `InventoryController.CheckChamber(weapon, status)` adds or takes a weapon away. Every caller of it in the client was read (the bots' aside): **known** as a look into the chamber begins with no malfunction (`FirearmController`'s idling `CheckChamber`: the look and `CheckChamber(true)` together; with a malfunction its repair or reminder, the chamber as it was; a launcher's own look the same); as a look at the magazine begins on a revolver or a weapon of more than one chamber (`CheckAmmo`, in either view, before its first-person check of the magazine); as a cylinder's reload shuts (`CommitReloadWithAmmo`, then `CheckChamber(true)`); and as a round carried over a slot lights it (`SlotView.HighlightItemViewPosition`, not a preview): a weapon's chamber (`patron_in_weapon…`) whose filter takes the round (`CanAccept`, the filter alone, full or not), or a slot whose name has "Weapon" (the sling's, the back's: not the holster's) holding a weapon with chambers whose first takes it. **Unknown** as a weapon is thrown (`ThrowItem`); at each round a cylinder fires (`CylinderFire`, the round taken from its chamber); and at each shot that feeds the chamber from a magazine, the chamber as the magazine was known before that shot (`FireOperation.UncheckOnShot`: `CheckChamber(weapon, CheckedMagazines has it)`, then the magazine's own uncheck where the settings' `UncheckOnShot` is on; with no magazine, unknown): a checked magazine's first shot keeps the chamber known, its second does not. Nothing else touches it: a magazine's reload, a weapon taken up, a malfunction. **What shows it**: in a raid the window that shows a thing (inspect-window spec, "The parts' panel"): a weapon's chamber slots (`chamber…`, `patron_in_weapon…`), and its cylinder's (`camora…`) where its magazine is a cylinder, locked while it is not known (`IsChamberLocked`, after the raid's own locks).

**Built** (`Carried::checked_chambers`, `know_chamber`; the controller's `chamber_checked` and `magazine_looked`, `inventory_view::settle`, `put_down`, `drag`'s `round_over_chamber`, `chamber_after_shots`): every rule above but the launcher's look (the app's launchers have no look of their own built). `PROVISIONAL`: none known as the run begins and after a death (the profile's list is not read; `courses10`'s `weapon-inspect-menu` began with the M4A1's unknown). The app's shots feed its magazine's item at rest, so the chamber's state after a burst is worked out there, shot by shot (`chamber_after_shots`). **Compared**: the take's unknown chamber and its slot (inspect-window spec); a look into the chamber (Shift and T) and the slot showing its round after (`local\ui_dump_weapon-inspect-menu3_s186.jsonl`): run, not compared with a take of the game's. The rest read from the code alone.

### A magazine's rounds drawn (`ObjectsFactory.CreateItemAsync`, `MagazineInHandsVisual`, `SpringMagazineVisual`, `AmmoActivenessData`, `TransformTools.FindAllTransformsWithNameContains`, `Magazine.GetMaxVisibleAmmo`, `MagazineTemplate.VisibleAmmoRanges`, `IconsHash`, `ItemIconCreator.RenderModel`, `ItemExtensions.CloneVisibleItem`; read 2026-10-18)

The rounds are drawn in the magazines' models too.

**A reloaded weapon's picture** (`QuickSlotItemView.OnItemAdded`, `OnItemRemoved`, `OnRefreshItem` with `CommandStatus.Succeed`; `ReloadExternalMagOperation.SwitchToIdlingState`): a view refreshes its picture on the move's success, which a magazine's reload raises as it ends. So the quick bar and the slot views show the weapon with its old magazine until the reload is over. The app keeps the weapon's picture key from the exchange to the reload's end, or until the weapon leaves the hands (`Carried::reload_key`, `shown_key`). A tube's or a cylinder's filling, round by round, is not read.

**Through a reload, and a magazine's picture at work** (some magazines, the GEN3 40-round among them, had shown no rounds, and loading had changed the magazine's view with every round, blinking it and doubling the loader). **The magazine in hand** was filled, at the reload's start (where the item's magazine is already the new one), by the logic's count, which is the old one's until `MagOut`: a reload from an empty magazine left the new full one empty to the end, any magazine of the same kind (one of another kind escaped it, its rig made anew while no magazine is in sight). Now through a reload the item's own count fills it (the game's new object is filled from its own: `FillAmmoObjectsOnStart`), and the old magazine, while still in the hand (before `MagHide`), keeps its rounds as they were drawn (`magazine_rounds::show_rounds`, `Firearm::old_magazine_in_hand`). **A magazine's picture at work**: the game's key (`IconsHash.GetItemHash`) has the visible figure and each stack's place and template, never a stack's count, and the view asks for it again only on the load's last round (`LoadMagazineProcess`: `RaiseRefreshItemsEvents(i == _count - 1)`), a stack added or taken away (`OnItemAdded`, `OnItemRemoved`), and an unload that leaves the magazine empty (`RaiseRefreshEvent(_ammoLeft == 0)`); while a new picture is made its loader shows with the picture off (`ItemView.IconChangedHandler`: the old one is not kept). TRP asked again on every round (the page drawn afresh), so the picture changed with the visible rounds (rounds 1 to 3 of a load on a `1-3` magazine; every round of a UMP's `1-25`), each change with its loader over the load component's (the two spinners). Now the key is held while the magazine works (`Carried::frozen_keys`, `inventory_view::shown_key`), taken again at a stack added or taken away and given up at the work's end (the load's last round, the unload left empty, a round that could not move). `PROVISIONAL`: a work stopped takes its own key again (the game's view keeps the old picture until it is made anew). Measured (a full AK55 unload): one picture asked for, the empty one at the end, where three had been (at 2, 1 and 0). Drawing only: the load component's fill made at the work's share and colour and the mouse's hold kept across the page drawn afresh, so that neither flickers each round. Not built: the blink fading only the main picture (TRP's fades every picture of the box).

**Where a magazine's model has rounds** (a weapon of one's own's magazine: its donor's magazine's object and points, its own model below them, since 0.65.0: `custom-weapons.md`, "The magazine's object"). A magazine's model holds empty objects named `patron_` and a number (the AKM's 6L10: `patron_001` to `patron_005` at its lips, `patron_030` at its foot). Whenever the game makes a magazine's object (`CreateItemAsync`: in a weapon in hand or on the ground, lying loose, for a picture) it gives it a visual: a belt's for a magazine with a belt, a stationary weapon's own for one of those, and otherwise `SpringMagazineVisual`. That finds the points once, breadth first through the magazine's own objects (`FindAllTransformsWithNameContains`, active or not), each with its number (the name without `patron_`).

**Filled once, when it is made** (`FillAmmoObjectsOnStart`): with no rounds nothing is done. Otherwise each point whose number is more than the count is switched off, and each other one switched on, with a round's own model made under it: the round as many from the top as the point's place in that breadth-first list, set at the point's origin a quarter turn about x, with its round's objects on and its case's off (`SetUsed(false)`).

**Every other frame after** (`UpdateCartridgesActiveState`, on the frames of its parity: each visual made takes the other parity from the one before it), a point whose number is more than the count is switched off. Nothing is switched back on: a round fired goes, a round loaded shows only in a magazine made afresh. **The spring is not moved**: the visual's spring animation is never given one (`_springAnimation` is only ever cleared), so `RefreshVisualParts` does nothing; the stationary weapon's own visual (`MagVisualController`) is the one that sets a spring's clip by the count.

**A picture** (`ItemIconCreator.RenderModel`) is drawn from a clone of the item with what it shows (`CloneVisibleItem`: a magazine's rounds kept unless its template hides its contents, `HideEntrails`), so a loaded magazine's picture has its rounds. **Its key** (`IconsHash.GetItemHash`) has, for a magazine, a figure of its visible rounds (`GetMaxVisibleAmmo`) beside its template and every stack of its rounds by kind and place (not by how many it holds): the count itself where a range of its template's (`VisibleAmmoRangesString`, ranges `start-end` joined by `;`; 1 to 2 where it has none or it does not read) holds it; below the first range, that range's start; past a range and short of the next, that range's end; past the last, the last's end. So pictures of counts the figure does not tell apart are one, drawn at the count first asked for (the 6L10's ranges are `1-3`: three rounds and thirty share a picture, and its points past the third lie inside its body).

**Built** (2026-10-18): `trp_sim::magazine_view` (the ranges, the visible figure, the points breadth first, which round a point shows, a point staying on), `Kind::visible_rounds` and `hide_entrails`, `trp_data::weapon::attach_round`; in the app `magazine_rounds` under the options' **`MAGAZINE ROUNDS`** (restart required; off, no round is drawn in a model or a picture). For the weapon in hand the rig's magazine (its default build's model, as the rig is) is filled when the rig is made and again when the magazine in the weapon changes, and its points go off by the logic's count, the game's item losing its round at the shot (the app's item catches up at rest); a magazine on the ground, or a weapon's there, is filled as its model is put down, from its item; a weapon hung on the body (`slung_view`) as its model is hung, its chambered round with it, the model made afresh when they change. Each round is its own model asked for as the round in a magazine (`loose::ROUND_MODEL`) and hung on its point when read. A magazine's picture has its rounds in its key (`icons::picture_key`: the templates of as many rounds from the top as `picture_rounds` says) and is drawn with them (`PROVISIONAL`: the lesser of the count and the game's figure, where the game draws a key's picture at the count first asked for). **Seen** in captures: the AK55 on the ground with its top rounds at its lips beside the same with the switch off, and its picture with them; **logged**: the AKM's magazine's six points (1 to 5 and 29) going off one by one as it is fired empty. **Tested**: the ranges, the figure, the points' order and their rounds (`magazine_view`); the 6L10's points and ranges and the AKM's magazine's points (`install`). **Not compared with the game.** **Not built**: a belt's rounds (`BeltMagazineInHandsVisual`) and a stationary weapon's spring; the rounds in a weapon's picture (its picture is its default build's, without its item's magazine).

### A fold from the menu (`ItemUiContext.FoldItem`, `ItemManipulator.CanFold`, `Fold`, `Resize_Helper`, `FoldOperation`, `Player.TrySetInHands`, `FirearmController.FoldStockOperation`; read 2026-10-17)

**What folds.** A weapon made to fold or slide in (`Foldable` or `Retractable` on the weapon), or a stock made to fold (`Foldable` on a stock): the game gives only these two a fold (`FoldableComponent`). A thing's fold is the first in it and its parts, itself first, each part before the parts fitted to it, through the places that merge (`GetItemComponentsInChildren`). It can be turned if its folded slot, where it names one, holds something (`CanBeFolded`: the AKS-74U's fold is its stock's slot); a weapon with any part that blocks folding (`BlocksFolding`: 54 parts in the install) cannot fold at all (`CanFold`).

**The lines.** Fold is offered where the fold can be turned and stands unfolded, Unfold where it is folded; either is dimmed with "You can't fold this item" where a part blocks it, and with "Can't fold the stock now" where the fold would not fit (below).

**Done** (`FoldItem`): the interface's stock sound (`MenuStock`) plays as the line is done; the fold is one inventory operation (`FoldOperation`), the same as the fold key's. It turns the fold at once and gives the thing its new size where it lies (`Resize_Helper`: from the folding part up, each weapon or part that lies in a grid is made its new size at the same corner and turn; with no room for that, refused and nothing changed: a weapon folded in a backpack may not unfold if something now lies where its stock would come out). Then, if the thing is in the hands, the hands are asked (`TrySetInHands`): a weapon whose logic idles plays its fold (`FoldStockOperation`, "The stock" in `shotgun.md`: the inventory's flag put down, so the arms come up out of the open screen's pose for it, and go back down when it is over); one that does not refuses, and the fold is undone. **A weapon keeps its fold wherever it goes**: drawn, its hands are told how it stands.

**Measured** (inventory spec's sizes, "Compared with the running game"): a recording with the M3's stock folded gives the M3 4 cells by 2 and its stock alone 1 by 2, which the model gives with the stock folded (`a_folded_stock_sizes_its_weapon_as_the_game_did`). **No recording has a fold made** (course 3's rifle had none to fold).

**Built** (2026-10-17, 0.47.0; `Item::folded`, `inventory::folding_part`, `can_be_folded`, `can_fold`, `fold`, `item_menu`'s lines, the app's `do_line` and `settle`): FOLD and UNFOLD by the fold's state, dimmed by the two reasons; done on a thing elsewhere, it is turned at once and its size with it, refused where it would not fit; on the weapon in hand, its hands are asked and the thing folds as they take it; the fold key folds the thing in hand too; a weapon drawn comes with its fold, its recoil and hold and the body's width told; the stock's sound. A loadout's fold is read (`upd.Foldable.Folded`), and a recording's from recorder 0.10.2 (`fold`). **Seen in the app**: the M3 in hand folded from its menu (`STOCK FOLD` played after the arms came up out of the screen's pose, `local\fold_frames_s89.txt`); the M3 in the crate of shotguns folded, 4 cells wide, its menu then offering UNFOLD (`local\shots\fold_crate_s89.png`). **A folded weapon's picture** since 0.47.2: its own, drawn to its folded size with the bones its fold's view turns (`icons.md`, "A fold"; the Mk 16 seen folded). The size rule gives the SCAR-L's black build 6 cells unfolded (its receiver's and barrel's forced cells, 2 each) and 5 folded: by the code and the install's figures, **no recording has one**. **Not built**: the fold's look on a thing on the ground.

### A tag (`TagComponent`, `ItemUiContext.EditTag`, `ResetTag`, `EditTagWindow`, `TagColorsPanel`, `ColorView`, `ValidationInputField`, `GridItemView.UpdateTag`, `ResizeTag`, `InfoWindow.UpdateTag`; read 2026-10-17, night)

**What takes a tag**: a magazine, a lockable container and a simple container (a case): each is made with its tag (`TagComponent`: a name and a colour's number). Their menus have Tag always (a simple or lockable container its Open before it), and Reset tag once the tag has a name, which sets no name and colour 0.

**The window** (`EditTagWindow`, opened as the thing's other windows are, `OpenWindow`: one a thing, cascaded 40 units from the newest window, or in the middle): its caption (`ADD/EDIT CONTAINER TAG`, the install's words) with its cross; the game's seven colours (`GetColor`: a number past them is the first), cells made by its colours' panel from a template into a grid of cells 25 by 25, 15 apart (`TagColorsPanel.Show`), the chosen one's border on; the count of the name's letters against the field's most (`{n}/{limit}`: 16 in the install's prefab, `m_CharacterLimit`); the field (`ValidationInputField`, a TextMeshPro field: its placeholder `container tag` while empty, its text at the top left 10 and 6 in); and `SAVE` (`DefaultUIButton`). **The field** takes the keys once pressed on (nothing gives it them as the window opens) and lets them go on a press anywhere else or on Enter; its validation, `Everything`, keeps letters and figures of any script, the underscore, the space and its list of marks (anything else typed is dropped), and up to its most. While it has the keys the game's commands are not heard. A colour cell pressed is chosen. **Save** (`EditTagWindow.Save`): an empty name takes colour 0; where name or colour changed the tag is set (`TagComponent.Set`); the window shuts. Escape or the cross shut it with nothing saved.

**Where a tag shows**: on the thing's view in a grid or a slot (`UpdateTag`, `ResizeTag`), a plate at its top left in the tag's colour (`TagPanel`, 14 high), under the caption: where the view's width less the caption's drawn width and 2 leaves less than 40 units the plate is the view's width and the name is not shown, else the name's drawn width and 12, from 40 to that room, with the name (12, left). In the window that shows a thing (`InfoWindow.UpdateTag`), a plate under its caption bar sized to the name's drawn width and 12, at least 40, with the name (14, centred).

**Built** (2026-10-17, night, 0.49.0; `inventory::Tag`, `takes_tag`, `set_tag`, `item_menu`'s lines, `screen::tag_window`, `input_limit`, `health_view::draw_tag_window`, the app's `tag_windows`): as read, with `TARKOV UI` the game's own window and plates, without it the app's look of the same. **Tested**: the lines by the tag (`a_tag_is_offered_and_reset_when_named`), the field's most read after its settings (`a_text_field_s_limit_is_read_after_its_settings`). **Seen in the app** (`local\shots\tag_window_s89.png`, `tag_typing_zoom_s89.png`, `tag_saved_zoom_s89.png`, `tag_info_s89.png`): the rig's first magazine, TAG, the window; the teal cell chosen, the field pressed, `ammo` typed (4/16, a caret), SAVE: the magazine's view with its teal plate the view's width under its caption (one cell: no room for the name), and its window with `ammo` on the plate. **Not compared with the game** (no recording or capture of a tag). `PROVISIONAL`: the seven cells drawn where the prefab's own seven of the same colours lie (the grid's placing as the editor left it; the cells the game makes are not those objects); the texts' and the grid's sizes the layout here does not ask of them, put on them as TextMeshPro's (a line's ascent to its descent; a text's drawn width) and the grid's one row; the field's text margins put into its rectangle (the panel drawing here lays a text in its rectangle as it is); `SAVE`'s look under the mouse and pressed (its `TweenAnimatedButton`) not read: drawn as the prefab stands. **The field** edits as the shared text field since 2026-11-10 ("A text field", below; `TagEdit::field`): its first press selects all it says (`m_OnFocusSelectAll`, on in the install), a press then puts the caret under the mouse, the keys move it and select, and its caret blinks (it was a line a unit wide that stood after the last letter and did not blink); the window without `TARKOV UI` draws `|` at the caret and `[`, `]` round a selection in its words. **Not built**: a tag a profile already has (`upd.Tag`), the error's flash (a name starting or ending with a space). **Found and fixed in 0.51.0**: from 0.49.0 the window of a thing with no tag showed its plate's placeholder words ("Blablabla"): the plate was hidden but its text, drawn apart from it, was not.

### A text field (`TMP_InputField`, `TMP_TextUtilities`, `ValidationInputField`, `CustomTextMeshProInputField`; read 2026-11-10)

**What it is**: every field the game types into is TextMeshPro's input field under one of two of the game's classes: `ValidationInputField` (the tag window's field, the MASTERING page's search field), which adds a check of what the field says, and `CustomTextMeshProInputField` (the field of the window that asks how many), which adds nothing. The rules below are that component's as Unity publishes it (the package's `TMP_InputField.cs` and `TMP_TextUtilities.cs`), for a field of one line that does not edit rich text, which these are. The field keeps two places in its words, each a gap between two letters: the caret's (`stringPosition`) and the selection's moving end (`stringSelectPosition`). With both at one gap nothing is selected and the caret is drawn; otherwise what lies between them is selected, and the selection's box is drawn and no caret.

**The figures are the install's** (`probe tagwindow`, `probe masteringpage`, `probe splitdialog`, each reading the field with `screen::input_field`), the same on all three fields: the selection's colour (0.659, 0.808, 1.0, 0.753), a caret 1 unit wide in the text's colour (none of its own), blinking 0.85 times a second, and all it says selected as it takes the keys (`m_OnFocusSelectAll`). The most letters it takes (`m_CharacterLimit`): the tag's 16, the search field's 20.

**Taking the keys and letting them go.** A press of the left button on the field gives it the keys, and nothing else does. As it takes them it selects all it says (`OnFocus`, `SelectAll`: the caret at the end, the moving end at the start) and its caret's blink begins (`ActivateInputFieldInternal`, `SetCaretVisible`); that press puts no caret. Enter lets the keys go, and so does a press anywhere else; without the keys neither caret nor selection is drawn.

**A press on a field that has the keys** (`OnPointerDown`) puts the caret at the gap under the mouse, or with Shift held the moving end alone, which selects from the caret to there. The gap (`GetCursorIndexFromPosition`): the letter nearest the mouse is the first whose drawn box holds it, else the one whose box's edge is nearest, the earlier of two as near; left of that box's middle the gap before the letter, from the middle on the gap after. A letter's box is its glyph's quad, a space's a sliver at its origin, and the field's line always ends with a letter of no width (a zero-width space the component adds), so a press past the words goes to the end. A press within half a second of the press before (`m_DoubleClickDelay`, the component's own 0.5; the press that gave the keys counts as one) selects the word the mouse is on (`FindIntersectingWord`: from the word's first letter's box to its last's, within the line's height), else the nearest letter. A word is as TextMeshPro counts words: letters, figures and hyphens together, an apostrophe between two of them with them.

**A drag.** With the button held from a press on the field, once the mouse has gone the event system's drag threshold from the press (5 pixels, "With the mouse"), the moving end follows the mouse to the gap under it (`OnDrag`), the caret staying. From the press that gave the keys the caret is at the end (all was selected), so the selection then runs from the mouse to the end.

**The keys** (`KeyPressed`):

| Key | What it does |
|---|---|
| Left, Right | The caret a letter that way, stopping at the ends. With something selected, only puts the caret at that end of the selection (`MoveLeft`, `MoveRight`). |
| Shift with them | The moving end goes alone, the caret stays: the selection grows or shrinks by a letter. |
| Ctrl with them | A word in place of a letter (`FindNextWordBegin`, `FindPrevWordBegin`): right, to after the first of the separators (space, full stop, comma, tab, line ends) that is a letter on or more, else the end; left, to after the last separator two letters back or more, else the start. With Shift, the moving end alone. |
| Home, Up, Page Up | To the start; with Shift the moving end alone. |
| End, Down, Page Down | To the end; with Shift the moving end alone. |
| Backspace | Takes away what is selected, the caret left where the selection began; with nothing selected, the letter before the caret. |
| Delete | The same, with nothing selected the letter after the caret. |
| Ctrl with A, and no Shift or Alt | Selects all. |
| Ctrl with C, X, V | Copies, cuts and pastes through the system's clipboard. |
| Enter | Lets the keys go. |
| Escape | Lets the keys go and puts back what the field said when it took them (`m_RestoreOriginalTextOnEscape`). |

After any key but those that let the keys go, and after a press, the caret's blink begins again if the field says anything (`UpdateLabel`, `SetCaretVisible`); a field emptied keeps the blink it had.

**A letter typed** (`Append`, `Insert`): what is selected is taken away first; then, unless the field already has its most letters, the letter goes in at the caret and the caret after it. A field of one line takes no tab and no line end. The game's own check comes after (`ValidationInputField.ValidateEnteredString`, hung on the field's change): where what the field now says has a letter its validation does not allow, those letters are taken out again (and the text flashes red, not built). So a letter not allowed still takes away what was selected, and it leaves the caret a letter further on, the end at most: the field moved its caret past the letter before the check took the letter out.

**Drawn** (`GenerateCaret`, `GenerateHightlight`): the caret's left edge at its gap (the origin of the letter after it; after the last letter, where that letter's advance ends, the spacing counted), its width to the right, the line's ascender to its descender; shown for the first half of each blink of 1 / 0.85 s. The selection's box from its first gap to its last, the same height. Both lie under the words (`TMP_SelectionCaret`, the text's first sibling).

**Built** (2026-11-10; `screens/text_field`: `TextField`, `FieldKey`, `Rules`, `field_key`, `FieldPlace`, `field_place`, `draw_field_marks`; `TmpFace::letter_places`; `InputField::limit`): one field with those rules, which the MASTERING page's search field ("Weapon mastering" in `skills.md`) and the tag window's field ("A tag") both are, each drawn from its own field's figures as read from the install. A caret is never drawn under a pixel wide (TRP's own, `split_view::caret_width`). **Tested as plain code** (`text_field::tests`, `tmp_text::tests::a_line_s_letters_lie_where_its_width_puts_them`): every key with and without Shift and Ctrl, a letter at the caret, over a selection, at the limit and one the check drops, the presses (the first, the next, with Shift, the second within half a second), the drag and its threshold, the words, the gap and the word under the mouse, and the engine's keys told to the field. **Seen in the app** (2026-11-10, at 1920 by 1080, driven by `--tap`, `--move` and `--drag`, whose words now name the arrows, Home, End and Backspace: `platform/keys::tapped`, tested by `a_run_can_tap_the_keys_a_text_field_hears`; every picture in `local\shots\`, `akm 74` typed first unless said): in the tag window, `field_tag_caret_keys_s282.png` (`74`, Page Up, `akm `: `akm |74`), `field_tag_select_keys_s282.png` (then Shift and Page Down: `74` in the selection's box, under the words), `field_tag_caret_click_s282.png` (a press between `k` and `m` puts the caret there), `field_tag_select_drag_s282.png` (a press and a drag: `m 74` selected), `field_tag_arrows_s282.png` (Left, Left, Right: `akm 7|4`), `field_tag_home_back_s282.png` (Home, Right, Right, Backspace: `a|m 74`, 5/16) and `field_tag_ctrl_a_s282.png` (Ctrl+A: all selected); the MASTERING search field's the same ("Weapon mastering" in `skills.md`); the window without `TARKOV UI`, `field_standin_window_s282.png` (opened, `container tag` faint), `field_standin_caret_s282.png` (Left: `akm 7|4`) and `field_standin_select_s282.png` (Shift and Home: `[akm 74]`). **Not compared with the game**: no recording or capture of the game has a field with the keys. `PROVISIONAL`: the caret a letter on after a letter the check drops (read from the two classes together, not seen); the letters' boxes go without the padding the text's material puts round a glyph, and the nearest letter is found across only. **Not built**: Escape's putting back (Escape shuts the window or the screen here, as before); the clipboard's keys (the app has no clipboard); the words moved in the field to keep the caret in sight once they are wider than it (`AdjustRectTransformRelativeToViewport`: 16 wide letters pass the tag field's width); the validation's red flash; the selection creeping while the mouse is held outside the field (`MouseDragOutsideRect`: in a field of one line the drag already reaches the ends). The field of the window that asks how many keeps its own code (`asking`): all selected or the caret at the end.

### The wish list (`WishlistContextInteractions`, `WishlistManager`, `ItemUiContext.AddToWishList`, `InteractionButtonsContainer.SetSubInteractions`, `SimpleContextMenu`, `SimpleSpecifiedContextButtons`, `WishlistGridView`, `ItemSpecificationPanel`, `StaticIcons`; read 2026-10-17, night)

**What it is**: the profile's list of kinds of thing (templates, not things), each in one of five groups (`EWishlistGroup`: Quests, Hideout, Trading, Equipment, Other; in the install's words Tasks, Hideout, Barter, Equipment, Other). Every thing of a kind on it is marked.

**The line and its menu of lines.** ADD TO WISHLIST (the table above says which things have it) opens lines of its own as the mouse comes onto it (`CreateInternalContextButton`'s hover): a second menu, a copy of the menu template its holder of lines names (`SubMenuTemplate`, a `SimpleContextMenu` laid out from the same three line templates as the first), its top left put at the line's top right, then moved in to stay on the screen and put on a whole pixel (`CorrectPosition`: `CorrectPositionResolution`); where that moved it sideways at all (`IsZero`: less than `float.Epsilon`), it goes to the line's left instead (its right edge at the line's left, not rounded again), at the height it was put at. A line's right edge is seldom on a whole pixel (the lines 5 units in and some 160 wide), so **it is nearly always on the left**: `courses10`'s `inspect-window`, four times of four with 1,100 pixels free on the right, its first line level with the line it is of. It shows on the frame the mouse comes onto the line. The line stays lit while it is open (`Blocked`: the selected colour). The mouse onto another line of the first menu shuts it (and opens that line's, where it has one); the mouse out of both menus and their shadows shuts it (`OnPointerExit`, unless into the other; a shadow is its menu's). Its shadow lies over the left ends of the first menu's lines, and a line there is not under the mouse (`courses10`'s `inspect-window`, frame 432: the mouse 6.67 pixels inside the shadow's right edge, 1242.67, and on the wish list's own line). Any button let go shuts both (each menu's `Update`). The line itself, clicked, does nothing but shut the menu.

**Its lines**, in order (`EWishlistInteractions`): Tasks, Equipment, Hideout, Other, Barter, each in the wish list's template (`_wishlistInteractions`: the blue line, its icon box 28 by 16) with its group's icon (`StaticIcons.WishlistSprites`); then REMOVE FROM WISHLIST in the discard template (the red line under its divider) with the icon `Characteristics/Icons/RemoveFromWishlist`. Their captions are the install's words (`EWishlistGroup/<group>`, `Wishlist/RemoveFromWishlist`) in capitals. A group's line is dimmed where the kind is in that group already ("Already in this group": "Already in this list", the install keeping the key in lower case, which the game's locale finds as it ignores case, `ConvertToLocale`); Remove's where it is in none ("Not in wishlist": "Item is not in Wishlist"). **Done**: a group's line puts the kind in that group, or moves it there from another (`ChangeItemGroup`); Remove takes it off.

**The marks** (`WishlistGridView`: shown where the thing is examined and its kind is on the list): the view's `WishlistGlow` (a picture over the whole view, 1 in from its edge), its border (`WishlistBorder` in a slot's view, 2 out from the view's edge; `WishlistBorders` in a grid's, 1 in; four edges a unit thick) and its `WishlistIcon` with the group's outlined icon (`WishlistSpritesOutline`), 18 by 14, the first of the view's bottom left column (a column of things, lowest at the view's bottom left 3 in: the icon, the armour's class, a part's marks, the weapon's round, each as tall as it asks). The window that shows a thing has its own `WishlistIcon` with the group's icon (`ItemSpecificationPanel._wishlistIcon`). Each follows the list as it changes.

**The icons** are two tables of the Resource `Static Icons`, kept as the game's enum dictionaries (a list of key names, then as many pointers): the first two whose keys are the five groups are the plain and the outlined.

**The hideout's own additions** (`IsInHideoutQolWishlist`: with the game's option on, what the hideout's next stages and favourite recipes need, marked in the Hideout group) go by the profile's hideout: not read here.

**Built** (2026-10-17, night, 0.51.0; `item_menu::Wishlist`, `WishLine`, `wish_lines`, `wish`, `screen::context_submenu`, `wishlist_icons`, the app's menu of lines and marks): as read; the list kept for the run, empty at its start; with `TARKOV UI` the game's own menu of lines and marks, without it the app's look of the same (a blue border and the group's name). **Tested**: the lines' dimming and their work (`the_wish_list_s_lines_go_by_the_kind_s_group`); the menu template and both tables of icons read from the install (`the_wish_list_s_menu_and_icons_are_read`); the menu of lines kept on the screen (`a_menu_of_lines_goes_right_of_its_line_or_left`), on the left where its corner is rounded (`a_menu_of_lines_goes_left_where_its_corner_is_rounded`, from `courses10`'s `inspect-window`), a menu kept on the screen on a whole pixel (`a_menu_kept_on_the_screen_is_on_a_whole_pixel`). **Compared with `courses10`'s `inspect-window`** (2026-11-02; until then the app put the menu of lines on the right, the rounding not read): the M4A1's menu of lines on the left, its ground at 117.12,881.67, 254.88 by 170.66, every line, caption, colour and divider on the game's pixels; BARTER and then REMOVE FROM WISHLIST done, the glow and the border on the slot's view on the game's pixels on the frame the button is let go, and gone on that frame. **Differs**: the group's icon on the slot's view (the game's 102.21,774.67, its foot on the weapon's mastering icon's top and centred in the column; the app's 92.88,813.33: its column leaves out the mastering icon); the app's own line under the slots ("M4A1: wish list, Barter"; the game writes nothing). **Seen in the app** (`local\shots\wish_menu_zoom_s90.png`, `wish_sub_zoom_s90.png`, `wish_mark_one_s90.png`, `wish_info_zoom_s90.png`, `wish_app_zoom_s90.png`): the rig's magazine's menu, the mouse on ADD TO WISHLIST and its lines at its right (the groups blue with their icons, REMOVE FROM WISHLIST red and dimmed under its divider); EQUIPMENT chosen: the three AK55 magazines (one kind) with the glow, the border and the outlined icon over the part's marks; the magazine's window with the group's icon at its preview's bottom left; without `TARKOV UI` the app's menu of lines, EQUIPMENT dimmed with "Already in this list", and the blue borders. **Not compared with the game** (no recording or capture of the wish list). `PROVISIONAL`: the list empty at the start (the profile's `WishList` not read), as the tag. **Not built**: the hideout's additions; the armour class's icon in the view's column (not drawn here, so not counted under the wish list's icon); the notification of a thing on the list found in a raid (`RaidWishlistNotification`).

### Disassemble (`ItemUiContext.Disassemble`, `DisassembleOperationsList`, `Weapon.Mods`; read 2026-10-17, night)

**When**: on a weapon of the character's own (`Owner`: not one in a container looked into), dimmed on one worn ("You can't disassemble equipped item"). **What it does**: the interface's disassemble sound (`MenuWeaponDisassemble`), then each part fitted to the weapon itself (`Weapon.Mods`: its own slots' parts, not their parts', which go with them; a chambered round is no part), in its slots' order, examined, is found a place as Ctrl and a click finds one (`QuickFindAppropriatePlace`, the order `MoveToAnotherSide`, no warnings); the moves found are run one after another, and a part that finds none, or may not come off where it is (a raid's vital or fixed parts, `MovePathCheck`), stays. No move's own sound plays.

**Built** (2026-10-17, night, 0.47.3; `inventory::disassemble`, the app's `do_line`): as read; the note says how many parts came off. **Tested** (`a_weapon_comes_apart_but_for_its_vital_parts`): a stock goes with its pad, a vital barrel and the chambered round stay. **Seen in the app** (`local\shots\dis_done_s89.png`): the M9A3 taken into the backpack from the crate of pistols, DISASSEMBLE: its magazine to the open crate, the other three parts (vital in a raid) kept, "1 of its 4 parts off". **Not compared with the game** (course 3 did not disassemble). **Not built**: the weapon's picture after (pictures are of each template's preset: the parts taken off still show).

### A switch on the head: a face shield, night or thermal vision (`TogglableComponent`, `ItemUiContext.ToggleItem`, `Player.ToggleGoggles`, `PlayerToggleOperation`, `HelmetDress`, `PlayerCameraController.OnFaceShieldComponentChanged`; read 2026-10-17)

**The switch** is a part's own state, on or off (`TogglableComponent.On`): a face shield on a hinge (`HasHinge`; down is on), night vision, thermal vision. A thing's switch is the first of its own and its parts' (`GetItemComponentsInChildren`). A face shield is switched from its helmet's menu, never its own (`CanToggle`: a face shield's thing must be a helmet).

**What switches it.** The menu's Turn on or Turn off (`FLIP DOWN`, `FLIP UP` in the install's words), whichever the state allows; the key `ToggleGoggles` (`N` in this install; `MoveInputTranslator`), which switches the switch of what is worn on the head (the headwear slot's thing and its parts), at any time. Either runs one inventory operation, made at once.

**What follows** (`PlayerToggleOperation.Dispose`): if the switch is on what is worn on the head and the character's state lets the hands act (`StateIsSuitableForHandInput`), the hands are asked for a gesture (`SetInteractInHands`): `FaceshieldOnGear` or `FaceshieldOffGear` for a face shield (the body's animator states `13 FShield On`, `14 FShield Off`), `NightVisionOnGear` or `NightVisionOffGear` otherwise (`11 NV On`, `12 NV Off`), each resetting what waits to play and forced. As every such gesture, it is refused in a sprint and while the thing in hand's animator is not at rest: so never from the inventory, where the weapon's hands are down. **The sound**: the worn face shield's, night vision's or thermal vision's state changed, the character's own clip for it plays (`Sounds.FaceShieldOn`, `FaceShieldOff`, `NightVisionOn` and so on, in the movement's bundle), not placed in the world, in the character's group, at full volume, through the movement's mixer group (`ClientPlayer.PlayToggleSound`). **It follows what is worn, not the key** (`Player.CreateSlotObservers`, `SlotObserver`, `PlayFaceShieldSound`, `PlayNightVisionSound`, `PlayThermalVisionSound`; read 2026-11-07): for each of the three kinds the player watches the first part of that kind on the headwear's thing and its parts, told when that part changes (put on, taken off) or its switch does, and its state is "there and on" (a part with no switch counts as on); the clip plays whenever that state differs from the one last heard. So a helmet taken off with its shield down sounds `FaceShieldOff`, one put on with its night vision on sounds `NightVisionOn`, and one put on or taken off with its shield up sounds nothing. The watchers are bound as the player is made with nothing heard (`BindableEvent.Bind` calls at once), so what is worn on is heard as the character appears. **Night vision's clips are thermal vision's** (`thermal_activate`, `thermal_deactivate`: `probe stepsounds`).

**What it does to the look**: a helmet turns its face shield on a hinge between two turns its prefab keeps, by two curves of a second's time (`HelmetDress`, `HysteresisFilter`; built since 0.50.0: body animator spec, "A worn thing's parts and a helmet's hinge"). Not built: in first person the face shield is a mask over the view with its scratches, faded in and out by the camera's own pair of curves (`VisorEffect`, the camera's `HysteresisFilter`); night and thermal vision are camera effects of their own.

**Recorded** (`courses2`, `3-menu-worn`, the ZSh-1-2M's face shield, `5aa7e3abe5b5b000171d064d`): from the menu, down 0.092 s after the click and up 0.075 s after another, with `glassshield_on` and `glassshield_off` and no gesture (the interaction index unchanged); by the key, down at 12.7596 s with the body's interaction `13` begun on the same frame and over at 14.1018 (1.342 s), up at 15.1270 with `14`, over at 16.4688 (1.342 s).

**Recorded since** (`courses8`, recorder 0.14.0, by hand: `visors-inside-and-out-during-other-animations`, the Ops-Core FAST MT worn with its visor, `5a16b672fcdbcb001912fa83`, flipped twice from the menu and 26 times by `N`; `visor-loose-in-inventory`, the visor lying in the backpack, fitted to the worn helmet at 5.26 s, flipped down and up from the menu and taken off again at 16.15 s). **The switch is made on the frame of the key going down, or of the menu's click let go**, and its clip plays on that frame, every time. **The gesture**: asked on the key's frame, with the body's interactions layer put to rest on that frame and the gesture's state entered on the next (the forced reset first); it plays at rest, in blind fire (above and to the side), seen from outside, and from outside with the sights up; each press restarts it with the other way's gesture, ten quick presses one after another, the last running its 1.345 s; pressed while the head light's gesture (`HelmetRailGear`, 15) plays, it takes the layer on the key's frame; never from the menu (the weapon's hands in the inventory are not at rest) nor in a reload (the switch made all the same, the visor moving with the hands busy). **Fitted from the backpack** onto the worn helmet, the visor is heard by the camera on the frame of the move, up (its switch off as it lay); taken off, the camera hears none; a visor lying loose in the inventory is never heard.

**Compared** (2026-11-01, 0.181.1; both, replayed from the recorded keys, `byhand --scenario` writing the goggles' key as `goggles` and a flip from the menu, which the replay cannot click, as `switch:<template>` on its frame; on the recorded frames, with what was carried as each recorded list has it: `local\c8_visor_run_s182.sh`): every switch on the game's frame, its state on every frame (`head_switch`), its sound on the game's frames and none elsewhere (`visor_sound`); the gesture's flag, its index and the hands' states on every frame of both, no gap: each of the cases above, the refusals in the menu and the reload, the restarts and the cut-in over the head light. **Found and fixed**: the app asked for the gesture after the controller's step, a frame late for the body's animator (the game's input comes before its animator); the goggles' key now has a system of its own before the step (`inventory_view::goggles_key`, as the head light's keys are), and the switch's sound and gesture are one rule for the key and the menu (`switch_followed`; `trp_sim::visor::toggle_goggles`, `switch_gesture`, `Interaction::of_switch`). The replay's own: under the inventory's screen it gave the weapon the trigger and the aim (the click on FLIP DOWN fired, a right click raised the sights), where the app's controller hears neither: now as the app's. **Not compared**: the span seen from outside (27.9 to 52.9 s in the first: the replay keeps one point of view a recording, so the camera and the weapon there were not set beside the game's; the switches, gestures, mask and hinge there were); after it, in first person, the reload's new magazine's weight and ergonomics (the replay keeps the recorded build's: the hip penalty's ergonomics 35.6 against the game's 47.975) and a shot's recoil (its draws are not the game's). In the first 27.9 s, first person, menu flips and presses at rest: the eye and the camera to 0.1 mm, the weapon within 5 mm, the hands within 0.62 degrees (as the inventory opens), the game's.

**Recorded since** (`courses2\6-helmet`, recorder 0.10.0, by hand, 59.5 s at 120 frames a second; the course: "N (down, then up) standing; once aimed; once walking; H (the light) twice"). The ZSh-1-2M's face shield flipped six times by `N`: standing, twice with the sights up, twice lying down with them up and once lying down; the helmet then discarded from the inventory with its shield down, and the Galvion Caiman put on from the backpack with its GPNVG-18 on and a WF-501B on its rail; night vision flipped seven times: standing, once just before walking off (the walk begun under the gesture), and once on the frame a jump left the ground (the gesture over the jump, its 1.342 s whole), once just before another jump (likewise); the head light's gesture eight times, four of them walking. **Every switch gave its gesture on its own frame** (`InteractionIndex` 13 or 14 for the shield, 11 or 12 for night vision; the flag up 1.342 s, once 1.343 and once 1.348), aimed, lying down and on the jump's frame alike. **Every switch's clip on the switch's frame**, and two with no key: `glassshield_off` on the frame the shielded helmet left the head (17.564 s), `thermal_activate` on the frame the Caiman came onto it (18.900 s). The head light's `weapon_light_switcher1` 0.800 s after each of its eight gestures (read off the recording; tactical-devices spec, "The look").

**Compared** (2026-11-07, 0.207.1; replayed from the keys recorder 0.10.0 wrote, which did not include `N`, `H` or the walking keys: `byhand --scenario` writes a switch flipped with the screen shut as the goggles' key, the head light's gesture as begun, as a door's, and the walk not at all; the head carried as each recorded list has it, a part put on the head with its switch as that frame writes it: `local\c2_helmet_run_s226.sh`). **The whole take**: every switch on the game's frame (`head_switch`), every sound of the head on the game's frame and none elsewhere (`visor_sound`, the 15), the gesture's flag, its index and the hands' states on every frame, no gap, the head light's eight gestures too. **The take to 28.88 s** (before the first walk; kept as `r45`): the movement state, the stance, both stamina pools and the turn limit on every frame, no gap; the eye within 1.9 cm on the first frames only. Its gaps: the take begins in a walk's glide (1.17 m/s on its first frame, the body still blending out of the walk for 8 frames), which leaves a steady 7.4 cm on the distance; the hands' and the weapon's turn (0.4 degrees on the mean, 1.2 at most), as in every recording made by hand (`r22`, `r39`, `r40`); the camera's own turn as the inventory opens while getting up (0.44 degrees at most, ours none). **Found and fixed**: (1) the sound went with the key and the menu, never with what is worn (`trp_sim::visor::SwitchSounds`, heard each frame in the app's `step_sound` after the inventory's moves); (2) the replay's character of a recording that lists no skills had an untrained one's weight limits, so its overweight was 0.1946 where the game's was 0.0741 and going down and getting up ran at 0.934 where the game's `TransitionMultiplier` read 0.975, five frames slow; now the Strength its top speed gives (40, exactly) makes them, the recorded inertia and overweight to the fourth place. **Not compared**: the walking spans (28.9 to 59.5 s, the walk's keys not recorded: there the movement is the replay's standing still, the switches, gestures and sounds compared all the same); the head light's own state and sound (the replay runs no devices where a recording lists none); the face shield's hinge and the mask (not recorded by 0.10.0); night vision's picture (not built: TRP's choice).

**Not built**: the look of it (the helmet's hinge turned, the face shield drawn at all: a helmet's own parts are not drawn; the mask over the first person's view; night and thermal vision); the switch's state from a loadout (`upd.Togglable`); the voice muffled behind a face shield; the head's light and the helmet's rail. (Since built: the hinge, 0.50.0, and a shield's own hinge, 0.118.0, body animator spec; the mask, 0.118.0, below; the icon following the parts and the switch, 0.118.0, icons spec. Night and thermal vision are left out by choice.)

### The face shield's mask (`VisorEffect`, `Hidden/VisorEffect`, `Hidden/FastBlur`, `PlayerCameraController.OnFaceShieldComponentChanged`, `Player.CreateSlotObservers`, the camera's `HysteresisFilter`; read 2026-10-24)

Read from the install's camera (`Cam2` in `resources.assets`: its `VisorEffect`, 35224, and `HysteresisFilter`, 35076; `trp_data::visor::read`), the shaders' DirectX 11 programs (`fxc /dumpbin`) and their parsed forms, the pictures, and the decompiled code that drives them; the reading's notes in our words: `local\visor_shader_s126.md`.

**Which shield.** The camera hears two slots (`SlotObserver`): the headwear's thing's first face shield, itself first and each part before the parts fitted to it, and the face cover's. Each is heard when the thing in its slot changes, and when that shield's switch or its hits change. As the camera is made both are heard, the head's first; after that the head's is handed on only while the face cover has none, and the face cover's only while the head has none. Handed on (`OnFaceShieldComponentChanged`): a shield sets the mask's kind and draws its hits' cracks; with or without one, the camera's filter is set going down where there is a shield and it is down (no switch, or its switch on), up otherwise, and at once the first time (`_onFaceShieldChangedIsLive`). None keeps the last kind.

**The fade.** The filter's value is the mask's intensity (`CameraManager`: `VisorEffect.SetIntensity`), the effect drawn while above nothing and only copying the frame below 0.01. The camera's curves: down, nothing until 0.6017 s and then straight to 1 by 0.8017 s; up, held at 1 while the clock stands at 0.2 s or more (0.6 s) and then down to 0 over 0.2 s. The hands' gesture takes 1.342 s; the mask comes and goes with the hand at the visor.

**The kind** (the item's `FaceShieldMask`; `SetMask`). Every kind starts from the camera's pictures (`SetDefault`: the mask `VisorMask`, the scratches `ScreenScratches_gud`, the blur's mask `ScreenScratches_blur_psd`, the distortion's `ScreenScratches_distort_psd`, the cracks' picture). **None** (the ZSh-1-2M's, the FAST's and most others): no frame (the shader's white), the scratches, blur and distortion. **Wide** (the Altyn's, the Rys-T's, the three welding masks): the frame `VisorMask`, a soft rounded window, besides. **Narrow** (the Maska-1SCh's two): every other picture cleared (the shader's black: no scratches, blur or distortion), the frame `VisorMask_maska_sch`, a soft slit.

**Where it is drawn**: on the main camera, after pain's blur and before the flash, as the camera's components lie; in first person only (the hideout shows it only in its first person, `HideoutPlayerOwner`: `VisorVisibility = FirstPersonMode`; a raid has no other).

**The mask's place** (each pass): the screen's place `s` (from the lower left) turned by the rotation about the middle, zoomed by `0.9 − |hit zoom|` (`0.9` as the pictures are drawn; the distortion's by `1`), the swing added, and lowered by `1 − intensity`: `m = 0.5 + (0.9 − |H|) R(s − 0.5) + swing − (0, 1 − I)`. A pixel is behind the shield where `m.y > 0`: the shield comes down from the top as the intensity grows. Its lower edge fades where `m.y < 0.1`, by `m.y / edge fade` at most 1 (`EdgeFadeCurve` of the intensity: about 0.1 as it moves, 0 down, where the edge is hard). Every pass's result `X` is laid on the frame `C` as `C + behind · fade · (X − C)`.

**The four passes** (each replacing the frame; figures `Cam2`'s): 0, the scratches laid over the frame toward their colour times `0.6` (`MinLightValue`, the light not counted: `IsAffectedByLight` off) by their alpha times `0.45`, both times `1 − flash` (`_BlindnessCoef`), and the cracks' picture toward a light grey by its own value; 1, the frame moved by the cracks' picture times `0.0075`; 2, the frame moved by the distortion picture's red and green times `0.0075` times twice the squared distance from the middle; then the frame blurred (`Hidden/FastBlur`: four taps into half its size, then twice a seven-tap blur up and down and across, `0.75` and then `1.75` half-size texels apart, the alpha the middle tap's) and 3, the blur laid over the frame by the blur mask's alpha (white at the sides) and the whole multiplied by the frame picture's alpha read at the mask's place turned top to bottom (its repeat wrap).

**The cracks** (read 2026-11-08: `PlayerCameraController.OnFaceShieldComponentChanged`, `VisorEffect.BreakCompletely`, `SetMask`, `DrawScratches`, `Draw`, `GetOrCreateGlassRenderTexture`, the material `GlassDamageMaterial` and its shader `Custom/Glass Scratch`; what marks a shield: body hits spec, "A face shield's marks"). At every change handed on with a shield (put on, switched, or its hits changed), in this order: where its durability is nought (under 0.001), ten cracks along the edges are drawn into the cracks' picture (`BreakCompletely`); the mask is set; and the picture is drawn anew (`DrawScratches`): **cleared to nothing**, which wipes the edge cracks just drawn (so a worn-out shield shows its hits' cracks and nothing more: read from the code's order, never seen), then the engine's random numbers seeded with the shield's seed, and for each of its hits, at most 20, four numbers drawn in turn: x and y each from −1 to 1, a turn from 0 to 360 (handed over as degrees, taken by the shader as radians: in effect any angle), and a size k from 1.6 to 2.6. Each makes a quad from `(x − k/a, −y − k)` to `(x + k/a, −y + k)` (`a` the camera's aspect as it is drawn), the crack picture's (0, 0) at the first corner and (1, 1) at the second, drawn with no matrix: a corner `p` lies at `((p.x + 0.09) / 1.09, (p.y + 0.09) / 1.09)` of the target, the picture is read at `t = 2 R(uv − 0.4) + 0.4` (`R` the turn by the angle, as the mask's own; the picture's star, near its (0.35, 0.44), lands near the quad's middle) and its alpha (`glassshield_damage2`, 1024 square, clamped at its edges, white with the cracks in its alpha) is added into the target's one channel. **The target**: one channel, the display's size, no levels. With no shield nothing is drawn (the last cracks stay, unseen while nothing shows). The mask reads the target's value at its place `m`: pass 0 lays the frame toward a light grey (`0.2 C + 0.8 × 0.6`) by that value (a flash takes it away, as the scratches), pass 1 moves the frame by it times `0.0075`; a narrow mask clears it (no cracks with the Maska-1SCh's or Killa's). Only the wearer sees them, in first person; the glass seen from outside has none (its shader takes no such picture). The hit's own sound on one's own visor (`EffectsController.OnPlayerDamaged` → `PlaySpecialSound`: any damage taken whose material is `GlassVisor`, `Helmet` or `HelmetRicochet` plays that material's effect's first-person bank, unplaced, on the voice's mixer) is heard only by a character that is hit.

**The swing** (`SetSwing`, while the mask is drawn): none while the walk's step frequency is under 0.01 (mounted, `ProceduralWeaponAnimation.VisorStepFrequency`); standing (a speed across the ground under 0.001) the swing and the turn go back toward nothing by ten times the frame's time and the clock to 0; moving, the clock runs at the walk's step frequency and the swing is the curves `DX`, `DY` at it (within ±0.0009 and +0.0076 of the picture), the turn `WalkRotation` (±0.008 rad), each repeating. A hit's swing and zoom (`HitSwing`) are never started in the code. **The flash** (`_BlindnessCoef`): its strength while it runs (`GrenadeFlashScreenEffect`: the square root of the strongest stun), kept after it (nothing puts it back as it stops).

**Not read, or not settled**: what the small white square the camera shows while the mask is drawn is for (`VisorPoint`, 10 cm ahead and 11 cm right of the eye, 0.2 mm: nothing reads it); the cracks' picture's orientation in the engine's render target; that a shield's complete break (`BreakCompletely`) is cleared at once by the hits' drawing after it, as the code's order has it.

**Built** (2026-10-24, 0.118.0; `trp_data::visor`, `trp_sim::visor`, `Kind::face_shield_mask`, the app's `visor_view`, `visor.wgsl`, a split of `contusion_view` at the flash; the switch `FACE SHIELD MASK`, a part of `VISUAL EFFECTS`, TRP's choice): the two slots heard as the game's, the filter, the three kinds, passes 0, 2, the blur and 3 on the frame's gamma-encoded numbers (`gamma_ui`, as the camera's other effects), the swing by the walk's step frequency (`Hands::step_frequency`) and the body's speed across the ground, the flash's strength kept (`Blindness`). The frame's own alpha is kept by each pass (the game's passes blend and mask it with the colour: nothing shows it; a screenshot would). `PROVISIONAL`: the swing's curves taken as repeating (their stored wrap read by what is known of the engine's numbering, checked once); the pictures sampled at their full size. **Tested** (`the_face_shield_s_mask_is_read`: the figures as read by hand, the three kinds' pictures, the filter's curve; `the_mask_fades_as_the_shield_moves`, `the_head_s_shield_and_the_face_s`, `the_mask_swings_with_the_walk`). **Seen** (first person, `local\shots\visor_*_s126.png`): the ZSh-1-2M down after `N` (`visor_zsh_beside_s126.png` beside it up: a grey haze of smudges, the sides blurred, no frame); the Altyn's frame (black about 7 % at the sides and a fifth at top and bottom, its edge soft: as the read figures put it); the Maska's slit; the Altyn's half-way down 0.7 s after `N`, the window's lower edge at the middle of the screen. **Compared** (2026-11-01, 0.181.1; `courses8`'s two visor recordings, above, which write the camera's `VisorEffect` and its filter every frame): the mask's intensity and the filter's clock, value and way on every frame of both, to the fourth decimal, no gap: from the menu (the screen shut half-way through the fade in the second), by the key at rest, in blind fire, from outside, in a reload, the ten quick presses (the clock never past 0.15 s, so never drawn), the visor fitted up (nothing moves) and taken off up. **Found and fixed**: the game's filter moves before a switch made in its frame: on the switch's frame its clock has not moved, and a switch turned back 13 frames later finds it moved those 13 frames toward the old way; the app moved it after hearing the shields, and heard them before or after the key's system as Bevy's order of two unordered systems had it (so the mask came a frame early, or right). Now the filter is moved first (`Visor::update`), then the shields heard (`observe`), then the swing drawn (`frame`, as the game's `SetSwing` at render), after everything that can flip a switch in the frame. **Not compared**: the mask's picture (the recordings' pictures were not set beside a capture); the swing (no recording writes it); seen from outside the game's effect stays enabled with its filter running (the recorder's view from outside is not the game's: the hideout's rule, first person only, is kept). **Built: the cracks** (`trp_sim::visor::{Cracks, CrackQuad, crack_quads}`, `Shield`'s hits and seed, `Visor::cracks`; `trp_data::visor::read`'s cracks' material and picture; the app's `visor_view` and `visor_cracks.wgsl`, passes 0 and 1 in `visor.wgsl`; the switch `FACE SHIELD CRACKS`, which needs `VISUAL EFFECTS`; what marks a shield: body hits spec, "A face shield's marks"): drawn anew at every change handed on with a shield, the four draws of Unity's numbers in their order by the shield's seed, each quad where the game puts it and the crack picture turned and read as its shader does, added into a one-channel picture cleared first; `BreakCompletely` draws nothing (its quads wiped by the clearing that follows, as read); pass 0's grey and pass 1's move by the picture's value at the mask's place; none with a narrow mask. `PROVISIONAL`: the picture of the view's size (the game's is the display's: only the cracks' size in pixels differs), drawn again from the same quads when the view's size changes; read by the mask with a repeating wrap (the engine's render target's own not read); the quads drawn with no face culled (the game culls back faces, and its quads, which are seen, must pass by the engine's flip for a target: not measured). **Tested**: `the_cracks_quads_follow_the_seed` (the draws' order and ranges, the aspect), `a_mark_draws_the_cracks_anew` (a mark heard by the observers' rule, at most 20, none keeps the last), `the_face_shield_s_mask_is_read` (the material's picture `glassshield_damage2`, 1024 square, clamped; read only when asked). **Seen** (first person, the ZSh-1-2M lowered, `--shield-hits`, against the open range: `local\shots\cracks_*_s239.png`, side by side `cracks_strip_s239.png`): none at 0; three faint lines at 3 (for that seed most of the stars' middles fall off the screen); at 8 star breaks over the sky's right and the weapon, the frame moved along them; at 20 a web over the whole view, grey where middles overlap; with the switch off, at 8, as at 0. **Not compared**: no recording has a cracked shield, and the seed is in none: the cracks' places follow the rule as read, never set beside the game's picture; the game's target's orientation (Direct3D's) taken by the engine's convention.

**Not built**: mounting's frozen swing (mounting is not built); the white square; the hit's own sound on one's own visor (nothing hits the character on the range; the effects' first-person banks, `SoundFP`, are read by nothing of ours, so its clip was not identified from the install).

### Sent to the other side (`QuickFindAppropriatePlace`, the order `MoveToAnotherSide`)

**What is looked in**, in order (`ItemUiContext`):

1. If a container's window is the priority one and the thing does not lie in that container: that container, and nothing else.
2. Else, if something is open beside the equipment and the thing does not lie in it: what is open, and after it the equipment if the thing is the character's.
3. Else the equipment.
4. And last, in every case, what the thing lies in now (a weapon and its parts counting as one thing: from a part, what holds the weapon).

**The equipment stands for four of the things worn**, in an order that goes by the kind of thing (`GetPrioritizedContainersForLoot`):

| The thing is | Looked in, in order |
|---|---|
| a round or a magazine | vest, pockets, backpack, secured container |
| money | secured container, backpack, vest, pockets |
| a grenade | pockets, vest, backpack, secured container |
| anything else | backpack, vest, pockets, secured container |

Anything else that is looked in offers its own slots and then its own grids.

**Each thing looked in is tried by itself, in that order** (the four worn count as one):

1. A stack of the thing's kind among what lies directly in it, with room for one more at least, takes the thing: all of it if there is room, else as much as there is room for, never the last one, and the rest stays where it was.
2. Else the thing goes to the first place found: a slot that takes it, or the first free room in a grid, rows from the top. **The search goes by the grid as it stands**, so the cells the thing lies on now are taken: a thing sent with nowhere else to go moves to the next free place in its own container.
3. A move that the first place found refuses ends the try.

The first try that works is the move. So a thing in the crate goes to the character (a magazine to the vest), a thing on the character goes to the crate, with nothing open a thing on the character goes to the first *other* place the character's order has for it, and with nothing worn that could hold it, it hops within the crate. Not read: quest items (they have a grid of their own) and pinned or locked things.

### Put on (`ItemUiContext.EquipItem`, `FindSlotToPickUp`)

Offered for equipment, knives, secured containers, what is searched, vests and weapons, and not for a thing that is in one of the equipment's slots already ("it is on already"). The slots tried go by the kind of thing, the kinds asked in this order:

| The thing is | Slots tried, in order |
|---|---|
| body armour | the armour's |
| glasses | the eyewear's |
| headwear, a face cover, a headset, a backpack, a vest, an armband | its own |
| a knife | the sheath |
| a revolver | the holster, the sling, the back |
| a pistol | the holster |
| any other weapon | the sling, the back, the holster |
| a secured container | its own |

The first that takes it by its own rules ("What a slot accepts") is the move; none, and the game says there is no free slot. (Not told apart here: a flare gun, which goes to the holster, and what goes only into the pockets' special slots.)

**A thing taken up off the ground goes the same way first** (`PickUp`): onto the slot of its kind if one is free; else, with the four worn taken as one, onto a stack of its kind or into the first room, in the order of the table above. The game offers to take a thing only if that would find it a place. (This replaces the order written here on 2026-10-11, which was marked PROVISIONAL: a slot that takes it, then vest, pockets, backpack, secured container.)

### A container's window, and the priority one (`GridWindow`, `PriorityWindowMode`)

A double click on a container opens a window with its grids, whose things are moved like any others; a double click on anything else opens a window that says what it is. With the game's setting `PriorityWindowMode` at `Auto`, as the install has it, a container's window becomes the priority one when it opens and whenever it is picked, and stops being it when it shuts. All windows shut with the screen.

**The window as the game draws it** (`GridWindow`, `ItemUiContext.OpenItem` and `OpenWindow`, `ContainedGridsView`, `Window`, `UIDragComponent`, `RectTransformExtensions.CorrectPositionResolution`; read 2026-10-25, built 0.124.0). Its prefab is the item context's (`_gridWindowTemplate`, a window whose top has the `GridWindow` script):
- **Its size**: a column padded 5 left, 4 right, 26 at the top and 4 at the bottom round the container's grids, as large as they ask and at least 128 by 64 units. The grids are the container's own layout where it has one (`Rig Layouts`, as the GEAR tab draws a rig's), else one row of them 6 apart, each 63 units a cell and one more. A Berkut's window is 261 by 347; a Triton's 284 by 289. No slot is shown, and nothing scrolls.
- **Its look**: a black ground, a shadow 25 out on every side and a dark border 5 pixels out, as the prefab has them. Its caption's bar lies 2 in from the top, left and right, 17 high: the search icon, the container's short name as the locale writes it (not in capitals, size 14, cut with "…" where the bar is too narrow), and the red cross 27 by 17 at the right end. A priority window has its icon gold (0.906, 0.788, 0.380) and its bar's grey at 0.4 alpha; the rest white and 0.298. In a raid the priority toggle, the tag, the sort and the search buttons are off.
- **Where it opens**: alone, in the middle of the screen; else 40 units right of and below the window opened last, kept on the screen. Opened again, a window is only brought to the top: it does not become the priority one again. **As its size changes** after (a searchable thing's search over: its grids in place of the unsearched panel) it keeps its middle, its pivot, moved back onto the screen where it would leave it and its top left corner put on a whole pixel, halves to even (`OnRectTransformDimensionsChange`: `CorrectPosition`, `CorrectPositionResolution`); its first place, laid out before it is put in the middle, is not rounded. `courses10`'s `backpack-search` (an empty Terraframe's window, frame 562, the search's end): the ground from 1007.33,616.67 545.34 by 206.66 to 1013,488 534.67 by 464, its middle at 1280,720 and 1280.33,720. The app's (`inventory_view::fit_windows`; the same bag laid, taken on the back and opened, `--lay 674da107c512807d1a0e7436`): 1007.33,616.67 545.34 by 206.66, then 1013,488 534.67 by 464 on the search's last frame, its caption 1039.33,620.39 and 1045,491.73, its grids' borders at 1017.33,516.33 and 1363.87,516.33, as the take's (before, its top left corner kept: 1007.33,616.67, the window down to 1080.67).
- **Picked** (brought to the top and, a container's, made the priority one) by a click on it or a drag begun on it, not by the press, and not where the press is on a thing in it (a thing takes its own click and drag). Its caption's bar moves it once a drag begins, kept on the screen at every move (a left edge off the screen moved right, else a right edge left; a bottom edge off moved up, else a top edge down), at whole pixels.
- **Shut** by its cross or by Escape (the top one, a press each). As any window shuts, the top-most container's window left is picked, as a click would. A container's window also shuts when its container, or anything that held it as the window opened, is moved anywhere, even within the inventory.

### The mouse's pointer (`CursorSwitcher`, `ECursorType`; read 2026-10-24, built 0.124.0)

The game draws the system's own pointer with a picture of its own (Unity's `Cursor.SetCursor`), never one drawn by its screens, at the picture's own size in pixels whatever the window's size. Each kind is a texture of the build's Resources with its hotspot in pixels from the top left: `cursor_idle` and `cursor_over` (11, 11), the four `cursor_stretch_*` (16, 16), `cursor_hand` (17, 10), `cursor_set_pin` and `cursor_set_lock` (7, 10). Over a button of the game's it is `cursor_over` (asked by the buttons' scripts as the mouse enters: `ButtonFeedback`, `DefaultUIButton` and the others the app lists in `cursor::HOVER_SCRIPTS`), else `cursor_idle`; the picture changes only when the kind does. A click on a button of the `ButtonFeedback` kind puts it back to `cursor_idle` until the mouse leaves the button and comes back; an `AnimatedToggle` alone does not. Over a stretching place of the inspect window (`StretchArea`, built 0.124.1) it is the stretch pointer of its sides: the top and bottom edges `cursor_stretch_vertical`, the left and right ones `cursor_stretch_horizontal`, the top left and bottom right corners `cursor_stretch_corner`, the other two `cursor_stretch_corner_2`; pressed there, that pointer is kept until the button is let go, wherever the mouse goes. Not built: the hand over a text's link (`ClickableUIText`; no text the app draws has one) and the stash's pin and lock pointers (no stash). Read from code only: a system pointer is not in a screenshot. **Shown and hidden two frames after a screen's key** (`InputManager.Update`, `DispatchInput`, `InputNode.TranslateInput`, `ClientApplicationInitOperation.CursorVisibilityChangedHandler`; read 2026-11-02): each frame the pointer is first set as the last frame's walk of the input tree asked (`ShouldLockCursor`: a screen shows it, playing locks it), then the frame's keys are handed down; a screen opened by a key joins the tree during that walk, so the next frame's walk is the first to ask for it, and the frame after sets it; a screen shut by its key still asks for the pointer as it handles the key. Locked, it stays where it was held, the window's middle. **Compared with** `courses10`'s `inventory-open-close`: shown and hidden two frames after each of the eight presses (93 → 95, 439 → 441, ..., 1268 → 1270, 1272 → 1274). Built (`cursor::hold`, which runs before the frame's keys and so already a frame behind, keeps the screens' state a frame more; the debug panel's own pointer is not delayed): not compared, a run nobody is at the keys of leaves the pointer alone, so `--dump-ui` writes it shown throughout.

### Part of a stack (`ExecutePossibleAction`, `SplitMax`, `TransferMax`, `SplitDialog`)

**With Ctrl held as a thing is let go, only part of a stack goes**, and a thing that is no stack goes nowhere:

- onto a free cell or an empty slot: so many are split off into it, all but one at the most, and into a slot one;
- onto a stack of its kind: so many are handed over, as many as that has room for and never the last one.

Where more than one may go, **a window asks how many**: headed "Split" or "Transfer", at the mouse, with a slider from one to the most, a field the figure can be typed into, a button that goes through with it and one that drops it; Enter goes through with it and Escape drops it. It comes up at half the most and one (integer half: 30 of 59). The slider stops at notches: one every twenty-fourth of the most, rounded up, the first at one, and the most at the end (for 59: 1, 3, 6 and on to 57, then 59); a typed figure may be any from one to the most. Where only one may go, it goes with no window.

Without Ctrl a stack let go on a stack of its kind merges if the whole fits, and else fills the other up ("Dropping one thing on another").

### The window that asks how many, as the game draws it (`SplitDialog`, `StepSlider`, `IntSlider`, `Slider`, `TMP_InputField`, `UIDragComponent`; read and built 2026-10-31)

**One window for a stack and for food or drink**: the item context makes it once from its `_splitDialogTemplate` (`sharedassets49.assets`; `probe splitdialog`, `local\splitdialog_s162.txt`) and shows it with one of its two sliders: the stepped one for part of a stack, headed by the install's `Split` or `Transfer` as the words have them, and the whole-number one for a share of food or drink, headed by `Use` in capitals (`ShowSplitMenuDialog`; health spec, "Built: the double click"). Its button says the install's `ACCEPT`.

- **Where it opens.** The window (`_window`, 418.1 by 200 units) has its middle as its pivot, and that is put at the mouse: where the stack was let go, or where the food was clicked. Then it is moved in to the screen where it would leave it (the left edge before the right, the bottom before the top) and its top left corner is put on a whole pixel of the screen, halves to even (`CorrectPositionResolution`). A press on its caption bar moved past the drag threshold moves it, held where the mouse was on it at that moment, kept on the screen the same way (`UIDragComponent`).
- **The screen behind it is darkened and shut off**: the window's own ground, as large as the screen, black at 0.422, takes every press that is not on the window and does nothing with it.
