- **What it is made of**, from its top left: a black ground; the caption bar 2 units in from the top and the sides, 17 high, (0.329, 0.345, 0.357) at 0.302, the title in it from 5 in (14 points, white); the cross, 27 by 17 at the bar's right end; a frame round the whole, sliced, 5 screen pixels out whatever the scale (`PixelPerfectSpriteScaler`), which alone of its pictures takes no press; across the middle a panel 10 in from each side and 50 high, (0.302, 0.263, 0.263) at 0.392; the slider in it, 300 by 20, 20 in and 8 down; the field 16 right of the slider; the button 133 by 18, 20 in from the bottom right corner, (0.329, 0.345, 0.357) at 0.302 on its slanted sprite, its word 11 points.
- **The slider has no fill.** Its row (`_notchContainer`, its `Background`, a row that sizes and stretches what is in it) is 3 units high a quarter of the way up, drawn as a line (0.329, 0.345, 0.357) at 0.403 the whole width, with its own last notch, 2 wide, at its right end. The script copies a notch into the row for every place but the last: the row gives each the same share of the width, and each draws a white mark 2 units wide at its own left edge, so the marks lie every 300 over their number units, the first at the start. The whole-number slider copies the dim template (white at 0.251) for the places before the first the knob may stand on (`IntSlider.Show`). Laid out unrounded, a mark covers 2 or 3 pixels by where it falls (60 places at 1440 lines: 3 and 2 by turns).
- **The handle** (18 by 18.2, a caret on it) hangs under the row, its middle at the value's share of the slider's width (`Slider.UpdateVisuals`), the slider counting from its least to its most.
- **Where the stepped slider's knob stands.** Slid, at the stop slid to. For a figure it comes up at or that is typed, the slider is given the place of the nearest stop counted from nought (the later of two as near, `GetIndexOfClosest`), while it counts from one: so the knob stands a stop short of the figure's own, the field saying the figure (30 of 59: the knob on 27's mark, 0.45 of the way; a capture of the game of 2026-10-31). At the most, it stands at the end; at one, at the start.
- **The field** (55.9 by 24.2, black) says the figure centred, (0.765, 0.773, 0.698), its size fitted (`enableAutoSizing`, 5 to 18 points, its own 16.7): TextMeshPro tries sizes by halving the way to its least while the words overflow the field and the way back up while they fit, in twentieths of a point, so a figure of up to four places is at 18. It has the keys as the window opens with all it says selected, the selection (0.659, 0.808, 1) at 0.753 drawn under the figure from its first letter's start to its last's advance, the line's ascender to its descender (`m_OnFocusSelectAll`, `GenerateHightlight`). A key typed takes the selection's place; after that a caret one unit wide in the text's colour stands after the last figure, shown for the first half of each blink, 0.85 blinks a second (`GenerateCaret`, `CaretBlink`); never drawn under a pixel wide (TRP's own, since 0.256.0: `split_view::caret_width`). A press anywhere but on the field takes the keys away (nothing is drawn in it then, and figures typed go nowhere); a press on it gives them back with all selected.
- **What a figure typed does.** The stepped slider's field emptied reads as its least, 1, and says it, unless the figure was 1 already, when it says nothing; a figure past the most is the most, and nought is one. The whole-number one's field emptied says nothing and keeps its figure; a figure typed is kept to 1 and what is left.
- **The mouse on the slider.** Only its pictures take a press: the row, its marks and the handle, not the rest of its 20 units. A press on the row puts the knob there at once; a press on the handle holds it where it was taken (`Slider.OnPointerDown`), and either follows the mouse from then on with no drag threshold (`OnInitializePotentialDrag`); the value is the nearest whole number, halves to even. A value the slider has already changes nothing (`Slider.Set` passes over it, so the stepped slider is not told): with the knob a stop short of the figure, a press on the handle, and the mouse moved less than half a stop, leave the figure as it was, and the first value reached is the stop before the knob's (30 of 59: "30" kept from the press until the mouse had gone 11 px left, then 24, not 27; `courses10`'s `how-many`, frames 458 to 476). The cross and the button act when the button is let go over them as they were pressed (`OnPointerClick`); Enter goes through with it and Escape drops it, as before.
- **The tints.** The cross, the button and the slider's handle are tinted by their state, as the engine's controls are by default: 0.961 under the mouse or chosen (the slider after a press on it, until a press elsewhere), 0.784 held, each change taking 0.1 s.
- **Its sound.** The stack's drop sounds as it is let go and the window opens: the drag's end plays the operation's sound whatever comes of it, and the window's operation, put off, has no place yet, so it is the kind's drop (`GridView.AcceptItem`, `SlotView.AcceptItem`: `PlayOperationSound`). Nothing sounds when the window goes through (`SplitResult.ExecuteWithNewCount`, the transfer through the item controller), is dropped, or, for food or drink, is dropped (`courses10`'s `how-many`: the drop on the release at frames 238 and 1171, nothing at `ACCEPT`, Enter or the cross).

**Built** (`TARKOV UI`): `split_view` (`trp_data::screen::split_look`, `SplitLook::laid_out`, `ScenePanel::copy_into`, `trp_data::tmp_text::auto_size`), the window's state in `inventory_view::Asking` (its knob, its field's keys and selection) and its presses in `ask`; with `TARKOV UI` off the app's own window, as it was, its bar at the same knob. **Compared with** three captures of the game at 2560x1440 (`local\game_split_s162.png`, `game_use60_s162.png`, `game_use14_s162.png`), each set beside the app's at the same corner, pixel by pixel (`local\windiff_s162.ps1`): the corner (1084, 317) and (840, 759) the same; the frame's lines, the ground, the caption bar, the panel, the row, every mark (21 for 59; 60 with 46 dim for 14 of 60), the handle and the selection on the same pixels; the screen behind darkened to 0.576 (the game's 0.578). What differs: the titles and `ACCEPT` by up to 99 levels on their strokes, the app placing a TextMeshPro text's picture on whole pixels and the game not (about a third of a pixel: as on every screen); the frame's one-pixel lines up to 11 levels and the marks' edges up to 21, the game's capture spreading about a twentieth of a pixel's light into the next pixel to the right, which the app's does not (not read). Not compared: the window at another size with the game (the app's at 2560x1366 checked against the layout's figures alone: corner (1126, 204), marks on the pixels the units give), the tints, the caret and its blink, the caption's drag, and the field without the keys (the game's capture of 14 has it so; the app's was taken as it opens). **Compared with `courses10`'s `how-many`** (2026-11-02; the interface trace beside the app's `--dump-ui`, `local\ui_dump_how-many_s185.jsonl`, the command in `workbench.md`): two splits by Ctrl and a drag (the slider, `ACCEPT`; the slider, the field clicked, "5" and "0" on the keypad, Enter) and a share of water by the menu's `USE`, dropped by the cross. Every box of the first two windows on the game's pixels (corners (829, 497) and (747, 499), the middle at the mouse), the third's on the same pixels from its corner (the window's middle at the click on `USE`, as the game's `Input.mousePosition`; the app's water lies elsewhere); every figure and the handle's place the game's (30, 24 at 1017, 3 at 877, 51 at 1197; 26 at 963.24, 33, "5" at 798.53, "50" at 1175; 60 at the end, 15); the windows opened and shut, and the splits made, on the game's frames; the drop's sound on the release that opens the window, nothing after (found with it: the app sounded the drop as the window went through, and set the stop at the knob's value on the press on the handle, 27 for 30: both fixed). The selection on "30" and the caret after "5" set beside the game's pictures (frames 271 and 1526) at four times: alike. Not compared: the tints (the recorder writes no `CanvasRenderer` colour), a mouse path through every stop (the app's script jumps).

### The keys (`Control.ini`, `InputManager`, `PlayerInputTranslator`)

The game's keys are bindings by name (`EGameKey`), each with one or two variants of keys held together and a way of being pressed; the input manager turns each into a command. As this install has them (read 2026-10-12; the app reads them at its start):

| Binding | Key | Pressed | What it does |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Inventory` | Tab | going down | opens and shuts the screen |
| `Interact` | F | held | works what is looked at |
| `ThrowItem` | Delete | going down | with the screen open: discards the thing under the mouse (in a raid at once, with no question) |
| `SecondaryWeapon` | 1 | going down | takes the holster's weapon in hand |
| `PrimaryWeaponFirst` | **2** | going down | takes the **sling's** weapon in hand |
| `PrimaryWeaponSecond` | **3** | going down | takes the **back's** weapon in hand |
| `Knife` | U in this install | going down | the sheath's |
| `Slot4` to `Slot9`, `Slot0` | 4 to 9, 0 | **coming up** | the quick-use keys |
| `ReloadWeapon` | R | **coming up** | reloads (a double press, `QuickReloadWeapon`, is the fast reload) |
| `CheckAmmo` | **T with Left Alt** | going down | looks at the magazine |

(The app had the sling's weapon on `3` "as remembered" until 2026-10-12: wrong by the install. Its reload was on the key going down and its look at the magazine on `T` alone until that night, when every key the character takes became the install's binding, heard as the game's input manager hears it: `settings.md`.)

**A weapon slot's key** (`SetSlotItem`): nothing if the slot is empty. Else the thing is taken in hand by a process of the player's: nothing if it is the thing in hand already; else what is in hand is put away by its own operation, and when it has gone the active slot is set and the new thing is drawn. A second key pressed while the first thing is still going takes the place of what was to come. Refused while the weapon in hand is in something that cannot be cut short, and while the inventory is moving a thing into or out of the hands (not built: the app refuses nothing here).

**The weapon in hand moved out of its slot** (`OutProcess`, `SetControllerInsteadRemovedOne`): it is put away, and the hands take the first other thing in the slots' order (sling, back, holster, sheath) or are empty. Moved from the sling to the back it therefore leaves the hands and hangs there until the back's key; moved back into the slot the hands last drew from, it is taken in hand at once ("The weapon in hand").

### Escape (`InventoryScreen.TranslateCommand`, `ItemUiContext.TranslateCommand`, `Window.TranslateCommand`, `InputNodeAbstract.TranslateInput`; read and built 2026-10-13)

Escape shuts the inventory screen, as the inventory's own key does, but **one thing a press**, by the order the game's tree of screens hands a key down: the windows over the screen are asked first, the one opened or picked last before the rest, and a window that has the key shuts and keeps it; with no window open the screen's own items are asked, where a menu on an item shuts and then the window that asks how many of a stack; and only with none of those up does the screen itself shut. (The window that asks how many is no window of the tree: with it up over an open container's window, Escape shuts the container's window first.)

**Built:** `inventory_view::toggle` (the screen), `windows` (the one on top), `ask` (the window that asks, when no other is open). The options tab is the app's own: while a key is being chosen there, or a way of being pressed asked for, Escape is that tab's (it keeps what stood) and the screen stays. **The menu on an item** (built 2026-11-02, `toggle`): shut by Escape, its menu of lines with it, the key kept from the rest, no sound; with a window open, the window has the key (the windows are `ItemUiContext`'s own nodes, handed the key before it, `InputNode.TranslateInput`; `ItemUiContext` is the last of `UIInputRoot`'s, `UIInputRoot.Add`, so before the screen). Compared with `courses10`'s `inspect-window` (frames 925 and 1613: both menus gone on the key's frame, the screen kept, no sound; the app shut the screen until then). **Not built:** Escape with the screen shut, which in the game opens its menu. **Compared with the game: nothing**; run by captures (a window open: the first press shuts the window, the second the screen; a key being chosen: the screen stays).

### The quick-use keys and their bar (`FastAccess`, `BindResult`, `InventoryScreenQuickAccessPanel`)

- **What a key is bound to.** Four keys go with slots and are never bound by hand: the sheath's, the holster's (1), the sling's (2), the back's (3). Seven, 4 to 9 and 0, are bound to things.
- **What may be bound** (`IsAtBindablePlace`): a weapon, a grenade, a knife, medicine, food or drink, a range finder, a compass or a radio transmitter, whole, that lies directly in the vest or the pockets worn.
- **Binding:** with the screen open, the key let go with the mouse over a thing of the character's binds it. A thing bound to another key leaves that key; what the key had is unbound. A thing dragged onto one of the bar's views is bound to it, and one dragged off its view is unbound (not built: the bar is only shown).
- **Unbinding:** whenever a thing is moved or discarded, it is unbound if it no longer lies where it may be bound.
- **In play** (`SetQuickSlotItem`) the key takes what it is bound to in hand: a weapon is drawn like a slot's; medicine is applied if it can do anything to the body as it is; food and drink are taken. Refused while a weapon is being changed.
- **The bar** is the screen's `Quick Use Panel`: 854 units wide and 115 high, its lower edge 30 over the screen's and its middle 25.6 to the right of the screen's, with a row of views 64 square, 70 apart: the grenade key's, the sheath's, 1, 2, 3, and after a gap 4 to 9 and 0. The scene has the row laid out by a layout script, so the places read are those it was saved with.
- **A view's caption** (`QuickSlotView.SetItem`: the thing's short name in the install's words) is one line as wide as the view, 64 at size 11 (`probe quick`): what passes the width is cut where its text says, **the battle screen's bar with an ellipsis** (overflow 1: "Velociraptor .300 BLK" shows "Velocirapt…"), the inventory's bar bare (overflow 3), by the rule of the grid's captions (not drawn whole past the view).

### Built (2026-10-12)

- `trp-sim/src/inventory.rs`: `quick_find` with the two orders (`Order::OTHER_SIDE`, `PICK_UP`), `loot_order`, `slot_to_wear`, `equip`, `pick_up` by the game's order (it gives back what is left on the ground: the thing refused, or the rest of a stack), `can_pick_up`, `split_most`, `transfer_most`, `transfer_item`, the grid's search as the game makes it (`find_other_room`), and `Bound` (`bind`, `settle`, `bindable`). A thing's class is asked by the database's name for it (`is`), which is the game's own type name.
- `trp-data`: `Install::key_bindings` and `game_setting`; the quick-use panel beside the equipment panel (`GearPanel::quick`, `quick_slots`); `probe keys`.
- The app (`inventory_view`, `keys`):
  - **A click is told from a carry.** The button going down on a thing takes nothing yet; moved 5 pixels with the button down (the game's event system's drag threshold, read from the install since 0.183.0, 10 before: "With the mouse"), the thing is carried as before; let go where it went down, it is a click, and two within 0.3 s a double one.
  - **Ctrl and a click, Alt and a click, a double click** as above. A double click on food or medicine says that using it is not built.
  - **Windows:** a container's grids or what a thing is (its name in the database, its class, weight, cells, stack and description; with `TARKOV UI` on, the game's own window, "The window that shows a thing"), moved by the caption, shut by the cross, the one on top taking the mouse; the priority one has its caption lit. With `TARKOV UI` on a container's window is the game's own too ("A container's window, and the priority one"), and opens, is picked and shuts as the game's; with it off, the app's window opens a little below and right of the mouse.
  - **The window that asks how many**, with the game's stops and its starting figure; the slider is slid with the mouse, a figure typed, Enter or ACCEPT, Escape or the cross. **This replaces "splitting stays at half"**: asked for in the brief of 2026-10-12; the window comes up at half and one.
  - **With Ctrl held a thing that is no stack is refused**, as the game refuses it (it used to be moved).
  - **The quick-use bar** where the install's scene has it, shown only: the four slots' things and the seven keys' things, each with the install's own key for it. A quick-use key let go over a thing binds it; a bound thing has its key after its name.
  - **The keys are the install's** (the character's too, since that night: `settings.md`): the inventory's, the key that works what is looked at, the discard key, the three weapon slots' and the seven quick-use keys (`keys`; where the install has none, what this one had on 2026-10-12). **The sling's weapon is now on `2`, the back's on `3`.**
  - **The weapon in hand is the one in any of the slots a weapon is held from**, of the kind the app has built: a slot's key puts away what is in hand and draws what hangs there; the weapon in hand moved to another slot is put away and hangs there. One rifle is built, with one logic: a second of its kind would be drawn with the first one's rounds (`PROVISIONAL`).
  - In play a quick-use key bound to a weapon draws it; one bound to medicine, food or drink says that using it is not built.
  - A thing on the ground is offered only if a pick-up would find it a place.

**Checked** (2026-10-12): the model's tests (`a_thing_is_sent_to_the_other_side`, `a_thing_is_put_on_by_its_kind`, `part_of_a_stack_is_split_off_or_handed_over`, `things_are_bound_to_the_quick_use_keys`); against the install (`things_are_sent_put_on_and_bound_by_the_install_s_classes`: the stock rifle onto the sling, a second onto the back and a third nowhere, the vest and the backpack onto their slots, a magazine to the crate and back to the vest, a magazine put down and taken up into the vest, the medical kit bound in the pockets and not in the backpack, the rifle on the sling not bound); the screen's own logic without a window (`clicks_put_on_send_over_and_open`, `ctrl_lets_go_of_part_of_a_stack`, `the_slider_has_the_game_s_stops`). **In the app, by scripted clicks and key presses** (`docs/workbench.md` has the commands): Alt and a click on each of the crate's three things puts them on (3.49, 7.48, then 12.32 kg carried; `local\shots\s31_f.png`); the `4` key let go over the medical kit in the vest binds it (its name then ends in `[4]`, and the bar's view for `4` shows it); Ctrl and a click on a magazine in the vest sends it to the crate's first cell; a double click on the backpack opens its window; a stack of 60 let go on a cell of that window with Ctrl held brings up the window at 30 of 59 (`s31_c.png`), and Enter makes it 30 and 30 (`s31_d.png`, with the window moved by its caption). The slot keys, with the animator's states logged (`local\frames_s31.txt`): the rifle in hand dragged from the sling to the back goes `IN INVENTORY`, `IDLE TO OUT` (0.26 s after the hold is reached, when the blend into it is over), and the empty hands appear 0.8 s later; `3` then draws it (`OUT TO IDLE`, at rest 0.95 s on); `2`, with the sling empty, and `3` again, with that rifle in hand, do nothing. (Written before the recordings of the afternoon; what those showed is in "Compared with the running game".)

**PROVISIONAL or not built:** the menu of the right button and the middle button's actions; searching (examining built 0.178.0); using food, drink or medicine (there is no health system); grenades and the grenade key; the knife and its key; the bar's views taking the mouse; the selectors the bar opens; a weapon slot's key refused in an operation that cannot be cut short; the fast draw of the holster's weapon on a double press; what the game shows when a quick move is refused (a notice; here the line under the equipment says it).

## The weapon in hand (`Player.SetFirstAvailableItem`, `TrySetInHands`, `DetectActiveSlot`; read 2026-10-11)

- **At the start** a character takes in hand the first thing it can from these slots in order: the sling's weapon, the back's, the holster's, the scabbard's. With none, its hands are empty, which is a controller and a rig of its own ("Empty hands").
- **The active slot** is the equipment slot the thing now in hand was drawn from. It is set whenever something is taken in hand, and stays set when that slot is emptied.
- **When something is moved into the active slot it is taken in hand at once.** Into any other slot it is not: it hangs there until its key is pressed (the keys for the sling's weapon, the back's and the holster's: `2`, `3` and `1` in the install, read since 2026-10-12; "Clicks, keys and windows", "The keys"). So a character that began with empty hands and puts a rifle on the sling still has empty hands until the key.
- The weapon in hand taken out of its slot leaves the hands for the next thing by the order above; here, empty.
- **From the empty hands, within the key's or the click's own call** (read 2026-11-08; the app since 0.223.3; `health.md`, "Where in the frame the item's hands come"). A slot's key, a quick-use key, the grenade's or the knife's key, a weapon put into the active slot (`TrySetInHands`) and the screen's "Use" all go through `Player.Proceed`, whose process drops the hands in hand first: the empty hands' `Drop` calls back at once, and the new controller is made (a firearm's by `FirearmController.CreateController`, not awaited) and spawned in the same call, before the frame's hands update, which then steps its draw. From a weapon in hand the next comes at its put-away's end, an event of its animator's. The app hears the screen's clicks and the quick-use keys before its controller's step, and from the empty hands takes what they ask there (`inventory_view::hands_first`); from anything else in hand, after the step (`settle`). The screen opens and shuts after the step as before (`toggle`: a screen opened on a frame has the keys from the next).

**Several weapons** (2026-10-12, evening). The app builds a rig for each of its weapons (the AKM, the M3 Super 90, the Five-seveN, the Model 700: `trp-app`'s `WEAPONS`), each with its own animator, logic and weapon motion, and one of them or the empty hands' is in the hands at a time (`weapon::Stowed`). Any of the four that hangs in a slot a weapon is held from is taken in hand by that slot's key; a pistol is put into the holster, whose key is `1`. Each rig keeps its weapon's rounds, fire mode and fold while it stands by. **One logic to a kind of weapon:** two weapons of one kind would share their rounds (`PROVISIONAL`, as before).

## Reach (`Player.InteractionRaycast`, `InteractionRay`)

What can be opened or picked up is found by a line from the point the player looks from, begun `BEHIND_CAST` (0.15 m) behind it and reaching `LOOT_RAYCAST_DISTANCE` (1.3 m) ahead of it; a door is nearer still (`DOOR_RAYCAST_DISTANCE`, 1 m). Both figures are the settings object's. A loot container is then opened by the interaction key, which plays the character's own animation for it and shows the container in the stash's place; searching it takes time, item by item (not built: here the crate is open at once).

## Opening a container, and the hands while the inventory is open (read 2026-10-11, midday)

**Corrected on 2026-10-12** by the recordings made by hand (`interactions.md`, "Working a door or a container"): the approach described next is the game's way to a thing that is **unlocked or kicked**. A container that is simply searched goes no such way: no approach, no crouch, no state; its gesture, and its screen 0.55 s after the key (the install's `DelayToOpenContainer`). What follows stands for the unlocking path only.

**Approaching** (`ApproachState`, a movement state of its own; `LootableContainer.GetInteractionParameters`). A thing in the world that is opened says where it is opened from, what is looked at, and which animation opens it (a container's own, or `ContainerOpenDefault`). On entering the state:

- the lean is let go and blind fire ended; a character lying down is no longer taken as lying;
- if what is looked at (or its handle) is more than 0.65 m under the ribcage, the character **crouches to it**: the stance goes to its lowest, and the stance it had is kept (`LevelOnApproachStart`). If it is above the ribcage the character stands up to it. A thing may forbid either;
- if the thing says so (`Snap`), the character is walked to the place it is opened from while its body and view turn to the thing: the time is the distance over 2.5 m/s for an unburdened character (down to 0.01 fully overweight), no less than the turn takes at 90 degrees a second, no more than 1.5 s; the way through is eased;
- when that is done the body's animator is told (`Approached`) and plays the animation for the interaction; at its end the thing opens and, for a container, the inventory screen with it.

When the container is shut (the screen closed), it is told so and **the stance kept is taken again** (`Player.TryInteractionCallback`).

There is also a movement state named for looting (`LootPlayerState`: the view held within 15 degrees either way and from 15 down to 3 up, no lean). Nothing in the game's code enters it or sets the animator's `Loot` flag from outside: like automatic vaulting, it is dead in this version.

**The inventory open** (`Player.SetInventoryOpened`, `WaitWeaponInventory`). Blind fire ends, a mounted weapon is taken off its mount, and the thing in hand is told: its own animator's flag for the inventory takes its hands layer through `OPEN INVENTORY` into `IN INVENTORY`, and `CLOSE INVENTORY` when the screen shuts. For the AKM those are the clips `akm_hands_out` (the left hand comes off and the weapon is turned aside in the right), `akm_hands_off` (held so) and `akm_hands_in`. Only once the hands are in one of the first two is the body's animator told (`Inventory`), which is the pose others see; while the hands do something else with the screen open (loading a magazine) it is told that too (`InventoryOperation`). A weapon taken out of its slot in the screen is put away from there (`IN INVENTORY` has a way of its own into the put-away state), and when the screen shuts it does not come back.

The three states, as the AKM's animator has them (read from the install, 2026-10-11; `probe states <weapon> INVENTORY`):

| State | Clip | Plays | Way out |
|---|---|---|---|
| `IDLE` | the resting clip, at half speed | | into `OPEN INVENTORY` on the flag, blended over 0.028 s |
| `OPEN INVENTORY` | `akm_hands_out`, 0.667 s | from its start | into `IN INVENTORY` half way through (0.333 s), blended over 0.25 s; it has no other |
| `IN INVENTORY` | `akm_hands_off`, 0.033 s | at a tenth of its speed, so over 0.333 s | into `CLOSE INVENTORY` when the flag goes, blended over 0.014 s |
| `CLOSE INVENTORY` | `akm_hands_in`, 0.667 s | from its start | into `IDLE` at 0.64 s, blended over 0.03 s |

`akm_hands_off` is not a still pose and **does not loop** (of the 85 clips of the weapon's whose names have an `a`, one is marked as looping, `hand_take`; the resting clip is not): its 33 ms carry the hands on to where they rest, and when it has run out the state stays on its last frame, as the engine leaves any state whose clip does not loop. Played over and over from its start, the left hand snaps 22 mm back every 33 ms, which is what the app did until the evening of 2026-10-11 (below). The way down has only the one way out, so an inventory shut at once still takes the hands down, and they start back 0.58 s after it opened at the earliest (half the way down, then the blend into the held state, which the animator does not leave while it is still on its way in).

**Which of the weapon's operations take it** (`FirearmController`, each operation's `SetInventoryOpened`). The idling one puts the sights down and lets the trigger go, both when the inventory opens and when it shuts, and sets the animator's flag at once. A shot, automatic fire or a reload in hand only notes it and sets the flag when it hands back to idling. Empty hands, a knife and a grenade have the same call.

**The animator's other ways into and out of the hold** (read 2026-10-11, night; `firing.md`, "The weapon goes by its animator", has them with their figures). A reload made or finished with the screen open does not bring the hands back to the weapon: `RELOAD IN` (with a round chambered) and `RELOAD CHARGE END` (after racking the bolt) leave for `RELOAD INVENTORY END`, which gives way to `IN INVENTORY`. A reload in hand tells the animator of the screen only once its magazine is out. A weapon put away while it is held down goes from `IN INVENTORY` to its put-away state. A weapon being drawn does not hear of the screen, but is told how it stands as soon as it has appeared, and then goes down for it (corrected 2026-10-12: `firing.md`, "Being drawn"). Automatic fire ends at the end of the cycle in which the screen is open.

**The body is told as of a reload.** The hands' controller, whatever is in the hands, calls the movement state's `OnInventory`, which sets the body animator's `ProneReload` flag, the one `OnReload` sets at a reload's start and end. Both write the one flag, so it is whatever was said last. It is what holds a lying body still (`movement.md`, "Looking").

## Empty hands (`Player.EmptyHandsController`, `EmptyHandsTemplate`; read 2026-10-11, evening)

A character with nothing in its hands still has something in them as far as the game goes: an item made in code (`EmptyHands`, with a template that names one bundle, `assets/content/weapons/empty hands/weapon_empty_hands_container.bundle`, and is in no database) and a hands controller for it like a weapon's.

**The rig** (read from the install; `probe tree`, `probe clips` and `probe states` take the bundle in place of a weapon's id). The bundle's root carries the same `WeaponPrefab` script a weapon's does, and the object it names (`weapon_empty_hands.generated`, 62 bones) is built like a weapon's rig: both arms from the collarbones to the finger tips under the same names, the animated camera bone, and `Weapon_root/Weapon_root_anim/weapon` with the two hand markers, the two reference markers, the elbows' goals, an aim bone and a fire port, and two bones for a thing held (`prop`). It draws nothing of its own. So everything that places the arms on a weapon places them here: the clips move the markers and the arms are solved onto them. (A second object in the bundle, `..._simple.generated`, is the same rig for the reduced animation sets.)

**Its animator** has two layers that play, the hands' and the left hand's gestures (the same gestures a weapon's has). The hands' states:

| State | Clip | Way out |
|---|---|---|
| `SPAWN` | `empty_hands_out`, 0.033 s | into `OUT TO IDLE` at once when the hands are made active |
| `OUT TO IDLE` | `empty_hands_out_to_idle`, 0.167 s; its mark `WeapIn` 0.034 s in | into `IDLE` at 0.15 s, blended over 0.017 s |
| `IDLE` | `empty_hands_idle` and `empty_hands_idle_down`, blended by `Aim_angle` between 0 and 90 | |
| `IDLE TO OUT` | `empty_hands_idle_to_out`, 0.167 s; `WeapOut` at its end | back to `SPAWN` |
| `LOOK` | `empty_hands_look`, 2.667 s | to `IDLE` at its end |

`Aim_angle` is the view's pitch in degrees, set every frame (`ManualUpdate`): level and above, the hands rest by `empty_hands_idle`; looking straight down, by `empty_hands_idle_down`; between the two, by a blend in proportion. **There are no states for an open inventory:** the animator has the flag set like any other and nothing answers to it, so with empty hands the screen changes nothing about the arms.

**The controller's life.** *Appearing* (`SpawnOperation`): the body's animator is given a weapon width of one and told the hands are busy (`ReloadFloat` 1), the hands' animator is made active, and when its `WeapIn` mark comes, 0.034 s in, the hands count as there and the body is told they are free. *Idling:* after five minutes with nothing done the animator is given its idle trigger; a look at the hands is `LOOK`. *Going* (`Remove`): the body is told the hands are busy and the controller is finished **at once**; `IDLE TO OUT` is not waited for, so empty hands vanish as the next thing is drawn. The inventory opening sets the animator's flag and tells the body's `ProneReload` as for a weapon.

**The weapon motion goes on, with nothing made afresh** (`ProceduralWeaponAnimation.ClearPreviousWeapon`, `InitTransforms`). The motion is one object for whatever is in the hands. For empty hands it is told to forget the weapon (no weapon data, so no recoil, no sights to bring the eye to, no line that feels for obstacles) and its sway and displacement strengths are put to 0.5; then it is tied to the new rig's bones and its springs are reset. What it is *not* told stays as the last weapon set it: the point the rig swings about, the damping of the position spring, the ergonomic weight. And the two strengths of 0.5 last only until the stance or the carried weight next changes, when they are worked out again from that left-over ergonomic weight (`OnAimOrPoseChanged`, `UpdateSwayFactors`). A character that has held no weapon has the player prefab's own figures: its `PlayerSpring` swings about (0, -0.17, 0) and damps the position spring by 0.5, and its ergonomic weight is nothing.

**The body's animator** is told the kind of thing in hand (`WeaponAnimationType`: empty hands are 4) and uses it only to choose between the rifle, pistol and launcher variants of additive layers; the unarmed upper body others see comes from this rig, which the body's arms follow as they follow a weapon's.

**Built** (2026-10-11, evening).

- *Reading:* the prefab as a weapon is read (`trp_data::weapon::assemble_prefab`, `EMPTY_HANDS`); the player prefab's own swing point (`HandsData::rotation_center`); the weapon motion's settings for hands that hold no weapon (`trp_sim::empty_hands_settings`): the prefab's swing point and damping, no weight, no recoil, no line for obstacles; the second clip of a resting state that goes by the view's pitch (`trp_sim::rest_blend`) and the clip of a named state (`state_clip`).
- *In the app, the first rig.* **A run begun with nothing in hand begins with the empty hands' rig alone** (the AKM, the StG 44, the M3 Super 90 and the Five-seveN load as every other weapon does): every weapon's rig, the run's own among them, is made when the weapon is first hung where the hands take one from, and shares the empty hands' bones (they have every bone a weapon's rig shares that draws anything, `the_empty_hands_have_every_bone_a_weapon_shares`). A weapon's slot key pressed while its rig is still being made waits for it (the StG 44's takes 1.15 s); another key, or the weapon leaving the slot, ends the wait. A rig is taken in hand only once its meshes and pictures are all on the graphics card (a few frames after it comes). A run begun with a weapon in hand (one hung as it begins: `--equip`, a capture, a loadout) still makes that weapon's rig first, as follows.
- *In the app, rigs made ahead, let go of, and pictures shared* (pictures shared by weapons that look exactly the same). **A weapon the mouse rests on (0.25 s) or that is taken up has its rig made ready before it is hung**, once no weapon hung waits for one, one at a time, a later one taking the place of one not yet begun, and once a rest (`Preparing::ask_ahead`, `inventory_view::foresee`). **A rig nobody has carried for 30 s is let go of** (`weapon::free_rigs`): carried is hung where the hands take a weapon from, or in them; a rig still going up to the card, the first rig (whose bones every later one shares) and the empty hands' are never let go of. Its own entities go (not the first rig's that it shares) and with them its meshes and its pictures, unless another rig draws with them; hung again, it is made again as the first time. **Pictures alike are shared**: each picture of a rig is marked by all it is (its size, format, levels, sampling and every byte; `PreparedLooks::mark_pictures`, on the rigs' thread), and a rig whose picture has the mark of one already on the card draws with that one and sends nothing (`render_data::SharedPictures`, with how many rigs hold each). **At most four rigs nobody carries are kept**, the one carried longest ago let go of for a fifth. (From 0.7.1 to 0.12.4 the rigs also kept within the graphics card's places for samplers: rigs nobody carried were let go of while what was drawn held more than 1,536 of the 2,048, and a rig was made ahead only with 160 to spare. wgpu 27, Bevy 0.18's, keeps each distinct sampler once, and since 0.13.0 nothing counts them.)
- *In the app, a second rig.* The empty hands' rig is made beside the weapon's at the start. Its arms and its camera bone are the weapon rig's own (44 of its 62 bones: the two prefabs name them alike, and the body's meshes and the arms' mesh are skinned to them once); its weapon bone with the markers is its own. What is in the hands is always one of the two: its rig, its clips, its clip player and its weapon motion are the ones everything draws and steps by, and the other set stands by (`Stowed`). Putting the weapon away changes the sets over when its clip has played out (at once, with the inventory open), puts the shared bones as the empty hands' prefab has them, tells the body's animator a width of one, hides the weapon's own bone and plays `empty_hands_out_to_idle` into the resting clip. Drawing the weapon changes them back at once and plays its draw clip. The resting hands are the first clip with the second laid over it by the view's pitch over 90 degrees.
- With the inventory open the empty hands do nothing, as their animator has nothing for it.
- *Since the night of 2026-10-11 the empty hands run on their own animator* (`trp_sim::EmptyHands`): made active they leave `SPAWN` for `OUT TO IDLE`, are there at its `WeapIn` mark (0.05 s after they are asked for, at 120 frames a second) and rest in `IDLE`, whose blend of two clips by `Aim_angle` is the animator's own blend tree, told the view's pitch every frame; told to go, they are gone at once. The app draws that animator's pose as it draws a weapon's, and no clip is played by name any more. The body's animator is told the hands are busy while they appear and from the moment they are to go. Checked against the install (`the_weapon_goes_by_its_animator`): four of the rig's 62 bones are elsewhere with the view straight down.

**Checked.** Against the install (`empty_hands_are_read_like_a_weapon`): the rig's arms and markers, the resting state's two clips and their values, no inventory states, 0.167 s to appear with the mark 0.034 s in. In the app, by scripted runs (2026-10-11): a bare start plays `empty_hands_out_to_idle` and rests on `empty_hands_idle`, the hands 0.43 m under the eye and out of the picture with the view level (`local\shots\eh_fp.png`), the left hand in the picture with the view 70 degrees down (`eh_down.png`); from outside the arms are bent at the elbows with the hands before the belt (`eh_body.png`), and in a sprint the body's own running arms take over (`eh_sprint_side.png`); and one run through the whole of it (`--loot --bare --drag 1100:180:170:450 --tap 150:tab --tap 200:3 --tap 500:tab --tap 620:delete --tap 720:tab`): empty hands, the rifle taken from the crate and drawn with `3` (`akm_out_to_idle`, then its resting clip), the inventory opened (`akm_hands_out`, `akm_hands_off`), the rifle put on the ground there, and the empty hands appearing again. **Compared with the game** (`courses8`'s `empty-hands`, first person, and `empty-hands-from-outside`, replayed from their keys): the hands' animator, the movement, the stance, the body's states and every curve and flag the game's on every frame of both; the body and the arms from outside within a degree but one frame and the first sprint's second; in first person the arms within 1.7 degrees rms (the breath's random roll and yaw, as with a weapon, and peaks in a fast look up and a sprint's end, not settled). The swing point and recoil pivot kept of the last firearm are the recordings' own (the pistol's, held last); the sway's left-over strength is not recorded, so the sway was not compared (with the strength the recording shows, 0.364, it agrees to 0.011 rms).

**What the motion keeps of the last firearm** (`ClearPreviousWeapon`, `UpdateSwayFactors`; built 0.169.0, `Hands::keep_from`): hands that take up a thing that is not a firearm (the empty hands, an item used, a grenade) take the motion as the last one left it: the swing point with and without the stock, the recoil's pivot, the position spring's damping, the ergonomic weight and the sway's strength as last worked out (the game's `MotionReact.SwayFactors`, which telling the motion to forget its weapon does not touch). The displacement's strength is put to 0.5. Both are worked out again from the ergonomic weight left, with the load as it stands, at the next change of stance, of aim or of the load. A character that has held no firearm has the prefab's figures, the displacement at 0.5 too. In the app the rig taken in hand gets them from the rig put away (`Stowed::change_to`), and since 0.172.0 the motion's running state with them (`Hands::carry_on_from`: the steps' tempo, the lean's reach, the blends), tied to the new rig as above (`Hands::tie_to_new_rig`: the springs' places, the turn-away's depth, the reaction to motion, the breath's and the walk's loops started again); recorded in `courses8`'s grenade throws and eating, the tempo and the lean's reach going on through every change.

**PROVISIONAL or not built:**

- (What the empty hands keep of the last weapon, and the displacement's 0.5 until the next change of stance: built 0.169.0, above.) The springs start at rest where the game's are reset in place.
- (The body's animator is told the kind of thing in hand since: `body-animator.md`, "The kind of thing in hand", which also has a sprint's fists; and that the hands are busy while they appear since the night after that.)
- (A weapon drawn with the inventory open is told of the screen when it has appeared, by the player's owner, and goes down then: read and built on 2026-10-12, the night before's "stays up" having missed that call. The draw's own way into `OPEN INVENTORY` at 0.78 of itself is taken only if the animator's flag is up before the draw ends, which happens in the game when a draw is cut short; that is not built.)
- The look at the hands, the compass, the idle trigger after five minutes, dropping the backpack, and every gesture of the left hand.
- If the install's empty hands cannot be read, the weapon and the arms are hidden as before.

## What an open screen does to the keys (read 2026-10-11, evening)

With the inventory open the character does not move, as in the game. Read from `InputSystem.InputNode`, `UI.Screens.UIScreen`, `InventoryScreen`, `PlayerOwner`, `GamePlayerOwner`, `MoveInputTranslator`.

- **The chain.** Every frame the game hands that frame's commands (one for each key pressed or let go) and its axes (movement, the mouse, the slow lean) down a chain of input nodes. Each node may take commands out of the list and may take the axes away. The player's node is added to the chain with low priority, so everything else is asked first.
- **A screen takes nearly all of it.** Every screen (`UIScreen`: the inventory, the menus, the settings) hands on no axes at all, and of the commands only five that are not the player's: the screenshot, the console, the inventory key and the two of the voice chat (`InputNode.GetDefaultBlockResult`). The inventory screen itself shuts on the inventory key or Escape.
- **So while it is open** the player's `Move`, `Look`, `Rotate` and slow lean are not called (not called with nothing: not called), and no key reaches it, **neither a press nor a release**.
- **The frame of the key.** The inventory key reaches the player's node, since the screen is not up yet. The player is told its inventory is open at once (`SetInventoryOpened`), whatever commands came after the key in that frame are dropped, and that frame's axes are still handed over. The screen has the keys from the frame after.
- **What that does to movement** (`movement.md`, "A frame with no input"): a walk or a crawl is left with no input and runs down as if the keys had been let go; a sprint loses its forward on the first such frame and ends through its stop; a jump keeps the direction it had; standing, nothing is asked. With the keys still down when the screen shuts, the walk starts again, since the axes are a state and not an event.
- **What was pressed or let go meanwhile is lost.** A lean key let go under the screen leaves the character leaning when it shuts, until that key is pressed again (which then straightens: a press toward the side already leaned to does), and the same goes for a held step aside. A sprint key let go under the screen is no stop command, and one pressed under it is no start. The aim key and the trigger are commands too: the sights came down at the opening, and a button still held when the screen shuts does nothing until it is pressed again.
- **The vault key.** The opening and the shutting both switch its per-frame try off (`OnStartInventoryOpen`, which `InventoryOpenRaiseAction` raises either way; `vaulting.md`, "Two moves, one key"). The key itself is not a state but a switch that every press and every release turns over, so a key held through the opening and let go after the shutting switches the try *on* with the key up: the hold's timer then runs, a jump is refused once it has passed its 0.6 s, and the next press switches it off again. Read from the code; not recorded.

Nothing of this section is recorded: the recorder's scripts cannot open the inventory.

**Out of the hands and into them.** A weapon is put away by its animator's own state for it (the AKM's clip `akm_idle_to_out`) and drawn by `OUT TO IDLE` (`akm_out_to_idle`, 0.97 s) from `SPAWN`.

**Empty hands** are an item of their own (`EmptyHands`) with a prefab like a weapon's and a controller with its own operations (appearing, idling, dropping the backpack, the compass). They have a section of their own below.

**Putting down and picking up.** Followed to its end on 2026-10-12 (`interactions.md`): the weapon's own layer for the left hand's actions (`hand_drop_stuff`, `Take Loot`) is not what plays for a firearm, whose calls for it are empty; the gestures are states of the body's animator. A thing picked up is taken by the body's `8 Take`, standing still only, and goes 0.6 s in to the first place found for it, by the game's own order (`QuickFindAppropriatePlace`; read on 2026-10-12, afternoon: "Clicks, keys and windows", "Put on"). A thing discarded falls as a thing of the world with no gesture at all: the game's list has one for it and nothing asks for it. Only a backpack taken off has one.

**Splitting** a stack: dragged with Ctrl held, the game asks how many in a window of its own and puts that many in the place (`SplitMax`; "Clicks, keys and windows", "Part of a stack", read and built on 2026-10-12).

## Examining (`ExamineOperation`, `InventoryController.Examine`, `Examined`, `Profile.Encyclopedia`; read 2026-11-01, built 0.178.0)

**What is known.** A character knows a kind of thing (`InventoryController.Examined`) when its controller was made knowing everything (`examined: true`: the hideout's shooting range, a player seen from outside), when the kind is known by default (its template's `ExaminedByDefault`, or a quest's item, `QuestItem`), or when the profile's list holds its template (`Profile.Encyclopedia`, a map of templates; a profile with no list knows everything, as SPT's bots do). It is the kind, not the thing: one examined, every thing of its template is known, everywhere at once. The player in a raid is made not knowing (`LocalPlayer`, `SinglePlayerInventoryController`, `examined: false`), so the list decides. The client adds to the list only by an examine (`LearnInEncyclopedia`, whose one caller is the operation; `Profile.LearnAll` is called by nothing): nothing is learned by a pick-up, a putting on or a raid's start. Of the install's 4,577 templates, 745 are not known by default (classes among them); a new character's list (`profiles.json`, each edition and side's `character.Encyclopedia`) holds 94 for the Standard USEC, 74 for the Standard BEAR. **Here**: the list of the new character the app's is made from (the Standard BEAR, or `--loadout`'s), with what the app learns kept in its options file between runs (`"examined"`, in the order learned); the F1 panel's INVENTORY row, "Examined", says how many and its FORGET forgets them. What the character carries in a slot as a run begins, at any depth, is known too, rounds aside: a slot of one's own refuses an unknown thing, out of a raid as well (`BackEndInventoryController`, `examined: false`), so the game's character can carry none there; what lies in a grid is as the list has it (the app's default kit is all known to a new BEAR). The switch `EXAMINING` (restart required) off: everything known, as the shooting range's.

**The examine.** Asked of a thing not known (an known one: nothing; one examine at a time: a second asked meanwhile gives the notice "You can't examine two items at the same time" and nothing else), it takes the item's `ExamineTime` over 1 + Attention's `AttentionExamine` (0.02 a level; `skills.md`), waited out in real time (`ItemController.RunOperation`, a delay); then the template is learned and the item's `ExamineExperience` added to the profile's experience (no skill is raised: `ExamineAction` and `ExamineWithInstruction` are completed by nothing). It is not cut short: the screen shut or the thing moved, it runs to its end. **Asked by** the menu's EXAMINE line, the middle button on the thing (`ExecuteMiddleClick`: examine first, else fold, unfold, switch on or off, else check a magazine), the inspect window's EXAMINE button, and in the world a loose thing's own action (below). From the inventory, its success says "Item examined: " and the thing's short name, " (+" its experience " exp)" (`ItemUiContext.Examine`, `DisplayMessageNotification`), with the notice's sound (`notification_exp`: no sound of its own); the world's action says nothing. **Its frames** (`courses10`'s `dead-scav-examine`, `examine-looted-item`, `examine-multiple`, six examines at Attention 40, 0.556 s each): begun on the release's frame (the middle button's, or the menu's line, the menu gone on it: the thing's view, its caption gone, the blink's first step and the game scene's panel all on it; no sound); the panel gone on the first frame its own clock is out, and the thing known on the next (its views drawn anew, its caption and figures, the notice and its sound): the operation's end is heard a frame after its delay.

**An unknown thing's box** (`GridItemView.UpdateInfo`, `ItemViewAnimation`, `ItemViewStats`): its picture black, at 0.7 (the colour (0, 0, 0, 0.75), its alpha then set to 0.7 for a thing not known, `UpdateMainImageAlpha`; 0.85 dragged); the box's colour panel black at 100 of 255 in place of its kind's colour; under it the box filled with `slot_unexamined_fill` (`NotExaminedBackground`, tiled, while not dragged); its caption " ??? " in (0.714, 0.757, 0.780) (`#b6c1c7`); each figure at its lower right "?" (a weapon's "?/?", a magazine's, a medkit's, food's, a key's uses, armour's, a repair kit's; a weapon of no capacity nothing), a weapon's round "?"; a stack's count shown as ever. Hidden: the part's and kind icons at the corner (`SetItemIcon`), a plate's class, the repair buff's icon, the wish list's marks. Kept: the tag, the found-in-raid mark, the lock and pin. The hover's tooltip "Unknown item"; a slot's own refusal first where there is one. An examine ended under the mouse renews the hover (its examination's view, what the mouse was on, gone from under it: `OnPointerExit`, then `OnPointerEnter`): the tooltip keeps its words on the known frame, is closed on the next and shown again with the thing's name its 0.6 s after (`dead-scav-examine`: "Unknown item" on 748, closed on 749, the name on 821); one begun under the mouse leaves it as it is (the view's label lies within the thing's view: `examine-multiple`'s third, from 592 through its start on 616 to 683). Drawn as the game's, the noise lies under the colour panel, and the panel lit under the mouse (white at 0.302) over the noise (`NotExaminedBackground`, then `Color Panel`). **Dragged**, its picture black at 0.85 (`DraggedItemView`). Every view of the template is drawn anew when it is learned (`GridItemView.OnExamine`).

**While examined**: its box blinks as one being added or removed (`Blinking`: the same fade to nothing over 0.5 s and back; its picture alone, `_mainImage`), its fade from 0.75, the colour `UpdateInfo` gives it again after the blink began and before the tween's first step, which is on the release's frame by that frame's time (0.722 on 681, 0.0093 s in: the click is heard before the tweens step), each half begun on the frame the last is done, its clock from there (0 on 739 and 740, 0.0331 on 741); its caption hidden (`OnBeingExaminedChanged`; a slot's view its mastering and durability bars too), its figures and round hidden there too but shown again at once by the `UpdateInfo` that `GridItemView.OnExamine` calls next (a key's "?/?" drawn through every examine of the three takes; a weapon's round read from the code only), and over it the examination's view (`ItemViewExaminationComponent`): a bar 117 by 10 at (5, 49) of a 127 by 64 view (its ground `slider_background_90`, its fill `slider_filler` in (0.275, 0.506, 0.635)), a spinner 44 square at (41.5, 10) in (0, 0.627, 1), turned clockwise 360 degrees a second about its middle from nothing on the examine's first frame (measured: -4.37 a frame on, -43.99 at 0.122 s, every examine from nothing; what turns it was not found, its object having no script but its picture: `probe prefab .../itemviewexaminationcomponent`), and over the whole view the percent done as a bare number, rounded (`RoundToInt`; no "%"), size 14, centred, in (0, 0.627, 1). It goes by the wall clock from the moment it began; a view made meanwhile begins its bar from nothing (the game's quirk). The box takes clicks still. **The game scene's panel** (`UsingPanel`, `Game Scene/UsingPanel` in `GameUIScene`, `probe usingpanel`; fed by the player's examine event, `OnPlayerExamining` at its `Begin`): "EXAMINING" (size 20, black, on white at 0.392) over the thing's own full name (`Item.Name`, shown though it is unknown; size 14, white, on black, broken into lines within 176, the plate and the panel's description growing upward with it by their groups and fitters: a line 15.4 high, two 31.36), a bar 287.8 by 8 filled from the left in the script's `_examineColor` (0.329, 0.757, 1) by the wall clock (`Progress`: `DateTime.Now` from its start over the time), 477.8 by 162 at the bottom middle (721, 842 at 1920 by 1080); shown on the examine's first frame, its fill 0, and gone, all of it at once, on the first frame its own clock is out, a frame before the thing is known; no fade, no sound. Its canvas, the game scene's (order 1), lies over the inventory's (0) and under the preloader's (100: menus, windows, tooltips, notices): **it shows over the open inventory**, across the quick-use bar (all three takes).

**The inspect window of an unknown thing** (`ItemSpecificationPanel`): caption "Unknown item", its kind "?", no description, the weight shown; no parts' panel, mastering or durability; each figure "?" (a bar at 0 with "?", a list "???"); the model drawn black (`_cameraImage` tinted black), white again once learned; the buttons EXAMINE and DISCARD (INSPECT left out). Under the figures the examine's own bar (`ExamineCharacteristicPanel`, 521 by 23): "ITEM HAS NOT BEEN EXAMINED YET!" in red, and while examined "EXAMINING (" the percent truncated ")" in (0.329, 0.757, 1), its slider the share done.

**The menu** (`ItemContextInteractionsSwitcher.IsActive`): a thing not known shows only Inspect, Examine and Discard; Examine shows for a thing not known that has a place (`CurrentAddress`), refused with "Item is being examined" while that same thing is. A double click opens the inspect window (a container: nothing, its Open not shown; food or medicine: nothing).

**What it refuses** (the player's own controller):
- **A slot of the character's own** (`Slot.CheckConditions`, `RemoveItemInternal`; the slot's item owned by the player's controller: the equipment and what is in it, not a container of the world or a body) takes no unknown thing but rounds ("Cannot equip unexamined item" in the equipment's own slots, "Cannot install unexamined item" elsewhere), after its being taken, its lock and a worn armour's plates, before its filter; and gives none up but rounds ("You can't take off unexamined equipment", "Item is not examined"), after its lock, before a malfunction. A grid has no such rule.
- **A drop on a slot** (`ExecutePossibleAction` to a slot's address) of an unknown thing, rounds too, whoever owns the slot: "Cannot install unexamined item". **A drop on a weapon** (`Weapon.Apply`) or of rounds on a magazine (`Magazine.ApplyItem`), the dropped thing or the target unknown: the same words, after a merge of stacks is tried (merging is never refused).
- **The quick slots and the hands**: an unknown thing is not bound (`IsAtBindablePlace`) nor taken in hand (`IsAtReachablePlace`). The grenade key takes only known grenades.
- **A reload** passes over unknown rounds (`ReloadWithAmmo`, a cylinder's, the barrels'; the round chooser lists only known rounds) and unknown magazines (their move refused); a weapon whose own magazine is unknown is not reloaded ("Attached magazine is not examined.", the install having no words for it).
- Nothing else changes: weight, armour, health and the rest read nothing of it.

**In the world** (`InteractionContextHelper.GetAvailableInteractionState`): a loose thing unknown is named "Unknown item" on every one of its lines, and offered **Examine** after TAKE (not for a body's gear): the character bends to it as for a pick-up (`PickUpState`, the look held within its limits; the lines and the name gone on the key's frame, the sense's dot whole meanwhile), the examine begins on the first frame past 0.6 s (the state's action, the inventory controller's `Examine` itself), and the state ends with the examine (its callback's `Examine(false)` leaves it) or after 5 s. The examine's end is heard a frame after its time runs out, as the inventory's: the battle screen's panel gone on the one, the state left, the kind learned and the prompt back with the short name and TAKE alone on the next. **No notice and no sound** (`ItemUiContext.Examine` posts "Item examined", not the controller's own). A second examine asked while one is under way (the inventory's, carried on after the screen shut) gives the warning, and the state, its callback never coming, lasts its 5 s. Compared with `courses10`'s `examine-world-loot`, the app's flare and slide laid ahead (`local\run_ewl_s186.sh`): the prompt over an unknown thing (box, lines, "UNKNOWN ITEM") within 0.03 pixels of the take's, EXAMINE chosen by the wheel; the prompt gone on the key's frame (a frame late before, TAKE's too); the dot at 0.9999 through the state (faded at five a second before, the sense not cast); the panel from the first frame past 0.6 s (app 73 frames after the key at 120 a second, the take 72 at its 117), gone 67 frames on (the take 66) and the prompt back the frame after with "SIGNAL FLARE (BLUE)" and TAKE within 0.02 pixels of the take's; the slide's two lines of name in the panel within 0.2 pixels; nothing said, nothing heard.

**Built** (`trp_sim::examine`, `item_menu`, `inventory`; `examine_view`, `inventory_view`): the list and its keeping, the examine and its time (Attention's buff with stimulators'), the box's look in both looks (framed as the game's, the noise under the colour panel, lit or not; the app's own, the noise over the black panel darkened by its share), the blink and the examination's view read from the install (its label in the label's own font; the loader turned, the fade from 0.75, the figures kept), the drag's picture, the inspect window's caption, kind, description, figures, model and buttons, the menu's lines, the middle button (all six of its lines, a reason said once), the notice and its sound (shown at once beside any others, its row opening: the malfunctions spec, "What the shooter is told"), the game scene's panel (`using_view`, with `TARKOV UI`: what is under way, however begun; over the inventory under its windows), the start and end frames, the tooltip renewed at the end, the refusals of slots, drops, quick slots, the slot keys, reloads (the warning too) and the grenade key; Attention no longer dimmed on the SKILLS page; the hover's "Unknown item" since `courses10`'s `hover-tooltips` ("A thing's name, and an armour's points"). The world's Examine built in s186 (`Movement::pick_up_to_examine`, `examined`; `inventory_view::reach`, `toggle`; `examine_view::finish_world`). **Not built**: the inspect window's own examine bar (`ExamineCharacteristicPanel`); the experience (the app keeps none; a take's notices of it: interactions spec, "Taking a thing up"); the menu's Reload of a weapon whose magazine is unknown (the key's is refused). Read from the code, the sim's rules tested (`tests/examine.rs`), the look seen in captures (`local\shots\examine_*_s176.png`). **Compared** with `courses10`'s `examine-multiple`, `examine-looted-item` and `dead-scav-examine`, the app's four keys in its pockets examined by the take's middle clicks (`local\run_em_s186.sh`) and a keycard by the menu: begun on the release's frame (a frame late before), the picture 0.7251 on it (the take's 0.7252; 1 before), its loader turned (-5.87 a frame on; still before), its "?/?" drawn (hidden before), the panel's boxes and its words' ink within 0.2 pixels of the take's (one line and two), its fill 0 on the first frame, gone on the frame the label reads 100 and the thing known on the next (before, the label never read 100), three notices up at once within a pixel of the take's rows, the tooltip closed on the frame after the end and the name 0.609 s on. Gear-dependent, not compared: the cells' places (the take's backpack and loot, the app's pockets), a key's uses (the take's used). **Not compared**: an examine's thing's round (no take examines a weapon), a slot's view examined. (The panel over the battle screen: "In the world", above.)

## Searching a container (`ActiveSearchContentOperation`, `SinglePlayerSearchContentOperation`, `PlayerSearchController`, `ActiveSearchController`, `SearchableView`, `SearchButton`, `TimerText`, `SimpleStashPanel.Show`, `GridItemView.UpdateInfo`, `Player.InventoryControllerOnSearchEvent`; read 2026-11-01, built 0.179.0)

**What is searched.** The grids of a searchable thing (`SearchableItem`: a loot container of the world, a backpack, a vest, the pockets, `MobContainer`; its grids are `SearchableGrid`s) hide what lies in them from a character until it is found; slots and other grids hide nothing. The character's controller keeps the searchable things it has searched (`_searchedItems`) and the things it knows where they lie (`_knownItems`, a thing with its place: moved elsewhere it is known there only if the move made it so). As the controller is made, everything the character wears is searched and known, at any depth (`UncoverContent`). A thing put into a searchable grid by the character is known there (`AddResult.RaiseEvents`: known for the move's length, then for good); one taken out is forgotten where it was (`RemoveResult.RaiseEvents`). So what is hidden is what lay in a container before the character came to it: a raid's containers, a body's gear. **Here**: the range's four crates, filled as the run begins (below), are hidden; the rest of the range, the armory's cases, boxes and cabinets, is known; a thing not found cannot be moved, so what is found need never be forgotten (`trp_sim::search::Searcher`: the containers not yet searched and the things not yet found). Since 0.180.0 a searchable thing lying in a hidden crate is itself unsearched (`hide`, at any depth: a bag found has to be opened in its own view before what it holds shows), as is one `--lay` puts on the floor (the world's, not the character's), and so is a dead dummy's gear (below, "Searching a body"); what the character carries at the start, and the armory, stay known.

**The search** (`SearchContents`, one operation a container): it is made at once (its timer counts from then, `StartTime`, and its luck is rolled) and **run on the next frame** (the player's inventory controller yields a frame before it runs an operation: `ExecuteAsync`, `Task.Yield`). Run, it waits to **open** a container never searched, **2 s** (`ACCESS_SECONDS`, `Task.Delay(2000)`), then marks it searched (its grids shown); a container searched before skips this. **Each wait begins when the step before it lands**, on the frame its wait's end is first seen (each `Task.Delay` awaited after the last has resumed on a frame), so a search runs late by a part of a frame a step and never early. It then finds what is still hidden, **one thing at a time**, the first not known in the order the container's grids hold them (`GetFirstLevelItems`), each after a wait of `Random.Range(1, 3)`, one or two seconds, over the pace, in whole milliseconds (`(int)(… * 1000)`); the pace is **1 + Attention's `AttentionLootSpeed`** (0.02 a level), and **Search's `SearchBuffSpeed`** (0.01 a level) besides where the container lies in someone's equipment (`RootItem is InventoryEquipment`: a body's gear). A thing found is known where it lies and makes its own **drop** sound (`PlayItemSound(ItemSound, drop)`). The search ends with the last thing, or at once if there was nothing to find. **At Attention's elite**, a first search (of a container never searched) is **over at once one time in two** (`AttentionEliteLuckySearch` 0.5, rolled as the operation is made): no wait to open, none between things, the luck sound (`looting_luck2`, as a thing's `other` sound) as it runs (`ExecuteInternal`, the frame after it is made) and no drop sounds. **Stopped** (the stop button, or its view shut: `SearchableView.Close`), it ends where it is: a container not yet opened stays unsearched (the next search waits to open again, and rolls its luck again), what was found stays found. **One search at a time** (`CanStartNewSearchOperation`), **two at Search's elite** (`SearchDouble`). A locked container is not searched (`CheckItemForLocked`).

**The view** (`SearchableView`, in the loot panel's `SimpleStashPanel` and a container's window, `GridWindow`; `UpdateSearchState`): the **unsearched panel** (`Unsearched Panel`, 400 by 125: the gradient, the tiled `search_pattern`, the sliced `unsearched_border_sliced`, "UNSEARCHED" at 24 in (0.773, 0.765, 0.698), and a loader beside it while being searched) covers the container until it has been opened, and its grids are not shown until then (`OnShowGrids`); a click on it begins a search where one may begin. The **search button** in the top bar (`SearchPanel/Search Button`: the `search_button` ground, the `search_icon` or, while searching, a loader; "SEARCH", or the key "SEARCHING..." while searching, which the install's English has as "SEARCHING", at 11 in (0.765, 0.804, 0.827)) shows while the container is not searched, is being searched, or still holds something hidden; a click begins a search (not while one is under way); **while it searches it takes no click** (`SetSearchStatus`: `interactable = !active`; a container's window has no stop button, so only shutting the window stops its search) and its ground is at its **off tint**: a `Button`'s colours on its ground (`BackgroundMask/Background`), idle (1, 1, 1, 1), off (0.184, 0.184, 0.184, 0.628), each change a straight fade of 0.1 s from the colour it had, the first step on the change's frame (`Graphic.CrossFadeColor`, `TweenRunner`); as the panel shows it is idle at once (`OnEnable`), so the search begun with the panel fades it from there; brought up to date while searching (the open, `ItemSearchedHandler`), it is made to take clicks and not again in one call, a step toward idle and one back (0.628 to 0.656, then 0.628 again 0.1 s on). The **stop button** (`close_button` with its cross) shows while searching, and the **timer** after it, the time since the search was made as whole minutes and two places of seconds ("0:07"), written as the timer is updated each frame (`TimerText.Update`; `Show` writes nothing): the panel shows and its search is made after the frame's scripts have been updated, so on that first frame the timer still says what it said when last hidden (the last search's time; the scene's own "10:20" before any, `PROVISIONAL`: not seen). The two **loaders** (the button's, and the unsearched panel's while it shows) turn clockwise a turn a second, each from upright as the search was made, together. **The loot panel begins a search** of a container not yet searched as it shows it (`SimpleStashPanel.Show`, whether a search may begin or not). **A thing not yet found** (`GridItemView.UpdateInfo`): every part of its view off but `UnsearchedBackground` (the same tiled `slot_unexamined_fill` as an unknown kind's, in (0.502, 0.502, 0.502) at 0.69, over the whole view), its view's group at 0.6 as a thing that cannot be taken out (`ItemView.Transparency`: the remove's `UnknownItemError`), so drawn at 0.414; no tooltip, no click, no drag (`ItemView.IsSearched`). Found, its view is shown in place on the find's frame with its drop sound; under a mouse already on it, it is not entered, so its tooltip waits until the mouse leaves and comes back (`GridItemView.OnPointerEnter` asks it only as the mouse enters). **The filters' column** is shown with the grids, not before (`SearchableItemView.ShowGrids` → `GridView.Show` → `FilterPanel.Show`), and a kind is lit only by a thing of it that is known there (`GridView.PrepareItems`: `IsItemKnown`; `ItemFoundHandler`: each thing as it is found).

**The sound**: while any search is under way the character plays a **search sound**, the last searched container's own (`SearchSound`, a clip of the things' sounds: `woodbox_looting`, `drawer_metal_looting`, `looting_body_extended` and seven more), looping, from just in front of the body (`PlayerColliderCenter` and a quarter of its forward), in first person not placed (plain stereo), in the player's movement group, at `SearchSoundVolume`'s first-person volume (the backend's `settings.json`, `AudioSettings.PlayerSettings`: 0.6; 0.45 heard by others), but for its first half second at that volume twice over, 0.36 (`SupportAudioSourceCoroutine`: the source's base volume set, then played at the same volume again, `SimpleSource.Play`; its next turn, after `WaitForSeconds(0.5)`, sets the base volume alone); it stops when none is.

**The range's crates** (the app's: filled as SPT fills them, from crates of the app's own): as a run begins, the wooden crate, the technical supply crate, the dead Scav and the drawer, and past the car the weapon box (`5909d76c86f77471e53d2adf`, 6 by 3), the toolbox (`5909d50c86f774659e6aaebe`, 4 by 3), the safe (`578f8782245977354405a1e3`, 3 by 3) and the airdrop weapon crate (`6223351bb5d97a7b2c635ca7`, 10 by 12) of `courses10`'s searching takes (all four where the car and the other boxes are), are each filled from **its template's table in Customs' static loot** (the install's `locations/bigmap/staticLoot.json`): how many by a draw of `itemcountDistribution` by weight, times the location's `staticLootMultiplier` (1), rounded halves to the even; which, each drawn by weight from `itemDistribution`, back in the pool after (SPT's `allowDuplicateItemsInStaticContainers`, true), the lootable blacklist (`item.json`) and the seasonal events' things left out (no event taken as on, `PROVISIONAL`: the Jason, fox and Grinch masks, the jar of pickles, the 40-degree fuel among these tables, by SPT's `SeasonalEventService` lists), a kind at the location's most (`lootMaxSpawnLimits`) passed over. Each is made **as the app makes things** (not SPT's way): a thing with a stock build as that build (empty magazines), money and rounds a stack between their template's `StackMinRandom` and `StackMaxRandom`, anything else one; laid where `find_room` finds room, a thing that does not fit passed over, and nothing more once more than `fitLootIntoContainerAttempts` (3) have failed (SPT's `LocationLootGenerator` and `ContainerExtensions.FindSlotForItem`, read in SPT's published source: its own place search tries a cell turned before the next cell; its weapons' magazines 25 to 100% full, its ammunition boxes full, its armour's plates by chance, none of which is done here). Then they are hidden. A respawn puts them back as the run began, hidden again. **The airdrop weapon crate has no table** in any location's static loot: SPT fills an airdrop from its `configs/airdrop.json` (the drop's kind drawn by `airdropTypeWeightings`; a weapon crate's `weaponArmor`: 6 to 8 weapons and 3 to 6 armours as their default presets, 11 to 22 things picked at random from the item types it names, 0 to 2 sealed weapon crates; `LootGenerator.CreateRandomLoot`, read in SPT's published source), which the app does not make; it is filled by the weapon box's Customs table instead (`main::STANDS_IN`, `PROVISIONAL`: the nearest weapon container among Customs' tables, its search sound the same, `drawer_metal_looting`).

**Built** (`trp_sim::search`, `trp_data::loot`, the app's `search_view`, `inventory_view`, `health_view::draw_stash`, `main::fill_crates`): all of the above but what follows, under the switch `SEARCHING` (restart required; off, the crates are empty and nothing is hidden). Since 0.180.0 every searchable thing whose view the screen shows is searched by that view (each a `SearchableView`: the loot panel's container, a body's vest, pockets and backpack, the character's own containers' rows, a container's window with its caption's button and its unsearched panel, `GridWindow`), each search stopped as its view goes, two at once at Search's elite; the unsearched panel's loader laid after its word (it lay over it). Since s187 (`courses10`'s five container searches, below): the search run a frame after it is made, each wait from the frame the step before landed, the loot panel a frame after the page, a hidden thing at 0.6 of its view, the filters' column and its kinds, the loaders' turn, the button's word, its off tint (the loot panel's), no click on it while searching, no tooltip for a thing found under the mouse, the timer's first frame, the sound's first half second. A find's experience notice since s187 (interactions spec, "Taking a thing up"). **Not built**: the skill's points a find gives (Attention's `FindAction`, the app keeps none); the off tint of a container's window's and a containers' row's buttons (each its own prefab's colours, not read), and a button's group at 0.3 while another search keeps it from beginning one (`SetEnabled`: `SetUnlockStatus`); the button's tints under the mouse and held.

**Compared with** `courses10`'s `dead-scav-search-a` (a toolbox), `dead-scav-search-b` (a dead Scav container), `weapon-box-search-a`, `-b` and `airdrop-crate-search`, at Attention 40, beside the app's `--start toolbox`, `scav`, `weapon-box` and `airdrop-crate` (`--seed 7`): **the open** 2.017 to 2.05 s after the panel's first frame in the takes (the game's: frame 323 at 2.9599 s, run on 324, opened on 559, 4.9835 s), 2.018 to 2.022 in the app (2.000 before, its search begun two frames before its panel); **the finds** each 2 to 12 ms over its 555 or 1111 ms in the takes, 1 to 8 ms in the app (before, from the due time: as often under as over); the toolbox's one find +2.585 s from the panel in both (2.551 before). **A hidden thing's ground** 0.414 in both (0.69 before). **The filters**: none before the open in either (the app drew them from the panel's first frame, a kind lit by what it hid); at the open all but All at 0.15, a kind lit on its find's frame. **The button's ground**: the game's from 0.892 on the panel's first frame (a 29 ms first step) to 0.628 0.08 s on, the app's from 0.948 (a 14 ms step) to 0.628 0.09 s on (1 before); at the open 0.6564, 0.6527, 0.6501 … 0.628 eleven frames on, the app's 0.6565, 0.6525, 0.6499 … 0.628 eleven frames on. **The words**: "SEARCHING" 77.21 wide and the timer at 2018.55 in both (the app's "SEARCHING..." 84.5 wide pushed the timer to 2025.84). **No tooltip** in the 2.7 s after a find under a still mouse (`airdrop-crate-search`, frame 1368), none in the app's 2.2 to 8 s, its name 0.6 s after the mouse comes onto it anew (the app's check, `tipcheck`). **The panel** 3 frames after the screen in both (2 before). **Not compared**: the loaders' turn (the dump does not hold it; the app's seen turning in step, in a capture, not measured; the takes': 3.00 degrees a frame at 8.3 ms, from upright on the search's first frame); the sound's volume (the takes record it as it starts, 0.36; the step at half a second is read from the code only: in `dead-scav-search-b`'s sound the loop lies 15 to 25 dB under the container's opening and near the raid's own sounds, and its level beside the clip's was too uneven, 6.5 dB, to show a step of 4.4); the timer's scene words on a run's first search; the button's tint's colour (the takes record its alpha only: its off colour is read from the install, and a capture of each looks alike).

## Searching a body (`PlayerSearchController`, `ActiveSearchController`, `ActiveSearchContentOperation`, `ItemsPanel.Show`, `ComplexStashPanel`, `ContainersPanel`, `EquipmentTab`, `SearchableSlotView`, `SearchableItemView`, `InventoryController.IsAllowedToSeeSlot`, `IsAllowedToSeeEquipmentSlot`, `ReplaceInventory`, `InteractionContextHelper.GetAvailableActions`, `Corpse`, `CorpseItemController`, `PlayerBody.SlotView`; read 2026-11-01, built 0.180.0)

**What is hidden.** The character's searcher uncovers only its own equipment as it is made, so another's gear is hidden from it the same way a raid's container is: a searchable thing on a body (`SearchableItem`: a `Backpack`, a `Vest`, the `Pockets`, a `LootContainer`, a `MobContainer`, by the database's class) is **unsearched** until searched, and what lies in its grids **unknown** until found, at any depth (a pouch in a vest: unknown, then unsearched). **What is in a slot is known** (`IsItemKnown`: only a searchable grid hides), so the body's gear worn shows whole, its vest and backpack themselves among it, and a weapon's parts. The body itself is no container that is searched. A thing the looter takes out keeps its state; a searchable thing put on before it was searched stays unsearched on the looter, and its row in the looter's own containers' column shows its unsearched panel (`SearchableSlotView` is the same view there).
