**What a looter sees** (`IsAllowedToSeeSlot`, `IsAllowedToSeeEquipmentSlot`): every slot of the body's equipment but the secured container (hidden from all but its owner) and a deleted slot. **A Scav's armband slot is deleted** as its inventory is made (`ReplaceInventory`, the Savage side), so its body has none; **its dog tag slot stays, and shows empty** (a Scav carries no tag; no code hides the slot from a looter: the client's rule, chosen over giving the slot to other players alone). A player's body shows its tag.

**The look's action** (`GetAvailableActions` for a `Corpse`, a loot item whose root is an equipment): one line, **SEARCH** (no TAKE, no EXAMINE), its name the corpse's (`Corpse.Init`: "Corpse", which the install's words give as "Body"), found by the look's line on the dead bodies' layer within the loot's reach (`LOOT_RAYCAST_DISTANCE` past `BEHIND_CAST`). It opens the inventory at once with the body's equipment as the loot (`Interact` succeeds for a body; `ShowInventoryScreenLoot`), no gesture; no reach is checked after.

**The panel** (`ItemsPanel.Show`: an equipment as the loot is shown by `ComplexStashPanel`, anything else by `SimpleStashPanel`): the header's toggle with the install's word for `LOOT` (`_complexGridName`), the name's box at its top right with the corpse's controller's `ContainerName` ("Corpse" in the install's words, in capitals: BODY; no nickname, no side), and its scroll view (`Containers Scrollview`, its own mask, elastic, its bar at its right) holding one column (`Content`, a `VerticalLayoutGroup`: padding 95 left, 60 above and below, 45 between): first **a copy of the player's gear panel** (`_equipmentPanelSource` is the left panel's `Gear Panel Template`, an `EquipmentTab`) with the body's slots as the looter sees them, then **a row for each container slot** the looter sees (`ContainersPanel._slotNames`: the vest, the pockets, the backpack; the secured container not), each the containers' row template (`SearchableSlotView`) as the looter's own column draws it, but with no special slots (`CreateSlots`: only one's own equipment shows them), and **the dog tag's view last in the pockets' row** (`_dogtagTemplate`, its `LayoutElement` 64 square, laid by the row after the grids, the row's spacing 7 and the pockets' 10 more). **No search begins by itself** (only `SimpleStashPanel.Show` begins one): each container's row has its own search, the button in its header's row (`Header Panel/SearchPanel`: SEARCH, or SEARCHING... with its loader, the stop button and the timer), the unsearched panel (400 by 125, in the row's place for the grids) until it has been opened, and the slot's spinner (`SlotView.SearchIcon`) while its thing is searched; a click on the panel or the button begins one where one may (one at a time, two at Search's elite), the stop button stops it, and a view shut stops its search.

**The pace** (`SearchContent`): Search's `SearchBuffSpeed` (0.01 a level) is added where the container lies in someone's equipment (`RootItem is InventoryEquipment`): a body's containers, and a searchable thing the character wears or carries (a bag put on unsearched); a crate's or one on the floor goes by Attention's alone. Search's elite `SearchDouble` lets two searches run at once (`CanStartNewSearchOperation`), each on its own clock.

**Taking off a body** (`PlayerBody.SlotView`, bound to its slot: a slot emptied destroys its model, `DestroyCurrentModel`): what is taken off is no longer drawn on the body.

**Sent toward a body** (Ctrl and a click, `ItemManipulator`'s quick find for an equipment: `GetPrioritizedContainersForLoot`, less, where the mover is not the equipment's owner, every container held by a `MobContainer`): a thing goes into the body's vest, pockets or backpack in the game's order for its kind, never into its secured container (money, which tries one's own secured container first, goes to the body's backpack).

**The tabs** (`ItemsTabController.TryHide`: leaving the gear tab or the health tab hides the items panel and closes its views, each of the two showing it afresh): a change of tab stops every search, and the loot panel's container, shown again, begins its own again where it is not yet searched.

**The range's bodies** (TRP's own: dead dummies can be looted, they are Scavs, and only the side is chosen): each dummy is given at the run's start **a Scav's body of a kit of its own** (each its own, kept as changed, from the PMCs' starting kits: a new USEC's or BEAR's starting kit with the AKM in its hands in the rifle's slot; until then a copy of what the player carried), every thing a number of its own, less the dog tag and the armband (`trp_sim::search::scav_body`), hidden as above where `SEARCHING` is on; a dead one's body offers SEARCH. Its health is a PMC's, its armour what its body has on. What is taken off its body or put on it stays so, drawn on it and kept through the debug panel's HEAL AND STAND UP (body-hits spec, "The dummy on the range"); a respawn of the player leaves the bodies' gear as it lies, every thing given a number of its own again (a thing of the player's or a crate's put on a body is back where it began under its old number), and each dummy told its things' new numbers in the same step (what it holds, held as it died and wears: `dummy::renumber_bodies`, 0.248.0; until then a respawned dummy's AKM was taken for one not in its hands, body-hits spec, "Its weapon in hand stays in its slot") and hidden again. `PROVISIONAL`: no line of sight is asked past the range's things (the game's `Linecast` against the loot's obstruction mask); a thing put on a body that the run had nowhere else at its start is not drawn on it (`dummy::show_dummies`); the body's gear panel is as high as the player's panel template's size, not its `LayoutElement`'s 780.

**Built** (`trp_sim::search` (`hide_body`, `hide_thing`, `forget`, `searchable`, `on_body`, `seen_on_body`, `scav_body`), `trp_sim::inventory::Rules::own`, `trp_data::screen::ComplexParts`, the app's `inventory_view::body_panel`, `body_list`, `row_search`, `search_body`, `health_view::draw_complex`, `draw_row_search`, `draw_dogtag_slot`, `grid_window_built`'s search, `search_view`, `dummy::follow_bodies`, `body_looked_at`): all of the above. With it, the inventory's moves tell the character's own equipment from a body's (`Rules::own`: only one's own slots refuse a thing not examined, and only onto it does the raid's limit count). `--search-body` opens the first dummy's body at the start, dead or not (a testing aid). The unsearched panel's word is the install's (`LocalizedText`, `UNSEARCHED`: the window's prefab keeps the Russian). **Not built**: whether a thing sent toward a body goes into a container of it not yet searched (the app's does; the game's quick find was not read that far); an unsearched container in one's own gear not looked into for magazines, rounds or medicine (`GetAcceptableItemsNonAlloc` goes into no unsearched `SearchableItem`); the skill's points a find gives (`FindAction.Complete(onCorpse)`; the experience notices of a body's gear, counted found as its screen shows, and of its things moved, since s187: interactions spec, "Taking a thing up"); a container's search's own sound for a body (each container's `SearchSound` is played, the game's too). **Compared with nothing** (no recording has a body searched): read from the code, the rules tested in the simulation (`tests/search.rs`, four new; `tests/sim/searchable.rs`, two on the install), the look seen in captures (`local\shots\body*_s178.png`, `bag*_s178.png`). **Compared with nothing** (no recording has a search): read from the code, the rules tested in the simulation (`tests/search.rs`, seven), the look seen in captures (`local\shots\search_*_s177.png`).

## Weight

- An item weighs its template's weight times how many are in its stack.
- An item with containers (slots, grids, a magazine's rounds) weighs its own weight plus everything in them (`ContainerCollection.TotalWeight`).
- **The figure on the GEAR and HEALTH tabs** (`HealthParametersPanel`): the carried weight below (at elite Strength, the elite sum), written to one place, over the upper overweight limit (`BaseOverweightLimits` y, 77 here) times the Strength skill's lift (`StrengthBuffLiftWeightInc`: 0.3 over 50 a level), coloured past the lower. The app leaves the skill out until the skills are built: a capture of the game has 95 at Strength level 40 (77 × 1.24), the app 77. **Compared** (2026-10-23): the sum is the game's to the gram in every recording of the kept regression (`after132`: 22, 16 and 15 items, 6.566 to 9.975 kg, 0 items differing). A "37 against 36.6" once taken for a weight was the HEALTH tab's temperature (`health.md`).
- **The character's carried weight** (`Inventory.TotalWeight`) is the sum over all fifteen slots of what is in each. With the Strength skill at its elite level it is the sum over nine of them only (TacticalVest, Pockets, Backpack, SecuredContainer, Earpiece, Headwear, Eyewear, FaceCover, ArmorVest): the weapons, the scabbard, the dogtag and the armband then weigh nothing.
- The controller reads that sum again on each event of the inventory for a thing in the equipment, a thing added, removed or refreshed, and works the weight out again where it has moved more than 0.01 kg (`PhysicalBase.LateUpdate`; `movement.md`, "The weight, as the inventory tells of it", says which changes are events: a shot is none, and its rounds count at the next); the Gear tab shows the sum as last told (`HealthParametersPanel`, on the same event). Built since 2026-10-11 (`Movement::set_weight`), on the inventory's events since 0.230.1 (`Movement::weigh`, `inventory::Weighed`): the inertia, the limits on speed and the stamina's costs are worked out again for the new weight with the character's skills as they are, the pools keep what they hold, and a character that has become fully overweight begins to pay for standing about. The curve under the walk's floor is then this character's again: in the game until another character's weight is worked out (`movement.md`, "The floor's curve is shared"), in TRP for good (each character its own, TRP's choice).

## The OVERALL tab (`OverallScreen`, `OverallStatsPanel`, `InventoryPlayerModelWithStatsWindow`, `InventoryClothingSelectionPanel`, `DropDownBox`, `BaseDropDownBox`, `CustomizationSuite`, `UpperBodySuit`, `LowerBodySuit`, `TorsoSkin`; read 2026-10-18)

**The character's light: the game's eight with `OVERALL FIGURE`; the inspect window's without**. **Built**: the eight read once with the OVERALL panel (`trp_data::screen::OverallPanel::lights`, `prism`: the camera's `CameraLightSwitcher` list followed to each `Light`, read by the one reader of a screen's lights, `edit_build::read_light`, that the edit build screen's and the inspect window's go through; each placed and turned in the view's axes from its transform up, so `PlayerMVObjectLights`' (2, 2, 1) scale moves the points and leaves their ranges), made by the one maker of a screen's lights the edit build screen's are made by (`edit_build::preview_light`: a point's fall-off and a directional's strength as there, `ESTIMATE`), each where the view has it about the OVERALL figure, on the OVERALL camera's lights' layer alone; ambient none; PRISM's filmic curve squared on the picture held to nought and one, in the game's numbers (`preview_tonemap`, the camera's tone 7, 0.3, 1 and 0.004, 0.06; no exposure, vignette or bloom, as its `PrismEffects` has them off). No other figure the game draws is lit by them (the reconnection screen's figure has a set of its own, `Lights`, four; TRP draws none), and the gear page's 3D figure is TRP's own (the game has its flat outline there): it keeps its one light. Not built: the camera's ambient occlusion (2.66, 0.05) and its undithering. Looked at beside pictures of the game (`overall_s251`): lit from the same sides (the warm key on the figure's right, the rim on its left), but lighter and flatter than the game's: Bevy lights in linear light where the game's project is in gamma space (`gamma_ui`), and the points' conversion is the edit build screen's `ESTIMATE`. The game's own (read): `Camera_inventory`'s `CameraLightSwitcher` turns on the eight lights of `PlayerMVObjectLights` (six points, a warm key at head height, a blue rim, four fills; a white front fill and a pale hair light, directional; no shadows) while it draws, the ambient forced black, no HDR (ARGB32), PRISM's filmic tonemap only. The app with `OVERALL FIGURE` off (and with it on where the eight could not be read): the inspect window's own preview's two directional lights (`inspect-window.md`, "The model"), no ambient, its photographic tonemap at 2, on a layer only the OVERALL camera sees; the gear page's figure keeps its one light (`ESTIMATE`). **Built: the eight summed as the game sums them**. How a surface takes them in the game: deferred, by its own copy of the engine's light (`Hidden/Internal-DeferredShading`, lighting spec, "Tarkov's surface shading"), every figure the gamma number (the raid's colour space is `Gamma`, `linear_intensity` false: the recording's `.scene.json`), a point light by the engine's attenuation (1/(1 + 25u), u = d²/R², faded to nought past 0.64 of the range squared), the sum clamped into the camera's eight-bit target, PRISM's filmic curve squared on those numbers. Bevy's sum in linear light made the front fill three times the game's on a face toward the camera and the key six times, and its inverse square left the key and the rim at about half the game's at the head's distance: the figure flat and light. Now, whatever `TARKOV SHADING` says (TRP's default, the player's to change), Bevy's PBR lighting is patched whenever `OVERALL FIGURE` is on (`shading::installed`), the OVERALL camera asks for the game's light (`shading::asking(0.0)`: no brightness term, the game's ambient pass being the main and optic cameras' alone), and the eight are handed in the game's units by the one maker of a screen's lights (`edit_build::preview_light`, `game`: the colour as stored, a directional at its intensity, a point at its intensity times 4π and its range; the edit build screen keeps Bevy's units and its `ESTIMATE`). The tonemap pass's write was corrected with it (`preview_tonemap.wgsl`: the curve's result written so that the picture, which encodes by the sRGB curve, stores it; before, a 0.06 was stored 0.027), which the inspect window's and the edit build screen's previews share. **Compared** (the recording `local\traces\overall_s278`, pic3 at turn 180, boxed at 624 by 890 as the app's capture `local\shots\ovl_after_s278.png`, mean Rec.709 luma of the stored numbers at the same places; the recording's kit but the suit and the rifle's parts): the helmet's shaded side 24 in the game, 52 before, 30 now; its top 44, 70, 42; its lit side 64, 78, 86; the balaclava's cheek 45, 72, 82; across the helmet at the box's row 60 (every 7 pixels) the game 39 88 57 39 34 37 15 13 15 17 15 15 49 27, before 49 94 71 64 64 48 13 20 42 33 37 50 89 38, now 47 103 80 65 57 47 9 15 23 15 18 23 57 31. The shaded side, the top and the shape across are the game's; what faces the camera is still lighter than the game's: the game's character materials (`p0/Reflective/Bumped Specular SMap_Decal`) give a face toward the camera only `_DefVals`.x of its colour (the vest 0.55, the head 0.8) and add their own specular and cube reflection, which TRP's figure did not have (Bevy's standard material's colour and gloss): built 0.250.0 ("The character's surface", below). Not built: the camera's ambient occlusion (2.66, 0.05 m), its undithering and antialiasing.

**The character's surface**. The game draws the menu figure with each material's own shader; the character's and its gear's are `p0/Reflective/Bumped Specular SMap` and its `_Decal` form (read on the vest, the BEAR turtleneck, the trousers, the head, the helmet, the balaclava, the earpiece and the glasses' frame), drawn deferred as the inspect window's preview draws an item (inspect-window spec, "The surface in the preview"): its G-buffer's colour the texel times `_Color` times kd (kd = `_DefVals`.x + `_DefVals`.y·f, f = ½(1 − c)², c the way to the eye against the normal), so that a face toward the camera takes only `_DefVals`.x of its colour (the vest 0.55, the helmet and the head 0.8); its specular the texel's alpha times `_Glossness`, ks = ½(`_SpecVals`.x + `_SpecVals`.y·f) and `_SpecColor`; its smoothness the gloss picture's red times `_Specularness`; its emission the material's own cube (`_Cube`: the helmet's and the earpiece's `patron_cubemap_metall`, the balaclava's and the glasses' `patron_cubemap_metall_matte`) at the reflection, times the texel's alpha, `_ReflectColor` and ks; all lit by the eight (above). **Built**: each install material's figures noted as it is made, with its cube read whole where it names one (`figure_surface::note`, from `render_data`'s two conversions: the body's things at the start, a suit put on, a thing worn later and the figure's own weapon read with their cubes, the cubemaps' bundle opened for them), and each piece on the OVERALL figure's layer drawn with a noted material drawn with a twin of it, one a material (`figure_surface::swap`): the game's surface in the preview's way, lit by the view's own lights through the game's light rule (`game_surface::GameSurface::lit_by_view`, `trp_game_light`), its pictures following the material's as the streaming changes their levels (`follow`). The dithered glass keeps its own twin, which in the OVERALL view now asks for the game's light whenever the patch is installed (`glass_view`); the sights' lenses keep theirs, a renderer that draws nothing its shadow-only twin. In the recording's kit: 89 materials of the figure's own, 77 with a cube of their own. `PROVISIONAL`: every install material but the glass and the lenses is drawn by the `p0` sums (the other shaders the figure wears are not read), as the inspect window's are. **Compared** (pic3 boxed as above, the app with the recording's kit, `local\shots\stepb_ovl3_s278.png`; mean luma, the game / 0.249.1 / 0.250.0): the helmet's lit side 64 / 87 / 58, its top 44 / 43 / 34, its shaded side 24 / 30 / 22; the balaclava's cheek 45 / 82 / 41; the vest's front at the left 93 / 115 / 83, at the right 39 / 63 / 37; the floor between the feet 44 / 43 / 43; the trousers 27 / 64 / 29 at the thigh and 27 / 52 / 31 at the shin (another suit: the recording's is not the app's BEAR Standard); across the helmet at the box's row 60, every 10 pixels from 260, the game 75 50 35 34 18 17 16 17 45, before 92 74 57 38 20 29 22 22 48, now 71 39 32 25 11 15 13 14 42. **What is left**: the game's picture carries a bluish term TRP's does not, at every place measured (blue 6 to 20 over TRP's, red 0 to 11), most at the helmet's top, seen at a grazing angle; the inspect window's comparison showed the same (its green and blue 3 to 7 under the game's). Not read: the deferred reflections pass with the scene's default reflection (left out, the inspect window's `ESTIMATE`), and whether the figure's picture's alpha lets the panel through. Before, the helmet's top matched in luma by an over-bright neutral (its colour whole and Bevy's grey specular), its blue 9 under the game's then too. Not built: the camera's ambient occlusion (2.66, radius 0.05 m), which darkens creases and would not close that gap.

**The shadow at the feet** (`MenuPlayerPoser.BottomShadow`, `PlayerModelLoader.CreatePlayerBody`: the very small shadow by the feet that moves as the character turns). The menu player's prefab has an object `BootShadow` on its root (not on the feet), which its `MenuPlayerPoser` names and `CreatePlayerBody` switches on by `enableBottomShadow` (true unless told; `PlayerModelView.Show` never tells it otherwise; the hideout's stash loader alone passes false). Under it five of Unity's own planes (`unity default resources` 10209: 10 by 10 m, its normal up), each a `MeshRenderer` on layer 19, so they stand where the idle's soles stand and **turn with the model** as it is dragged, without following the animated feet. In the root's axes (y up, z the model's forward): `Over` at (-0.023, 0, -0.015), turned 47.8° about y, scaled 0.217 by 0.186 (2.17 by 1.86 m), and `Over (1)` (0.207, 0.009, -0.095), 47.8°, 0.61 by 0.62 m, and `Over (2)` (-0.241, 0.009, 0.148), 21.9°, 0.94 by 0.89 m, the three drawn with `boot_shadow2` (a soft round blob, its alpha at most 189 of 255); `Left` (-0.230, 0.001, 0.153), -3.0°, 0.37 by 0.37 m, and `Right` (0.214, 0.001, -0.107), 39.3°, 0.43 by 0.41 m, with `boot_shadow` (a sole's shape, solid in its core). Both pictures are 256 by 256, DXT5, black, the shape in their alpha. Both materials name `Unlit/Transparent Colored` (the shaders' bundle; read with `fxc`'s disassembly of its programs): the texel times the vertex colour (white: the plane has none), blended `SrcAlpha, OneMinusSrcAlpha` **for colour and alpha alike**, depth tested, not written, both faces, offset -1, -1, drawn after what is opaque (the materials' other figures are left over from another shader and unread). Over the camera's cleared picture a plane so leaves black at an alpha of a² (a its texel's), and the page lays the picture over the panel by its alpha in the screens' gamma numbers: the panel times 1 − a² (under a sole black; under the soft blob's middle 0.45 of the panel). **Built**: read with the figure on a worker, from the install (`trp_data::body::boot_shadow`: the object the poser names, each object below it with its own place, turn, scale and flag, its mesh the prefab's or Unity's own by the default resources' route, its material checked to be drawn by that shader, its picture and that picture's scale and offset), made on the OVERALL figure's root on its layer once both are there (`boot_shadow`), drawn by the standard material unlit and blended, its alpha blended as its colour and its depth offset as the shader's (`AlphaAsColour::specialize`); under `OVERALL FIGURE` (no switch of its own: the main session's default). `PROVISIONAL`: the planes are sorted as Bevy sorts what is blended, by their middles' distance (the engine's order not read), which moves the overlaps a little. **Compared** (the recording's pic3, and pic5 at turn 85 against a drag of 317 pixels, `local\shots\feet_turned_pair_s278.png`): the floor's luma across the box at its row 770, every 20 pixels from 100, the game 69 67 64 60 56 51 47 45 45 47 48 44 38 35 20 14 34 41 51 62 70 75 78 82, TRP 76 72 68 64 59 53 49 45 44 44 44 42 38 36 16 20 34 41 52 64 71 76 78 79 (before, the panel's 80 everywhere but the boots); between the feet 43 against the game's 44 (the panels behind 79 and 80 in both); the halo and the two soles' marks turn with the figure as the game's do. The panels behind differ (the game's a blue picture, TRP's the blurred range), so the halo's figures are near only where the panels are.

The OVERALL page is built with its statistics dimmed, as nothing tracks them, and with the clothing dropdowns to change the character's look: every side's suits offered to any character, each loaded only when chosen, as the app's other assets are.

**What the page is** (the inventory scene's `InventoryScreen/Overall Panel`, `probe scenepanel level44 "InventoryScreen/Overall Panel" 1920 1080`): on the left (`LeftSide`, a column 624 wide from (23, 48) at 1920 by 1080), the character panel and under it the clothing panel; on the right (`RightSide`) the statistics; a back button.

- **The character panel** (`CharacterPanel`): the character drawn by a camera of its own into the panel (`PlayerModelView`, turned by a drag across it: `DragTrigger`, `XCoordRotation`), on a ground of black at half alpha, a tile along its foot (`bottom_bar_tile`); over it the level panel (`Level Panel`: the rank's icon, the level in large figures, and six short statistics, `ShortStatsPanel`: raids, kills, the rate of leaving, of surviving, kills to deaths, time online), the side's icon (`icon_usec`, `icon_bear`, none for a Scav) and the prestige's, and at its foot the nickname and the experience.
- **The clothing panel** (`ClothingPanel`, 60 high, switched off in the scene): two halves, upper (`upper_body_icon` and a dropdown) and lower (`lower_body_icon` and a dropdown).
- **The statistics** (`OverallStatsPanel`, `StatisticsSpawn`): three toggles (OVERALL, PMC, SCAV), two lines of the dashboard and a scrolling list, all from the profile's counters.

**The clothing** (`InventoryPlayerModelWithStatsWindow.ShowClothingPanel`): shown only out of a raid. The suits offered are those of the character's side (`CustomizationSolver.GetAvailableSuites(side)`), split into the upper ones (`UpperBodySuit`: a body and the hands that go with it) and the lower ones (`LowerBodySuit`: the legs); each dropdown lists its suits by their names (the locale's `<id> Name`), the one worn chosen (the suit whose body, or legs, the character has on). Choosing one puts its parts on the character (an upper suit its body and hands, a lower one its legs: `SetClothingsToProfile`) and draws the preview afresh.

**A dropdown** (`DropDownBox`, `BaseDropDownBox`): a box 31 high (its ground black at 0.67, the chosen suit's name in it, an 18 by 18 button at its right with an arrow down, up while open); a click on it opens or shuts the list (the game's sound `MenuDropdown` as it opens). The list hangs under the box (`Open Panel`), its lines made from a template line 28 high, at most 120 units of them in sight and the rest scrolled (`ScrollRectNoDrag`, a bar 10 wide at its right); a click on a line chooses it (the sound `MenuDropdownSelect`) and shuts the list; a click anywhere off it shuts it; the box's ground lights while the pointer is over it (`_highlightedColor`).

**A suit's parts** (the install's customization list, `templates/customization.json`: 95 upper suits and 70 lower, of the sides `Usec`, `Bear` and `Savage`, some of two or three): each part's bundle holds its mesh as one `Skin`, or levels of detail (`LoddedSkin`) of `Skin`s or of `TorsoSkin`s. **A torso of the newer kind** (`TorsoSkin`; 54 of the parts) names its `Skin` and three meshes, of which the game draws one by what is worn (`TorsoSkin.ApplySkin`): the armour's (`_armor`) where body armour is worn that is not vest-like, the vest's (`_vest`) where vest-like armour, a tactical vest or a backpack is, the bare one (`_base`) otherwise. Every part hangs on bones of the character's skeleton: over all 165 parts, the default suits' bones and ten more (the ribcage, `Base HumanGear1` to `5` and `4_1`, `Weapon_root`, `Base HumanBackpack`, the root).

**This work's, by choice**: the clothing is offered in a raid, on the OVERALL tab; every side's suits are offered to any character, by their names in one list each (by name: the game lists a side's own in its solver's order); a suit's parts are read when it is first chosen (lazy loading); the statistics are drawn dimmed, nothing of them kept. A suit the locale has no name for is listed by its own (`_name`): the game never lists one so.

**The model's view** (`PlayerModelView`, `CameraImage`, `PlayerModelLoader`, `XCoordRotation`): the character is put in the view's own axes 1 m below it and 5 m on, turned half round (`Show`: `position` unless told; `CreatePlayerBody`), the view's scale undone so that it stands in metres (`Update`). Its camera is the scene's (`CameraImage.targetCamera`, the object `Camera_inventory` under `PlayerMVObject`, the view's first child): 28 degrees up and down, its planes 0.3 and 25 m, clearing to black at none, no antialiasing, drawing the layer the model is put on (`WeaponPreview`), at (0, -0.2, 0.49) in the view's axes, unturned: 0.8 m above the feet and 4.51 m before them, looking level (the camera's fields by the engine's 2022.3 layout of a camera, `typetree_2022.3.43f1.json`). It draws onto a picture twice the view's size in the screen's units (`RecreateTexture`), shown in the view. A drag across the view turns the model (`InventoryPlayerModelWithStatsWindow.DragHandler`): its turn less the pointer's move across, in the screen's pixels, times `_rotationSpeed` (0.3 in the scene), kept within a turn (`ClampYaw`); the model made anew (shown, or a suit put on) is at its own turn (`Init` reads it). The drag begins once the pointer has moved the engine's threshold from where it was pressed, and turns the model by each frame's move from that frame on (`PointerEventData.delta`; the engine's input module sends the drag on the frame it begins, `ProcessDrag`, from its published source).

**The hold and the random looks** (`PlayerModelLoader.CreateWeapon`, `GetItemInHands`, `MenuPlayerPoser.ChangeWeapon`, `UpdatePersonGrip`, `LateUpdate`, `OnRandomizeAnimation`, `TurnOffLights`, `PlayerBones.ShiftWeaponRoot`, `Kinematics`, `TransformLinks.GatherIK`, `HandPoser.SimpleGrip`, `GripBlender`, `Player.ValueBlender`; each checked as called; read 2026-11-09, built in the simulation in 0.239.0, `trp_sim::figure`, its weapon moves in 0.241.0; drawn by the app since 0.240.0 with `OVERALL FIGURE`, "Built" below). **What it holds**: the item in hand in a raid, else the first of the two primary slots, the holster and the scabbard. Its model is the item's own prefab, made animated (`ObjectsFactory.CreateItem(.., isAnimated: true)`), held only where that is a weapon's prefab with its animator: a firearm, a thrown thing, a knife, the rangefinder, a radio transmitter (`CreateItemAsync`'s list of the items whose prefab is a `WeaponPrefab`). **Medicine, food and drink** are made as their loot models (`Prefab`, not the hands' `UsePrefab`), which are no weapon's prefab: the body is still told the item's kind (0, the held idle, its arms as the clip has them), nothing is hung in its hands or solved, and no look is ever picked (no weapon animator). A knife, a thrown thing and the rangefinder are no `Weapon` (the game's `Knife`, `ThrowWeap` and `PortableRangeFinder` are items of their own): their animators are told nothing of a stock, a magazine or a chamber, and their one move is `Look`. The rangefinder's controller has no `PATROL`: asked for it, it stays where it is (its `SPAWN`, then its idle once `Active`). The kind the body is told (`WeaponType`, `WeaponTypeFloat`, `UI_WeaponType`): −1 for nothing or the empty hands, 1 for a `Pistol`, 2 for a thrown thing, 3 for a thing with a knife's component, 0 for anything else, **a revolver among them** (the game's `Revolver` is no `Pistol`: the Rhino's take has 0). A weapon's model is the item's animated one, hung from the ribcage at nought; its animator is asked for `PATROL` on its hands' layer, told the stock's fold (the `Stock` layer's weight and `StockFolded`), the magazine's capacity (`AmmoInMag`, 0 with none), whether a magazine is in (`MagInWeapon`), the rounds chambered (`AmmoInChamber`), the magazine's reload clips (`RelTypeOld`, `RelTypeNew`, with a magazine), updated by 3 s, then told it is on (`Active`) and on patrol (`Patrol`), asked for `PATROL` again (so the hands' layer enters it on the next frame and plays none of that frame) and told whether the weapon reloads by its magazine (`CanReload`: a magazine, a reload mode neither `OnlyBarrel` nor `InternalMagazine`, no rocket launcher). The weapon moves a pick chooses among: `Look`, then `CheckChamber` (no rocket launcher), then `CheckAmmo` and `Reload` (where it reloads). **Every frame after both animators** (the poser's late update, only with a weapon animator: the empty hands are the body's animation alone): where the body's `Hand_Right` curve keeps any of the right hand, the weapon is hung by its right marker from the right palm (the marker on the palm, turned as it); else, with `Weapon_3rd` over 0.01, its `Weapon_root_anim` goes to the body's `Weapon_root_3rd_anim` by that share (the menu's `PlayerBones` has no offset and no extra turn); else it stays as its animator has it. The body's elbow goals go toward the weapon's by one less each `Elbow_*` curve. Each arm (its weight one less its `Hand_*` curve) is solved onto its marker by the menu prefab's own limb solvers (the player's settings: toward the goal, at full weight, keeping none of the palm's turn) and its forearm's twist bones relaxed by its own `TwistRelax` (crossfades 0.3 and 0.7 on the left, 0.33 and 0.7 on the right). **The grips**: the left hand takes, of the part grips for it that are on, an underbarrel weapon's first and then the deepest: where it has both a special grip (alternative or an underbarrel weapon's) and a common one, a blend of the two by `Patrol` (on patrol the special one), which counts as alternative; the right takes its alternative grip first, then the deepest. A hand is pulled onto its grip by how near its marker is to its reference (wholly at nought, not at all from 0.1 m), the distance of an alternative grip times one less a value that stands at 1 on patrol, so **on patrol the left hand is wholly on a special grip**: the AKM with the Zenit B-10M handguard holds its left palm on the handguard's alternative grip, 4 cm from the marker. The fingers: the left hand's by the left arm's weight, the right's always whole, each toward the weapon's own hand's finger, or the grip's by the hand's share of it. **The looks**: near the end of each 7.33 s loop of the idle (7.3048 s, its clips' event), with a weapon animator, the poser draws `Random.Range(0, 3)`: 0 nothing (`UI_RandomTransition` 0); 1 a transition `Random.Range(1, 5)` (1 `LookOut`, 2 `LookOut_Subtle`, 3 `LookOut_Alert`, 4 only for a thrown thing or a knife, `WeaponCheck_Melee`; the value stays set until the next pick); 2 a weapon move, `Random.Range(0, moves)`; never 3, the gestures. Without a weapon animator nothing is drawn and the idle plays on. **A weapon move** (`OnRandomizeAnimation`, `TurnOffLights`, `OnWeaponOperationStart`, `OnWeaponOperationEnd`, `AnimatorEventHandler` and the poser's `IEventsConsumer`, the `Patrol` setter): a move whose trigger the weapon's animator lacks is put back to none and plays nothing (`UI_RandomTransition` 0). Otherwise `UI_WeaponOperation` goes on, `UI_RandomTransition` to 0, the weapon off patrol (its animator's `Patrol` off, the left grip's blend aimed at the common grip, the alternative grip's hold aimed at nought, each at 2 a second from its value then) and the first `IdleStart` awaited. The body leaves `GUI_IDLE` for `Hands_Operation_In` at once where the kind is 0 or 1 (on the frame after the pick: the event comes after both animators' update), the weapon its `PATROL` by `PATROL OUT` for its `IDLE`, whose `IdleStart` only clears the wait. At the In clip's event (1.0732 s) the move's trigger is raised on the weapon's animator (the last move picked: it is never put back), which enters the move's state on the next frame (the AKM's `LOOK` 3.333 s, `CHECK CHAMBER`, `CHECK` 4.333 s, `RELOAD START` and the reload's states; the Rhino's check of its cylinder); with none ever picked the start ends the move at once. Back in its `IDLE`, the weapon's `IdleStart` (handed to the poser with its other marks in its late update, each if its conditions hold as the ones before left them) puts `UI_WeaponOperation` off: the body leaves its loop for `Hands_Operation_Out` on the next frame, whose event (0.2567 s) puts the weapon back on patrol (`PATROL IN` on the next frame, the blends aimed back) and tells it again whether it can reload, and `GUI_IDLE` comes at Out's end (1.5 s), its loop from nought, the next pick 7.3048 s on. **A reload's rounds**: each `AddAmmoInMag` or `AddAmmoInChamber` the weapon's animator hands on counts down from a draw of 2 to 10 (`RandomAmmoCount`, `Random.Range(2, 11)`, at the first), and at nought tells the weapon it can reload no more and draws again (the AKM with its chamber empty charges its bolt and counts one). **A thrown thing or a knife**: its pick of a move still turns the weapon's patrol off and `UI_WeaponOperation` on, but the body never enters `Hands_Operation_In` (it wants a kind below 2), so the move's trigger is never raised and the weapon stays off patrol in its idle, `UI_WeaponOperation` on, until the figure is made anew; its random 4 plays `WeaponCheck_Melee` (`inv_idle_handsdown_weaponcheck`, 7.33 s, no event). **Nothing is heard**: the weapon's other marks (its `Sound` ones among them, the cylinder's button, its opening and closing, the hands on and off) reach the poser, which does nothing with them, and no sound player is on the figure's weapon (the weapon's `BaseSoundPlayer` is made ready only by a player or an observed player: `Player`, `ObservedPlayerHandsController`); the Rhino's take holds the fourteen `Sound` marks of its two checks and no sound of theirs (its trace's `sounds` and `plain_sounds` list none; its tape, `anything-023818.wav`, stays at the room's level, an RMS of 0.00015 against the backpack's opening's 0.0037, over both checks). The body's states and their times: `body-animator.md`, "The stance of the game's screens". **This work's**: the draws are Unity's generator from a seed of the host's (the game's draws share its one generator, whose state is not known: a reload's count among them). **Compared with** three takes of recorder 0.17.0 (`overall2_s252`), frame by frame from the making, each take's recorded picks steered in and each frame stepped by the engine's own frame time (the trace's `dt`, paired by frame: `the_figure_as_the_three_takes_recorded_it`): the AKM over all 6,979 frames, **the Rhino over all 6,662** (its two checks of the cylinder, picked on frames 1960 and 3447, an idle pick and a `LookOut_Subtle` among them; 0.241.0, where 0.239.0 stopped at its frame 1960), the empty hands over all 6,737: every recorded layer of the body in the game's state and entering the game's on every frame, the `GUI` layer's state on every frame, the weapon's hands' state on every frame (the Rhino's `PATROL`, `PATROL OUT`, `IDLE`, its check, `IDLE`, `PATROL IN`), every layer's time within 0.000002 s, the five curves exact, every pick on the recorded frame (AKM 6, Rhino 5, empty hands 7), **everything that reached the poser on its frame and in its order** (the Rhino's 5 picks, 2 starts and 2 ends of the move, 4 `IdleStart`, 2 `StartUtilityOperation` and its 14 `Sound` marks by their names; the AKM's 6 picks, the empty hands' 7), every recorded bone within 0.36 mm (the palms 0.12 to 0.36 mm, the markers 0.19 to 0.22, the elbow goals 0.12 to 0.20, the feet 0.07 to 0.11, the head 0.12; the Rhino's largest after its first move, at its frame 6814) and 0.18 degrees. Tested from the install besides (`tests/sim/figure.rs`): a look out steered in plays from the loop's end and back to the idle 7.33 s later; **the AKM's four moves** each steered in, every step on its frame as above (the look 3.333 s and the magazine's check 4.333 s whole, the check of the chamber, the reload charging its empty chamber and counting its round), the next pick 7.3048 s after the idle comes again; **a knife (the ER bayonet) and a thrown thing (the F-1)** each hung by its right marker from the right palm, no arm solved, `WeaponCheck_Melee` from 7.35 to 14.68 s for a random 4 and no `Hands_Operation_*` for a move; the rangefinder held by both hands on its markers with no `PATROL`. Read from the code only: the fingers, a knife's and a grenade's hold beyond the install's tests, medicine and food held as nothing, the folded stock and the chamber's figures, a reload's count (no take had them).

**What the page's scripts write** (`InventoryPlayerModelWithStatsWindow.Show`, `PlayerLevelPanel.Set`, `ShortStatsPanel.SetStats`, `OverallStatsPanel.FillStats`): the level as two figures and its rank's picture (`Level Icons/rank` and the level's next five up, at most 80), the nickname, the experience in groups of three, the side's picture (`_bearSprite`, `_usecSprite`, `_noSprite` for a Scav; at its own size), the prestige's (`_noSprite`, the scene's `Cross`, for none), the short statistics as their localized formats with the figures (`Raids Short` and the rest: TextMeshPro's colour tags round the figure), and the dashboard's counts, a rate with no raids `-`, the online time as `{0}h{1:00}m`. The dropdowns are each told their suit as the page shows, which plays the choosing sound for each (`PrepareDropDownBox`: `UpdateValue` with its sound), the first of the list where the suit worn is not among them.

**A dropdown, read further** (`DropDownBox`, `BaseDropDownBox`): the box's ground lit under the mouse or over its list is its colour times `_highlightedColor` (0.24 grey at 0.67: `CrossFadeColor` with its alpha, the engine's multiply), so the black box at 0.67 goes to 0.45; the list's panel is put on the menus' layer as it opens (over all of the page), its scroll area as high as `_maxVisibleHeight` (120) or its lines, its column of lines with its padding (2 left, 1 right) and 2 between, each line its template's copy (`_elementTemplate`, 28 high: its ground grey at 0.15, its words 14 in grey), the bar 10 wide; a line's button's transition is an animator's it has none of, so nothing about it changes under the mouse; any button pressed off the box and its list shuts it; the list's content is not put back to its top as it shuts.

**As the page shows** (`OverallScreen.Show`, `PlayerModelView.Show`, `PlayerModelLoader.Load`, `ProgressSpinner`, `StatisticsSpawn.SpawnStats`, `AnimatedToggle`; read 2026-11-02, with `courses10`'s `inventory-tabs`, the page on the click's release, frame 2464). **The model is made anew** at every showing, and the view's spinner (`Progress Spinner`) shows from then until it is in (`_progressSpinner.Show()`, closed by `OnLoadingCompleted`): its fill at the loading's share (0 as it shows, the bundles' share times 0.9 as they come, 0.9 once they are had, `SetProgress`), its fill growing and turning by an animation of its own; in the take five frames, the fill 0.8308 on the click's frame and 0.9 on the fifth, the box 70.67 to 93.36 pixels and turned −7 to −24 degrees, gone on the sixth. The time is the loading's, not a figure. **The chosen toggle's look comes in over 0.1 s** (its animator's `On`: in the take its ground `button_back_big` from alpha 0 by 0.1965, 0.3451, 0.498 … to 1 on the eighth frame after, and its label from (0.7725, 0.7647, 0.698) to black on the same frames, linear in time from the click's frame). **The statistics come one section a frame** (`SpawnStats` → `UI.AddViewListAsync`, as the skills' views: "Common stats" on the click's frame, then "Health and physical condition", "Looting", "Combat"). Built of these: the spinner at every showing, until the model made anew for it is drawn (`overall_view`, `figure_view::OverallFigure::remake`; the app has no loading, and its drawing takes its own frames, 3 at the least), the model's picture not in its view meanwhile, as the game's is not in its until it loads; no switch of its own (it is the page's, under `TARKOV UI`, as the model is). The app's run (`local\run_tabs_s186.sh`): the spinner on the click's frame and three more, the model in on the fifth (the game's: five, the model in on the sixth). Not built: the spinner's fill and its animation (the app's spinner is the scene's, its fill drawn whole, as while a suit is read); the toggles' look and its fade, the app not drawing the toggles the game spawns (`UIAnimatedToggleSpawner._object`, `sharedassets44.assets` 8925: a ground, a size label and a label) but the scene's own placeholders, dimmed; the sections one a frame, the app drawing no sections at all (only the dashboard over them, dimmed): both would be new pieces first.

**Built (2026-11-10): the box the game's size, the name, the statistics plain**. **The clothing panel below the box** (`health_view::below_the_box`): left out of `LeftSide`'s column (as `LayoutElement.ignoreLayout`), so the character panel keeps the height the game gives it with the panel off (624 by 891 at 1920x1080), and laid where the column would lay it after the box: the column's spacing (8) below its foot, as wide as it, its own 60 high. A list that would then run past the screen's foot (every screen is 1080 units high: the boxes' foot is at 992, a list 122 high) opens above its box, its layout mirrored about the box (the game's `DropDownBox` only drops down; where the panel is is TRP's departure). **Each list its own bar** (`overall_view::bar_of`): the scroll view read with the panel is the upper dropdown's, and until 0.248.0 both lists drew and held its bar, the lower list's handle under the upper box. **The name's and the experience's rows** (`BottomField`: `NicknameAndKarma`, `Experience`, each a `HorizontalLayoutGroup` with a `ContentSizeFitter`, middle-centred, 5 apart; the field 10 apart): laid as the game lays them, the experience's icon as large as its sprite (an image's preferred size, 44 by 31) and each label as wide and high as its words (TextMeshPro's preferred size, set on a copy of the panel each time the page is drawn); until 0.248.0 the icon was laid 0 by 0 and not seen. The name (`_nicknameLabel`) is the options' `CHARACTER NAME` (the last row of the FEATURES page's CHARACTER section, since 0.260.0; in a section of its own, IDENTITY, before; taken at once; kept as `"character_name"`; the newest profile's `Info.Nickname` until one is kept; at most 15 letters, `ESTIMATE`, the game's field's `characterLimit` not read), written in the colour of the profile's member category with that category's mark in the crown's place (`ChatSpecialIcon.Show`, the name coloured: `ChatSpecialIconSettings.GetDataByMemberCategory`, of the category's flags the first in its `Priority`, its `IconSprite` and `IconColor`; the object `ChatSpecialIconSettings` in `resources.assets`, `hard_settings::MemberIcons`; the category the profile's `Info.SelectedMemberCategory`, none for a Scav and without a profile; the recorded profile's, 2, the gold crown, (0.792, 0.541, 0)). The experience as the window writes it (`#,0` with a space between the thousands). **The statistics**: every figure of the short statistics and the dashboard, the online time and the account's lifetime written as the game's `-` for a figure not had, a hole's unit going with it (`S/R: -`, not `S/R: -%`), and drawn whole (dimmed at 0.3 from 0.69.0 to 0.247.0). **Compared** with the recording's pic 3 scaled to 1920x1080: the box, the rank icon, the level, the short statistics, the BEAR badge (100,247), the prestige's cross (100,385), the crown (297,864) and the name (from 316) at the game's places to the pixel.

**Built (2026-11-09): the OVERALL figure as the game's menu figure**. A figure of its own beside the gear page's: the body's pieces again by their handles on the menu player's skeleton (`menuplayer.bundle`'s bones, `trp_sim::figure::FigureData::paths`, the body's meshes' bones among them), on a layer only the OVERALL camera sees; every piece put on later (a suit, a worn thing read later, a part fitted to the head) goes on it too, shown as the body's is; its outfits' skeletons posed on it as it moves. **At each showing** (the page shown anew, `OverallFigure::remake`) it is made anew from what is in hand then (`HandsHeld`): a firearm's model assembled for the item's build, its backup sights as any model of it is made, a grenade's or a melee weapon's own prefab, read away from the frame and kept for the next showing (the spinner shows meanwhile, as the game's while its model loads); the body told the item's kind, the weapon's animator its magazine, chamber (`ChamberAmmoCount`: a revolver's cylinder none) and stock (`trp_sim::figure::Loaded`); the weapon's model hung on the ribcage at nought, its rig posed by the figure every frame; nothing for the empty hands. Its hung weapons are the character's but the item it was made holding, so the weapon it holds is never also hung (`slung_view::Body::Overall`). **Its clock** runs from nought on the frame its picture is first shown (the spinner going: the game's figure is made as its spinner goes), stepped by the frame's time, its picks from a seeded source (a capture's `--seed`; else the clock); **its camera draws every frame while the page is shown** (the game's animator runs on); its turn as before (the drag at 0.3 degrees a pixel, half round at each showing). Shut, its camera is off and its weapon's model goes. As the run begins it is drawn a few frames into the small picture after the gear page's drawing (the two one after the other, as before: a picture's level goes by the cameras drawing it, `streaming`). Off, the OVERALL tab draws the gear page's still figure by the inspect window's light, as before. **Since 0.241.0**: the weapon moves as above; what it holds by the game's rule (the rangefinder and a radio transmitter by their own prefabs, told 0; medicine, food and drink told 0 with nothing made for the hands; `figure_view::held_now`); **its weapon's devices as the item has them** (`tactical_view::show_figure_devices`: each device's model shows its state, `mode_000` off or its mode's object, as the game's factory makes any item's, `TacticalComboVisualController.UpdateBeams`; its light made once for each model and lit as its device stands, on the figure's own layers, its pieces' and its lights', so seen by the figure's camera alone and lighting the figure, for its camera's exposure); **its magazine's rounds and its chambered round** (`magazine_rounds`, `Owner::Figure`: the points under its magazine's slot filled by the item's count and kinds, the chamber's round on its slot's object, on the figure's layer, with `MAGAZINE ROUNDS`, as a hung weapon's; a cylinder's not built); nothing heard (above). `--figure-pick <idle|random:N|move:N>` steers the first picks for a capture. **Compared with** the pictures of `anything-012229` (`overall_s251`), in their kit (the AKM as recorded, with its Zenit B-10M handguard and light, the Rhino in the holster, the helmet, the rig, the Berkut; `local\akm_overall_s264.json`), at 1920 by 1080, the figure's clock held at each picture's time (`--figure-hold`) and turned as each: pictures 5, 6, 11 (turn 0), 16, 19, 20 (+35.7 to +40.2 degrees), 7, 8, 9 (−63.3, −190.5, −287.1): every one the same hold, facing and foreshortening as the game's; after scaling by the views' heights (891 against the app's 823, the dropdowns kept), the muzzle within 1 to 6 pixels of the game's, the left hand 2 to 12, the magazine and the stock 2, the face 4 to 10 lower in the game's (its head another; the hands and the face read by eye, a few pixels either way); between the turn-0 pictures the app's muzzle rises 4 px from 0.29 to 1.29 s and the hands move 5 px by 6.31 s, as the brief measured the game's. The light: above. The gear page's picture with the switch on is the same, pixel for pixel, as with it off.

**Built (2026-10-18)**: the tab as the game's (`TARKOV UI`; `health_view::draw_overall`, `overall_view`), the model drawn by the scene's camera into its view, the statistics dimmed by choice (their groups at 0.3, the alpha the game's screens give what does not work) with the figures as the scripts write them for a profile with nothing counted, the dropdowns as above with their sounds, a suit read when first chosen on another thread and put on (`clothing`): an upper suit's body in place of the body's top, on the body, both models and the corpse, and its hands in place of the hands the character's own eyes see; a lower suit's legs; a torso of the newer kind with its three meshes, the one for what is worn drawn (`body_view::show_torso`), armour taken as vest-like where its bundle has a `VestlikeArmor`. Without `TARKOV UI`, the app's own look at the game's places. The choice is kept in `local\options.json` and put on as the next run begins (`--suit` over it).

**When the range's things and the texts are made** (the app's own rule: what is best made at the start). The weapon tables' cases and boxes, the storage cabinets and the range's other containers are filled as the run starts, as before: they are data alone, the run's own weapon and kit are taken from them at the start, and made at a first opening they would put their making on that frame; what they cost was the search for room, now 70 ms for all of them with every thing where it was. The install's texts are read once as the run starts (`trp_data::inventory::Locale`) and kept as every key's words in small letters, which the item texts, the screens (on their thread), the options, the menus and the edit build screen take theirs from; the inspect window's own preview is read once with the rest of the screens, on their thread (`HealthLook::inspect`), for the OVERALL tab's character, the inspect window and the edit build screen.

**When the model and the corpse are made** (the app's own rule: made at the start where that saves much of the frame's time and its stutters). The model (the gear page's and the OVERALL tab's) and the corpse are built with the body as the run starts, each on a skeleton of its own from the body's own meshes and pictures by their handles (about 1 ms of the start between them); the corpse is hidden and out of the engine's work until a death. Built later, at the first opening or at the death, each would put that work and its first drawing on the frame it is wanted. The OVERALL tab's camera is made as the inventory's screens are, and the model drawn once as the run begins into a picture of 16 by 16 pixels, which builds the renderer's pipelines its drawings take without the view's textures at their whole size; the page's first showing makes the picture at its own size and draws the model anew, as every showing does. Measured: the first opening drawn on the same frames, pixel for pixel; 64 to 72 MB less of the graphics card's memory held until then; the camera made at that showing instead cost 24 to 30 ms on that frame and the next.

## What the install holds

- `templates/items.json`: for every item its class (parent), weight, width and height, extra size, slots with their filters, grids with their sizes and filters, and the slots it blocks. `probe item <id or part of a name>` prints one.
- **A thing made whole with no stock build** (`ItemFactory.CreateAndFillItem`; read 2026-11-07): where the game makes one of every item (`CreateAllItemsEver`: the profile editor's list, the loot test), it makes the kind and puts into each slot that has a locked filter the plate its first filter naming one names (`Plate`), with no rule asked (`AddWithoutRestrictions`), the plate itself not filled in turn. Six helmets of the install have no stock build (the FLUX and its MultiCam, the two Strike helmets, the winter 6B47, the Christmas Maska-1SCh); made bare, their inserts' slots (`_required`) are empty and the character refuses them (`MissingParts`). The app makes what has no stock build so in the range's storage and in loot (`Item::fill_locked`).
- **Unfinished items hidden** (read and built 2026-11-07: things the game has not finished are hidden by a switch, on by default, the GK-01, the KAC QDC 6.5x35, the NXS 2.5 and the Sparrow 22 among them, which the live game's wiki says cannot be had): SPT keeps a list of things it never hands out (`configs/item.json`, `blacklist`, 65 things; `ItemFilterService.IsItemBlacklisted`, which its traders, flea market, bots and rewards go by): the four, the ELCAN Specter and PAG-17 scopes, the GP-34, the infected's arms (the knife with no picture), the NSV and its belt and rounds, the AXMC's parts marked not to be used, shrapnel, test keys and balaclavas, the Sanitar kit, the Dead Blow Hammer, signal flares and the like; none has a flea price. The game's own edit build screen lists every part there is (`ItemFactory.CreateAllModsEver`). **Here**: with `HIDE UNFINISHED ITEMS` on (as it comes), the list (`trp_data::loot::item_blacklist`, `GameData::unfinished`) is left off the range's weapon, medical and grenade tables, their magazines and rounds, the gear cabinets and both benches' lists; the crates keep SPT's own fill, whose Customs tables name three of them. Off, everything is laid out as before.
- `templates/profiles.json`: for each edition of the game and each side, the character a new account starts with, and in it the whole tree of its inventory as a flat list: each entry an id, a template, the id of what it is in, the slot or grid it is in there (`slotId`), for a grid the cell and the turn (`location`), and for a stack how many (`upd.StackObjectsCount`). The entry whose id is the inventory's `equipment` is the equipment item itself. `probe json templates/profiles.json /Standard/usec/character/Inventory 1` shows one.

The Standard edition's starting character wears (read 2026-10-11): a rifle on the sling, a knife, a face cover, a helmet, a headset, body armour, a tactical vest, a backpack, the pockets and a secured container, with magazines, rounds and medicine in the vest, pockets and container.

## Built (2026-10-11)

- `inventory`: reads the equipment tree of a starting character from the install (`Loadout::load`), a weapon as its stock preset has it (`Loadout::of_preset`), an equipment list out of a recording (`Loadout::of_recording`), each item's class chain, weight, size and extra size, slots (with whether one is required), grids, blocked slots, stack size, whether a part may be changed in a raid, the slots it stays in, and the game's own short names for items (`short_names`, from the install's English texts).
- `screen`: the install's scenes by name, a scene's objects with their screen layout, and the inventory screen's equipment panel (`GearPanel`).
- `inventory`: the model with no engine types: items with slots and grids, the slot's and the grid's rules above, finding room, an item's size and weight, and the character's carried weight both ways; and since 2026-10-11 **moving** (`move_item`, by the nine refusals above bar the fifth), **dropping one thing on another** (`apply_item`: merge, put into, top up), the rules of a raid with the install's limit on backpacks (`Rules::of`), and a recorded equipment list set beside the model (`Equipment::of_recording`). Everything that stands by itself (the equipment, an open container) is a root, and a move goes between roots or within one. A move that is refused changes nothing.
- The app:
  - **It starts bare**: nothing worn, nothing in hand. `--loadout <edition>/<side>` still gives a starting character's outfit.
  - **The crate is larger inside and holds every weapon** (every weapon added goes into the airdrop crate, made larger inside with a scroll bar): ten cells across as the install's, and thirty down where the install's has twelve, which is the app's and not the game's; each of the app's weapons as its stock preset has it, with no round chambered; two full stacks of its own round for each weapon the kit is not made for; and the key of the range's locked door. Its panel in the screen is as high as the screen leaves and is scrolled by the wheel, a cell a notch, or by the bar at its side (`scroll_loot`). A run begun with `--equip` or to make a capture puts on the weapon it was begun with, the vest and the backpack, as before.
  - **Each container opens at its top** since 0.9.1: the panel's scroll had been one for every container; now the screen opened on a container, or another one shown, scrolls it back up (`scroll_loot`). Seen in captures: the rifles' case scrolled down to the Mk 17s, shut and opened again, at its top.
  - **The tables stand in an L, and the airdrop crate is the clothing's** (the last four weapon sets turned 90 degrees to the right, the green box labelled for clothing and given a lid, the AKM in the rifles' case). The rifles', submachine guns', machine guns' and snipers' tables stand along the wall as before; the shotguns', pistols', launchers' and custom weapons' in an arm turned towards the start, 6.8 m to the right of the lane, the first 0.4 m from the wall, their fronts to the lane and their backs to the right (`range::weapon_table`; the app's). The airdrop crate is labelled `CLOTHING`, over it and on the screen, and has a lid hinged along its back edge that opens to 80 degrees with the gestures of a lid hinged up (`ESTIMATE`, `PROVISIONAL`: its prefab is not read). It holds the vest, the backpack, the suits and the door's key; **the run's own weapon is the one in its class's gun case**, which a run that begins with it on takes from there (where no case has it, it lies in the crate as before, as `--add-weapon`'s do). `--start clothing` stands before it.
  - **Since 2026-10-14, night, each class has a table, its weapons in a gun case on it and what loads them in a box under it** (a small table for each weapon type, the weapons on it in a green gun case, long and short, and their magazines below in a black square box, each with a lid that opens). In place of the eight crates below, eight tables (1 m long, 0.85 m high) along the same wall, the label over each: on each a long, low green **gun case** (0.95 by 0.34 m, 0.13 high), which is the game's own weapon box (`WeaponCrate`, a grid of 5 by 2: a long case) holding the class's weapons; under each a black **magazine box** (a cube of 0.42 m, its front with the table's), the game's own lootable ammunition box (`AmmoBox`, 3 by 3), holding what loads them (one of every model of magazine, loaded, and a separate box with one stack of every round that fits: one of every magazine the table's weapons' magazine slots take (a class in a filter standing for every item under it; a stock preset's own besides), full of the first such weapon's own round where it takes it, else its first; and beside it, under the eight tables of weapons, a second `AmmoBox`, olive, with a full stack of every round the weapons' chambers or those magazines take, by calibre and name: `main::table_loads`; the RIFLES' 79 magazines and 72 rounds, the PISTOLS' 32 and 58). **A weapon fed from magazines whose stock preset has none** (the RPK-16's alone, `RPK16 nomag`) is given the magazine its template names as its default (`defMagType`) when the database is read, in the weapon and as its spares (the app's, since 0.9.2: the game's client never reads `defMagType`, and SPT's server gives it as the magazine of a weapon whose build has none). Each is ten cells across inside and as deep as what it holds, as the crates were (the rifles' case 53 rows, their box 30). Both open as the range's crates do (the gestures recorded for a lid hinged up, 29 and 30), each **with a lid hinged along its back edge, by the wall, lifted towards it**: the case's to 80 degrees, the box's to 55, which is as far as the table over it lets it (`ESTIMATE`, both, and the shapes: the game's models of them are not read). The case is reached standing; the box, under the table, crouched (the game's reach is 1.3 m from the eye). **Since 0.10.0 a ninth table, `MEDICAL`, stands at the arm's end**: its case light, with one of every medical item of the install in it, and its box, `PROVISIONS`, with one of every food and drink (86 in all; quest items left out); the screen calls them `MEDICAL CASE` and `PROVISIONS BOX`, and `--start medical` stands before it. Using them is not built yet (`health.md`, "Not built"). The screen calls them by class: `RIFLES WEAPON CASE`, `RIFLES MAGAZINE BOX`. `--start rifles` and the rest stand before a table, its case in view.
  - **Since 2026-10-19, six storage cabinets against the staircase** (inside the hall's left wall since 0.76.0, the stairs outside it; `range::CABINETS`: where the barbed wire was, against the staircase, cabinets like the drawer at the back, for rigs, backpacks, helmets, armoured rigs and armour, masks and hats, and headsets): each the row's drawer in its cabinet (0.8 m high, the game's drawer container, ten cells across and as deep as what it holds), its back to the stairs' side and its front to the lane, from the start's side on `RIGS`, `BACKPACKS`, `HELMETS`, `ARMOR`, `MASKS AND HATS`, `HEADSETS`, each labelled; opened as the drawer is (the gestures recorded for it, 25 and 20). In each one of every item of the install of its sort, as its stock preset has it where it has one (an armour or a helmet with its inserts and plates), else bare (`gear_cabinets` in `main.rs`): **rigs** the vests without armour's slots (36), **armour** the body armour and the vests with them (72), **helmets** the headwear with them (64) and, since 0.98.0, the helmets' own parts (35: the face shields, mandibles, ear covers, side armour and appliqués, armoured equipment of no other sort; the visors among them), **masks and hats** the face covers and the other headwear (166), **headsets** the headphones (15), **backpacks** (45); armour's slots are any but an attachment's (`mod_`), or an armour class of the item's own; quest items (not needed), items with no model and the suits of one's own (the `CLOTHING` crate's) left out, and the built-in soft inserts (347, which come out of nothing in the game) and the armbands not asked for. **Since 0.75.0 a plates box and an eyewear dresser among them**: beside the armour an olive box (0.6 m along, 0.6 high, 0.45 deep) with a lid hinged along its back by the stairs, opened as the range's boxes are (29 and 30), on the game's ammunition box's container, with every armour plate (`ArmorPlate`, 39); at the row's far end a dresser (1 m along, 1.1 high), its drawer near its top, with every pair of glasses and goggles (`Visors`, 33). The row begins by the stairs' foot since (3.6 m behind the start's line). The screen calls them `RIGS DRAWER`, `PLATES BOX`, `EYEWEAR DRESSER` and the rest; `--start rigs`, `--start plates`, `--start masks-and-hats` and the rest stand before one, facing it. The app's, as the tables.
  - **Since 2026-10-17, a table of grenades** (`range::GRENADES`, after `MEDICAL`, the last on the L's arm): every grenade of the install in its case and every melee weapon in its box, named `MELEE` (37 in this install, quest items left out), for there to be any to take: the starting kit has a knife and no grenade (`grenades-and-knife.md`, "Built"). The app's, as every table.
  - **Since 2026-10-14, late, every weapon lies in a crate of weapons by its class, and the airdrop crate holds the kit alone** (every weapon can be chosen: labelled crates along the small wall, by class). Eight crates of the airdrop crate's kind stand along the knee-high wall to its right (the wall now reaches 10.5 m to the right of the lane, where the door wall stops at 4), each with its label drawn over it within 6 m: RIFLES, SMGS, MACHINE GUNS, SNIPERS, SHOTGUNS, PISTOLS, LAUNCHERS, CUSTOM (five by class and three of the app's for what those leave without a place). A weapon goes by the class its template gives it (`weapClass`: assault rifles and carbines are rifles, marksman's and sniper's rifles snipers, grenade launchers and special weapons launchers), a weapon of one's own to the last: 53, 22, 8, 18, 15, 29, 3 and 1, every weapon of the install with a stock preset (148) and the StG 44, each by its name. Each has with it what loads it: one fed from magazines its own full and **three more of its kind full of its round** (its reloads take them: `firing.md`, "The quick reload"), any other two full stacks of its round, and the rocket launcher nothing (it cannot be loaded). Each crate is ten cells across and as deep as what it holds (80 rows for the rifles, 30 for the rest). The airdrop crate keeps the run's own weapon, the vest and the backpack of the kit, the outfits and the key (and what `--add-weapon` asks for). `--start rifles` (or `smgs`, `machine-guns`, `snipers`, `shotguns`, `pistols`, `launchers`, `custom`) starts before one, turned to it; its screen's caption is its label. The choice of eight and their figures are the app's.
  - **A crate stands on the range**, two and a half metres ahead and a little to the left: the install's airdrop crate as a container (one grid of ten by twelve cells; the install's weapon boxes are five by five at most and do not hold all three), holding the app's weapon as its stock preset has it, and the vest and the backpack a BEAR of the Standard edition starts with, each with what the database has in it **but for the magazines and the rounds, which are the weapon's own**. For the AKM that is three of its own 30-round magazines of 7.62x39 PS in the vest and two lots of that round, 60 and 30, in the backpack, beside the medical kit, the water and the ration that were there. Looked at from within the game's reach it shows `F SEARCH`, and `F` opens the inventory screen with the crate in the stash's place. The box on the range is the app's own; the game's model for the crate is not read, and it opens at once with nothing to search.
  - **The screen** (`Tab`, or `F` at the crate): the tabs in the game's order; Gear with the equipment's slot views where the install's own panel has them, over a paper figure, each container in its slot with its grids beside it, and what is open on the right. **Things are moved with the mouse**: held with the left button and let go on a cell, a slot or another thing, `R` turning what is held; what is held is outlined green where it would be taken and red where not, and the line under the equipment says what the last move came to or why it was refused. While the screen is open the mouse and the keys are the screen's, as in the game (since the evening of 2026-10-11; before that the movement keys still worked).
  - **What the equipment holds is what the character is:** its sum is the carried weight the controller goes by from the next frame (unless `--weight` was given); what sits in the eight drawn slots is on the body, each by the mesh for what it is then worn over (a backpack sits differently over a vest); and the weapon in hand is the one on the sling, by the rules above: taken with `3` the first time, at once after that. With empty hands the weapon's keys do nothing; the hands themselves are the game's own rig for them since the evening of 2026-10-11 ("Empty hands"), where before neither weapon nor arms were drawn.
  - A run nobody is at the keys of (a capture, a scripted run) has the crate's three things on from the start, put on by the model's own moves, so that it has its weapon as before, and weighs what `--weight` says (nothing by default), as it did before there was an inventory, unless it is run for the inventory (`--inventory`, `--loot`, `--equip`: then the equipment's sum); `--bare` keeps it bare, `--equip` does the same for a played run, `--loot` starts with the crate open, and `--drag x:y:x:y` (as often as wanted) drags the mouse from one place in the window to another in such a run.

  - **Since midday, 2026-10-11** (an un-equip animation, looting as an animation and a state, dropping and splitting, and placeholder pictures):
    - *The weapon goes down while the screen is open and comes back when it shuts*, by the hands' own clips for it; taken off the sling in the screen it does not come back; put away outside the screen it goes by its put-away clip; taken in hand it is drawn by its draw clip. The clips were found by the ends of their names and played one after another until the night of the same day, since when the weapon's animator does all of it (below). With the screen open the weapon's keys do nothing (`R` used to reload as well as turn what was held).
    - *At the crate the character does what the approach does*, as far as it is built (`Movement::approach`, `let_go`): the lean let go, the crouch to a thing reached for more than 0.65 m under the ribcage (the crate's top is 0.5 m up, so it crouches), and the stance taken again when the screen shuts. Not built: the walk and turn to the place, the state of its own, the animation for opening, the wait before the screen opens.
    - *Putting down:* `Delete` puts the thing held or under the mouse down 0.4 m ahead of the character, where it lies as a plain box (`take_out`: what a raid keeps in its slot stays there). *Picking up:* `F` with the thing looked at (`pick_up`: a slot of the equipment that takes it, else the first room in the vest, the pockets, the backpack, the secured container, which order is PROVISIONAL). The weight follows both.
    - *Splitting:* with Ctrl held a stack let go on a free cell leaves half of itself there (`split_item`). The game asks how many; half is TRP's choice for now.
    - *Pictures:* each item is drawn with a plain shape for its sort (a rifle, a magazine, rounds, a helmet, a vest, a backpack, a cross, a can, a headset, a knife, a part, a box), chosen by the nearest of its classes that has one and painted by the app at its start. They stand in for the game's own pictures of its items and are none of the game's.
    - A capture run can press keys as well as drag (`--tap <frame>:<key>`).

  - **Since the evening, 2026-10-11** (the character could still be walked about with the screen open, and the weapon and arms shook while it was):
    - *The open screen has the keys* ("What an open screen does to the keys"). The simulation takes it as two things a host says each frame (`movement::Input`): `screen`, a screen has the keys and the mouse, and `inventory`, the character's inventory is open. With `screen` nothing is handed over: no movement, no turn of the view, no key; each key is noted as it stands, so that what it did meanwhile is lost. With `inventory` the body's animator is told as of a reload, and a change either way switches the vault key's try off. The app sets both while its screen is open, and tells the weapon nothing of its keys meanwhile either (the aim key and the trigger are noted, `Firearm::note_trigger`). The screen's systems now run after the simulation's frames, so a screen opened in one frame has the keys from the next, as the game's has.
    - *The hands go down by the animator's own states* ("The inventory open"): the weapon's logic is told (`Firearm::set_inventory`) and takes the hands through the three states with the install's own exits, blends and speeds, holding the last one on its last frame; opened in a reload or a shot it waits for that to end; at rest it puts the sights down and lets the trigger go. The states and the two blends taken on a flag are read into `FirearmSettings::inventory` (`trp_sim::firearm_settings`); a weapon whose animator lacks them keeps its hands up. The clip player no longer starts a resting clip again from its beginning when it has run out unless the clip is marked as looping. A weapon taken out of the hands has its logic put back to rest (`Firearm::put_away`), and one taken in hand with the screen open is drawn first and lowered after.
    - (Until the night after, the weapon's logic counted as busy until the hands were back. It now idles throughout, as the game's does, and a press of the trigger while the hands are down or coming up is the idling operation's: with a round chambered it fires, the animator asked for its fire state outright. Read from the code; not recorded.) (The lean stood here too until later: it is now the game's presses and releases, `movement.md`, "Lean", so a lean whose key was let go under the screen stands when it shuts.)

  - **Since the night, 2026-10-11** (tried by hand: the shake is gone; the weapon's standby while looting did not match the game's, which blends better with an empty reload; and things' pictures stretched with the grid's size where they should shrink and grow):
    - *The weapon goes by its animator* (`firing.md`, "The weapon goes by its animator"). What was seen was the reload: the app finished it, brought the hands back onto the weapon and then took them off again, where the game's animator goes from the reload straight into the hold (`RELOAD INVENTORY END`), from a chambered round and from an empty chamber alike. The weapon's logic is now the game's operations on the weapon's own animator, and the app draws that animator's pose, between the simulation's frames, in place of clips played by name. The same goes for the draw (`OUT TO IDLE`, or `OUT TO IDLE ARM` with an empty chamber), the put-away (`IDLE TO OUT`, from rest or from the hold) and the empty hands, which have their own controller on their own animator (`trp_sim::EmptyHands`).
    - *What changed with it, all from the game's code:* a weapon taken off the sling in the open screen is put away from the hold by its animator (0.82 s) and only then do the empty hands appear, where it used to vanish at once; **a weapon taken in hand under an open screen is drawn and stays up**, where it used to be lowered after the draw (a draw does not hear of the inventory, and nothing tells the new weapon; "Being drawn" in `firing.md`); the weapon's logic idles while the hands are down, as the game's does, so the PROVISIONAL note about it counting as busy is gone; the body's animator is told the hands are busy through a draw and a put-away, and for the 0.034 s the empty hands take to appear.
    - *The pictures keep their shape.* Each sort's picture is painted as wide and as high as its shapes reach (a rifle long, a magazine tall) and drawn whole in the middle of the item's box at the largest size that fits, so a larger box has a larger picture and never a stretched one; a thing that lies turned in its grid has its picture turned a quarter with it (`inventory_view::Picture`). `local\shots\pics_fit.png`.
    - *Checked in the app by scripted runs* (`--log-frames` now writes the state the rig's animator is in or entering): a reload begun at 2.1 s with the screen opened 0.8 s into it goes `RELOAD OUT`, `RELOAD IN`, `RELOAD INVENTORY END` (2.9 s after the key), `IN INVENTORY` (`local\shots\an_reload_inv.png`); the run through the whole of it (`--loot --bare --drag 1100:180:170:450 --tap 150:tab --tap 200:3 --tap 500:tab --tap 620:delete --tap 720:tab`): the empty hands appear (`SPAWN`, `OUT TO IDLE`, `IDLE`), `3` draws the rifle (`OUT TO IDLE` for 0.95 s), the screen takes the hands down (`OPEN INVENTORY`, `IN INVENTORY` 0.34 s on), the rifle put on the ground there is put away from the hold (`IDLE TO OUT`, gone 0.80 s later) and the empty hands appear again; with the hands held down and settled the left hand moves at most 0.17 mm from one drawn frame to the next (0.14 mm at rest), as before. **Compared with the game: nothing**; and nobody but the scripts has tried it.

  - **Since 2026-10-12** (the left hand's gestures for opening boxes, doors and the rest; `interactions.md` has all of it):
    - *The crate is worked with the left hand before it is shown.* `F` begins the game's own course: the lean let go and the crouch as before, the body's gesture for a container's opening, the thing told to do its part 1.6 s in, and the screen opened when the game's state for it is over. The walk is refused meanwhile, the view turns, and the inventory key does nothing.
    - *A thing on the ground is taken with the left hand,* standing still only (walking, `F` does nothing, as the game's call does), with the view held for the 0.6 s until the thing goes into the inventory.
    - *With the screen open the left arm is the body's,* in its own pose for an open inventory, once the weapon's hands have gone down; with empty hands it is not.
    - *Putting a thing down has no gesture,* as before, and now by the reading and not for want of one.
  - **Since the recordings of 2026-10-12** (nine made by hand and six scripted, with recorder 0.6.0; `interactions.md` and `firing.md` have what each showed):
    - *The crate is searched, not approached.* `F` asks the left hand for the crate's gesture and holds the character to nothing: no crouch, no lean let go, the walk and the view free. The screen opens 0.55 s after the key (the install's wait, `DelayToOpenContainer`; read on the afternoon of 2026-10-12, where the morning had the class's 0.5 s and 0.05 s more for the screen), which stops the gesture where it is; a character that has gone a quarter of a metre further from the crate by then, or is in a jump, is shown nothing. `Movement::approach` and `let_go` are no longer the crate's.
